28 JANUARY 2003. — Act on the allocation of the family home to the spouse or legal cohabitant who is a victim of acts of physical violence by their partner, and supplementing article 410 of the Criminal Code (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
The Chambers have adopted and We sanction the following:
Article 1. This Act regulates a matter referred to in article 78 of the Constitution.

### Art. 2. Article 410, paragraph 2, of the Criminal Code, inserted by the Act of

24 November 1997 and amended by the Act of 28 November 2000, is supplemented as follows:
"Furthermore, in the case referred to in article 398, paragraph 1, the maximum penalty is increased to one year's imprisonment."

### Art. 3

In article 223 of the Civil Code, the following paragraph is inserted between paragraphs 2 and 3:
"If one spouse has committed against the other an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code or has attempted to commit an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such behaviour, the victim spouse shall be granted, except in exceptional circumstances, the use of the matrimonial home if he or she so requests."

### Art. 4

In article 1447 of the same Code, inserted by the law of 14 July 1976, the following paragraph is inserted between paragraphs 1 and 2:

"The request made by the spouse who has been the victim of an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code or of an attempt of an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code shall be granted, except in exceptional circumstances, either when the other spouse has been convicted on that ground by a decision that has acquired the force of res judicata and the divorce has been pronounced against him or her, or when the decision pronouncing the divorce is based in whole or in part on that act."

### Art. 5

Article 1479 of the same Code, inserted by the law of 23 November 1998, is supplemented by the following paragraph:
"If a legal cohabitant has committed against the other an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code or has attempted to commit an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such behaviour, the latter shall be granted, except in exceptional circumstances, the enjoyment of the common residence if he or she so requests."

### Art. 6

In article 1280 of the Judicial Code, amended by the laws of 14 July 1976, 2 February 1994, 30 June 1994 and 20 May 1997, the following paragraph is inserted between paragraphs 6 and 7:
"If a spouse has committed against the other an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code or has attempted to commit an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such behaviour, the victim spouse shall be granted, except in exceptional circumstances, the enjoyment of the matrimonial residence if he or she so requests."

28 JANUARY 2003. — Law on the allocation of the family home to the spouse or to the legal cohabitant who is the victim of physical violence by his or her partner and supplementing article 410 of the Criminal Code (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
The Chambers have adopted and We sanction the following:

### Article 1

This law regulates a matter referred to in article 78 of the Constitution.

### Art. 2

Article 410, second paragraph, of the Criminal Code, inserted by the law of 24 November 1997 and amended by the law of 28 November 2000, is supplemented as follows:
"Moreover, the maximum penalty, in the case provided for in article 398, first paragraph, is increased to imprisonment of one year."

### Art. 3

In article 223 of the Civil Code, the following paragraph is inserted between the second and third paragraphs:
"If a spouse has committed against the other an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code, or has attempted to commit an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such behaviour, the spouse who is the victim shall, except in exceptional circumstances, be granted the enjoyment of the matrimonial residence if he or she so requests."

### Art. 4

In article 1447 of the same Code, inserted by the law of 14 July 1976, the following paragraph is inserted between the first and second paragraphs:
"Except in exceptional circumstances, the request made by the spouse who is the victim of an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code or of an attempt of an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code shall be granted, either when the other spouse has been convicted on that ground by a decision that has acquired the force of res judicata, or when the decision pronouncing the divorce is based in whole or in part on that act."

### Art. 5

Article 1479 of the same Code, inserted by the law of 23 November 1998, is supplemented by the following paragraph:
"If a legal cohabitant has committed against the other an act referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code, or has attempted to commit an act referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such behaviour, the latter shall, except in exceptional circumstances, be granted the enjoyment of the common residence if he or she so requests."

### Art. 6

In article 1280 of the Judicial Code, amended by the laws of 14 July 1976, 2 February 1994, 30 June 1994 and 20 May 1997, the following paragraph is inserted between the sixth and seventh paragraphs:
"If a spouse has committed against the other spouse an offence as referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403 or 405 of the Criminal Code, or has attempted to commit an offence as referred to in articles 375, 393, 394 or 397 of the same Code, or if there are serious indications of such conduct, the spouse who is the victim shall, except in exceptional circumstances, be granted the enjoyment of the matrimonial home if he or she so requests."

We promulgate this law, order that it be sealed with the Seal of State and published by the Belgian Official Gazette.
Given at Brussels, 28 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Employment and Equal Opportunities Policy, Mrs L. ONKELINX
The Minister of Justice,

20 JANUARY 2003. — Law on the strengthening of legislation against racism (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
The Chambers have adopted and We sanction the following: CHAPTER I. — General provision
Article 1. This law regulates a matter referred to in article 78 of the Constitution.

## CHAPTER II

Amendments to the law of 30 July 1981 on the suppression of certain acts inspired by racism or xenophobia

### Art. 2

In article 1 of the law of 30 July 1981 on the suppression of certain acts inspired by racism or xenophobia, amended by the law of 12 April 1994, the following amendments are made:

1° The following paragraph is inserted between paragraphs 1 and 2:
"Any conduct consisting in ordering anyone to practise discrimination against a person, a group, a community or their members is considered as discrimination within the meaning of this law.";
2° in the former paragraph 2, which has become paragraph 3, in 1°, the words "on the grounds of his race" are replaced by the words "on the grounds of an alleged race";
3° in the same paragraph, in 2°, the word "rassenscheiding" in the Dutch text is replaced by the word "segregatie" and the words "het ras" are replaced by the words "een zogenaamd ras";
4° in the same paragraph, in 3°, the words "his race, his colour, his descent, his origin, or his nationality" are replaced by the words "an alleged race, his colour, his descent or his national or ethnic origin";

20 JANUARY 2003. — Law on the strengthening of legislation against racism (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Our Greetings.
The Chambers have adopted and We sanction the following: CHAPTER I. — General provision

### Article 1

This law regulates a matter referred to in article 78 of the Constitution.

## CHAPTER II

Amendments to the law of 30 July 1981 on the punishment of certain acts inspired by racism or xenophobia

### Art. 2

In article 1 of the law of 30 July 1981 on the punishment of certain acts inspired by racism or xenophobia, amended by the law of 12 April 1994, the following amendments are made:
1° The following paragraph is inserted between the first and second paragraphs:
"Any conduct consisting in ordering anyone to discriminate against a person, a group, a community or their members is considered as discrimination within the meaning of this law.";
2° in the former second paragraph which has become the third paragraph in 1°, the words "on the grounds of his race" are replaced by the words "on the grounds of an alleged race";
3° in the same paragraph in 2°, the word "rassenscheiding" in the Dutch text is replaced by the word "segregatie" and the words "het ras" are replaced by the words "een zogenaamd ras";
4° in the same paragraph in 3°, the words "his race, colour, descent, origin or nationality" are replaced by the words "an alleged race, his colour, descent or national or ethnic origin";

5° in the same paragraph, in 4°, the words "race, colour, descent, origin, or nationality" are replaced by the words "an alleged race, colour, descent or national or ethnic origin".

### Art. 3

In article 2 of the same law, replaced by the law of 12 April 1994 and amended by the law of 26 June 2000, the following amendments are made:
1° in paragraph 1, the words "his race, his colour, his descent, his origin or his nationality" are replaced by the words "an alleged race, his colour, his descent or his national or ethnic origin";
2° in paragraph 2 the words "race, colour, descent, origin or nationality" are replaced by the words "an alleged race, colour, descent or national or ethnic origin".

### Art. 4

In Article 2bis of the same law, inserted by the law of 12 April 1994, the following amendments are made:
1° in the first paragraph, the words "of his race" are replaced by the words
"of a so-called race";
2° the following paragraph is inserted between the first and second paragraphs:
"The same penalties apply when discrimination against a group, a community or their members is based on the so-called race, colour, descent or national or ethnic origin of these members or some of them."

### Art. 5

In Article 4 of the same law, amended by the law of 12 April 1994, the following amendments are made:
1° in the first paragraph, the words "of his race, his colour, his descent, his origin or his nationality" are replaced by the words "of a so-called race, his colour, his descent or his national or ethnic origin";
2° in the second paragraph, the words "of the race, the colour, the descent, the origin or the nationality" are replaced by the words "of a so-called race, the colour, the descent or the national or ethnic origin".

### Art. 6. In the same law, an Article 5ter is inserted, worded as follows

"Art. 5ter. Without prejudice to the powers of judicial police officers, the officials designated by the King shall monitor compliance with this law and its implementing orders.
These officials shall exercise this monitoring in accordance with the provisions of the law of 16 November 1972 concerning labour inspection.".

## CHAPTER III

Amendments to the law of 15 February 1993 establishing a Centre for Equal Opportunities and the Fight against Racism

### Art. 7

In Article 2 of the law of 15 February 1993 establishing a Centre for Equal Opportunities and the Fight against Racism, amended by the law of 13 April 1995, the following sentence is inserted before the sentence "the Centre is also responsible for stimulating the fight against trafficking in human beings": "The Centre also has the mission of ensuring respect for the fundamental rights of foreigners, informing the public authorities on the nature and scale of migratory flows and developing consultation and dialogue with all public and private actors concerned with reception and integration policies for immigrants."

### Art. 8

In Article 3 of the same law, amended by the law of 13 April 1995, the following amendments are made:
1° A 4°bis is inserted, worded as follows:
"4bis. to receive, within the limits of its mission defined in Article 2, complaints, to process them and to carry out any mediation mission it deems useful, this without prejudice to the powers of the federal mediators;".
2° The article is supplemented as follows:
"9° to collect and publish without possibility of identifying the parties involved, statistical data and case law decisions useful for the evaluation of the aforementioned law of 30 July 1981 and the law of 6 January 2003 aimed at combating discrimination and amending the law of 15 February 1993 establishing a Centre for Equal Opportunities and the Fight against Racism;
10° to request the competent authority, when the Centre invokes facts that allow the existence of discrimination to be presumed, as referred to in the aforementioned laws of 30 July 1981 and 6 January 2003, to inform itself and to keep the Centre informed of the results of the analysis of the

### Art. 8

In Article 3 of the same law, amended by the law of 13 April 1995, the following amendments are made:
1° A 4°bis is inserted, reading as follows:
"4bis. to receive and handle complaints within the limits of its mandate as defined in Article 2, and to carry out any mediation task it deems useful, without prejudice to the powers of the federal ombudsmen;"
2° The article is supplemented as follows:
"9° to obtain and publish statistical data and decisions arising from case law that are useful for the evaluation of the application of the aforementioned law of 30 July 1981 and of the law of 6 January 2003 to combat discrimination and to amend the law of 15 February 1993 establishing a centre for equal opportunities and opposition to racism, without the parties concerned being identifiable;
10° to request the competent authority, when the Centre presents facts indicating a presumption of discrimination, as referred to in the aforementioned laws of 30 July 1981 and of 6 January 2003, to inform itself and to keep the Centre informed of the results of the analysis of the facts in question. The authorities shall inform the Centre in a reasoned manner of the action taken.

### Art. 9

In Article 4 of the same law, the following paragraph is inserted between paragraphs 1 and 2:
"The Minister of Justice shall communicate annually to the Centre the judicial statistics relating to the application of the aforementioned laws of 30 July 1981 and 6 January 2003, as well as the judicial decisions taken in application of these laws, without the parties concerned being identifiable."

## CHAPTER IV

Amendment to the law of 13 May 1999 containing the disciplinary statute of members of staff of the police services

### Art. 10

Article 26 of the law of 13 May 1999 on the disciplinary statute of members of staff of the police services is supplemented by the following paragraphs:
"When the Centre for Equal Opportunities and Opposition to Racism communicates to the Standing Committee for the Supervision of the Police Services or to the General Inspectorate of the Federal Police and the Local Police facts suggesting discriminatory treatment within the meaning of the law of 30 July 1981 to suppress certain acts inspired by racism or xenophobia or of the law of 6 January 2003 to combat discrimination and amending the law of 15 February 1993 establishing a Centre for Equal Opportunities and Opposition to Racism, the chairman of the Standing Committee or the Inspector General, as the case may be, shall have an investigation carried out into these facts, inform the competent authority and refer the matter to the disciplinary or judicial authority if the facts so justify. The chairman of the Standing Committee or the Inspector General, as the case may be, shall inform the Centre of the follow-up given to its actions and in particular of the action taken by the disciplinary or judicial authority in examining the facts.
If the Standing Committee or the General Inspectorate is seized of facts suggesting discriminatory treatment within the meaning of the aforementioned laws, the chairman of the Standing Committee or the Inspector General, as the case may be, shall inform the Centre for Equal Opportunities and Opposition to Racism without communicating the identity of the parties concerned. He shall also inform it of the action taken by the disciplinary or judicial authority in examining the facts."

### Art. 11

This law enters into force on the day of its publication in the Moniteur belge.
We promulgate this law, order that it be sealed with the Seal of State and published by the Moniteur belge.
Given at Brussels, 20 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Prime Minister,
G. VERHOFSTADT
The Minister of Employment, Mrs L. ONKELINX
Seen and sealed with the Seal of State: The Minister of Justice,
M. VERWILGHEN

### Art. 11

This law enters into force on the day of its publication in the Belgian Official Gazette.
We promulgate this law, order that it shall be sealed with the Seal of State and published by the Belgian Official Gazette.
Given at Brussels, 20 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Prime Minister,
G. VERHOFSTADT
The Minister of Employment, Mrs. L. ONKELINX
Seen and sealed with the Seal of State: The Minister of Justice,
M. VERWILGHEN

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

F. 2003 — 571

[C − 2003/11040]

N. 2003 — 571

[C − 2003/11040]

20 JANUARY 2003. — Royal Decree establishing the programme, conditions and jury of the practical aptitude examination for certified accountants and certified accountant-tax advisers

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
Having regard to the law of 22 April 1999 on the accounting and tax professions, in particular Article 51;
Having regard to the opinion of the Higher Council for Economic Professions of 20 June 2001;
Having regard to opinion No. 32.902/3 of the Council of State, given on 24 September 2002;
On the proposal of Our Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes,
We have decreed and decree:
CHAPTER I. — Definitions
Article 1. For the application of this decree, the following is meant by: 1° the law: the law of 22 April 1999 on the accounting and tax
professions;
2° the Institute: the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, created by Article 43 of the law;
3° the framework law: the framework law of 1 March 1976 regulating the protection of professional titles and the exercise of intellectual service-providing professions;
4° the Chambers: the Executive Chambers, referred to in Article 6, § 3, of the framework law;
5° the Chambers of Appeal: the Chambers of Appeal, referred to in Article 6,

### § 3, of the framework law;

6° the Council: the National Council, referred to in Article 6, § 3, of the framework law;

7° the Bureau: the Bureau of the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, referred to in Article 34 of the Royal Decree of 27 November 1985 determining the rules of organisation and operation of the professional institutes created for intellectual service-providing professions;
8° the Internship Commission: the examination jury referred to in Article 51 of the law and in Chapter IV of this decree;
9° the practical aptitude examination: the examination provided for in Article 51 of the law.

## CHAPTER II. — Programme of the practical aptitude examination

### Art. 2

The practical aptitude examination has the purpose of verifying the candidate's capacity, at the end of his internship period, to apply his theoretical knowledge to the practice of the profession of certified accountant or certified accountant-tax adviser and his aptitude to exercise the profession in compliance with the laws and rules of professional ethics.

### Art. 3

The practical aptitude examination comprises an equivalent written test and an oral test, covering directly or indirectly the following subjects:
1° general accounting;
2° legislation relating to accounting and annual accounts of enterprises;
3° general principles of financial management;
4° preparation, analysis and critique of annual accounts;

5° organisation of accounting and administrative services of enterprises;
6° value added tax;
7° personal income tax; 8° corporate income tax;
9° tax procedures;
10° company law and legislation relating to enterprises in difficulty;
11° principles of registration and succession duties;

20 JANUARY 2003. — Royal Decree concerning the programme, conditions and examination jury for the practical aptitude examination of certified accountants and certified accountant-tax advisers

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all who are now and will be in the future, Our Greetings.
Having regard to the law of 22 April 1999 on the accounting and tax professions, in particular Article 51;
Having regard to the opinion of the Higher Council for Economic Professions of 20 June 2001;
Having regard to opinion No. 32.902/3 of the Council of State, given on 24 September 2002;
On the proposal of Our Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes,
We have decided and decide:

## CHAPTER I. — Definitions

### Article 1

For the application of this decree, the following is understood by: 1° the law: the law of 22 April 1999 on the accounting and tax
professions;
2° the Institute: the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, established by Article 43 of the law;
3° the framework law: the framework law of 1 March 1976 regulating the protection of professional titles and the exercise of intellectual service-providing professions;
4° the Chambers: the Executive Chambers, as referred to in Article 6, § 3, of the framework law;
5° the Chambers of Appeal: the Chambers of Appeal as referred to in Article 6, § 3, of the framework law;
6° the Council: the National Council, as referred to in Article 6, § 3, of the framework law;
7° the Bureau: the Bureau of the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, as referred to in Article 34 of the Royal Decree of 27 November 1985 determining the rules concerning the organisation and operation of the professional institutes established for intellectual service-providing professions;
8° the Internship Commission: the examination jury as referred to in Article 51 of the law and in Chapter IV of this decree;
9° the practical aptitude examination: the examination referred to in Article 51 of the law.

## CHAPTER II. — Programme of the practical aptitude examination

### Art. 2

The practical aptitude examination has the purpose of verifying whether the candidate at the end of his internship period is able to apply his theoretical knowledge in the professional practice of a certified accountant or certified accountant-tax adviser as well as whether he is able to exercise the profession in compliance with the laws and the rules of professional ethics.

### Art. 3

The practical aptitude examination consists of a written and an oral test, which are equivalent, dealing directly or indirectly with the following subjects:
1˚ general accounting;
2˚ legislation on accounting and the annual accounts of undertakings;
3˚ general principles of financial management;
4˚ the preparation, analysis and critical assessment of annual accounts;
5˚ organisation of the accounting services and administrative services of the undertaking;
6˚ value added tax; 7˚ personal income tax;
8˚ corporate income tax; 9˚ tax procedures;
10˚ company law and legislation relating to undertakings in difficulty;
11˚ principles of registration and succession duties;

12˚ principles relating to customs and excise;
13˚ principles of labour law and social security;
14˚ professional ethics of certified accountants and certified accountants-tax advisers.

### Art. 4

§ 1er. The written test of the aptitude examination for access to the title of certified accountant or certified accountant-tax adviser concerns the resolution of one or more practical cases relating to the subjects referred to in article

### 3. §

2. In the written test of the aptitude examination for access to the title of certified accountant-tax adviser, a more thorough knowledge of the subjects referred to in article 3, 6˚, 7˚, 8˚, 9˚, 11˚ and 12˚ will be required.

### Art. 5

The oral test comprises a commentary on the written test and an examination on the practice of the function, the tasks, the responsibility and the professional ethics of certified accountants and certified accountants-tax advisers.
Only candidates who have obtained at least fifty per cent of the points after the written test are admitted to the oral test.

Candidates for the title of certified accountant-tax adviser must in addition have obtained at least sixty per cent of the points in the written test concerning the subjects mentioned in article 4, § 2, to be admitted to the oral test.

## CHAPTER III

Conditions relating to admission to and organisation of the practical aptitude examination

### Art. 6

The candidate who wishes to participate in the practical aptitude examination shall address a written request to the Internship Commission, referred to in chapter IV.

### Art. 7

The candidate is admitted to the practical aptitude examination as soon as he has completed his internship in accordance with the legal and regulatory provisions as well as the guidelines issued by the Council, which must be observed by the trainee accountant or trainee accountant-tax adviser during his internship.

The Institute shall notify in writing within a period of one month the trainee who has submitted a written request, as referred to in article 6, of the date of the next practical aptitude examination. If the trainee does not satisfy the conditions for participating in the practical aptitude examination, he shall be informed thereof in writing. He may submit a new request, in accordance with article 6, as soon as he satisfies the conditions of access referred to in the first paragraph.

### Art. 8

The practical aptitude examination is organised at least twice a year by the Institute and may be taken in one of the three national languages at the candidate's choice.

### Art. 9

The results are notified by the Institute to the candidate within three months of the practical aptitude examination and are added to his file.

### Art. 10

Candidates who, after the oral part, have obtained sixty per cent of the points, have passed and are considered to have submitted an application for registration in the register of holders of the profession referred to in article 46 of the Royal Decree of 27 November 1985 determining the rules of organisation and functioning of the professional institutes created for the intellectual professions providing services. The list of candidates who have passed is transmitted to the Chamber which shall proceed with their registration in the register of holders of the profession, in accordance with article 8, § 1er, 1˚, of the framework law.

### Art. 11

If the candidate has not passed in accordance with article 10, he may submit in writing a new request, in accordance with article 6 with a view to being admitted to the following practical aptitude examination and this, until the expiry of the maximum internship period of 36 months referred to in article 51 of the law.

## CHAPTER IV. — The Internship Commission/The examination board

### Art. 12

An Internship Commission is established within the Institute. This Internship Commission acts as examination board, as referred to in article 51 of the law and is responsible for conducting the practical aptitude examination organised by the Institute.

### Art. 13

In the exercise of its mission, the Internship Commission has the most extensive means of supervision and control. It may delegate to one or more of its members the tasks entrusted to it.

### Art. 10

Candidates who, after the oral part, have obtained sixty percent of the points to be achieved, have passed and are deemed to have made their application for registration on the tableau as referred to in Article 46 of the Royal Decree of 27 November 1985 determining the rules concerning the organisation and operation of the professional institutes established for the intellectual service professions. The list of successful candidates is transmitted to the Chamber. It registers them on the tableau of professional practitioners in accordance with Article 8, § 1, 1°, of the framework law.

### Art. 11

If the candidate has not passed in accordance with Article 10, he may submit a new application in accordance with Article 6 to be admitted to the next practical aptitude examination, until the expiry of the maximum traineeship period of 36 months as provided for in Article 51 of the law.

## HOOFDSTUK IV. — The Traineeship Commission/Examination Jury

### Art. 12

A Traineeship Commission is established within the Institute. This Traineeship Commission acts as examination jury, as referred to in Article 51 of the law, and is responsible for conducting the practical aptitude examination organised by the Institute.

### Art. 13

The Traineeship Commission has the broadest powers of supervision and control in the exercise of its mandate. It may entrust the tasks assigned to it to one or more of its members.

### Art. 14

§ 1er. The Traineeship Commission has twenty effective members, including the president and vice-president of the Institute. Ten members are French-speaking and ten members are Dutch-speaking. The effective members are, on proposal of the Bureau, appointed by the Council for a renewable term of four years. This appointment takes place at the second meeting following the elections of the newly constituted Council. The appointments of the members of the Traineeship Commission are subject to the approval of the Minister who has the Middle Classes in his attributions.

### § 2

The president and vice-president of the Institute are also president and vice-president of the Traineeship Commission.

### § 3

Where appropriate, the Council may, on proposal of the Bureau, appoint alternate members whose mandate expires at the same time as that of the effective members elected in accordance with § 1. The alternate members may only attend meetings of the Traineeship Commission when they are summoned to replace an effective member.

### Art. 15

§ 1er. There is incompatibility between the mandate of member of the Traineeship Commission and the mandate of:
1° Effective or alternate member of the Chamber;
2° Effective or alternate member of the Appeal Chamber.

### § 2

The president and vice-president excepted, the Traineeship Commission may include a maximum of five Dutch-speaking members and five French-speaking members who are also members of the Council.

### § 3

At least one French-speaking member and one Dutch-speaking member must be professors or lecturers at a university, in an institution of higher education of university level or in an institution of higher economic education.

### § 4

One of the members of the Traineeship Commission must possess sufficient knowledge of the German language.

### § 5

The other members of the Traineeship Commission must be members of the Institute or persons chosen on the basis of their specific knowledge of matters relevant to the profession.

### Art. 16

§ 1er. The Traineeship Commission meets on convocation of its president or, in case of absence, of its vice-president. The member who is prevented gives timely notice to the president.

### § 2

The meetings of the Traineeship Commission are chaired by the president or, in his absence, by the vice-president. If both are absent, the session is chaired by the oldest member. The acting president has the same prerogatives as the president.

### § 3

The Traineeship Commission deliberates by linguistic role. It can only validly deliberate when at least six members of each linguistic role are present. Decisions are taken by simple majority of votes. In case of a tie, the president's vote is decisive.

### Art. 14

§ 1. The Traineeship Commission consists of twenty effective members, including the president and vice-president of the Institute. Ten members are Dutch-speaking and 10 members are French-speaking. The effective members are, on proposal of the bureau, appointed by the Council for a renewable term of four years. This takes place during the second session of the newly constituted Council following the elections. The appointments of the members of the Traineeship Commission are subject to the approval of the Minister who is competent for the Middle Classes.

### § 2

The president and vice-president of the Institute are also president and vice-president of the Traineeship Commission.

### § 3

Where appropriate, the Council may, on proposal of the Bureau, appoint alternate members whose mandate expires together with the effective members appointed in accordance with § 1. The alternate members may only attend the meetings of the Traineeship Commission when they are summoned to replace an effective member.

### Art. 15

§ 1 There is incompatibility between the mandate of member of the Traineeship Commission and the mandate of:
1° effective or alternate member of the Chamber;
2° effective or alternate member of the Appeal Chamber.

### § 2

The president and vice-president excepted, the Traineeship Commission includes at most five Dutch-speaking and five French-speaking members who are also members of the Council.

### § 3

At least one French-speaking and one Dutch-speaking member must be a lecturer at a university, an institution for higher education of university level or at an institution for higher economic education.

### § 4

One of the members of the Traineeship Commission must possess sufficient knowledge of the German language.

### § 5

The other members of the Traineeship Commission must be members of the Institute or persons chosen on the basis of their specific knowledge of matters relevant to the profession.

### Art. 16

§ 1. The Traineeship Commission meets on invitation of its president or, in his absence, of its vice-president. The member who is prevented gives timely notice to the president.

### § 2

The meetings of the Traineeship Commission are chaired by the president or, in his absence, by the vice-president. If both are absent, the meeting is chaired by the oldest member present. The acting president has the same powers as the president.

### § 3

The Traineeship Commission deliberates per linguistic role. It can only validly deliberate when at least six members are present. Decisions are taken by simple majority of votes. In case of a tie, that of the president is decisive.

## CHAPITRE V. — Exemptions

### Art. 17

§ 1er. Certified accountants, members of the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, who hold a diploma allowing access to the title of certified accountant-tax adviser, and who apply for their registration on the tableau of certified accountants-tax advisers, are exempted from completing the traineeship. They are registered by the Chamber on the tableau of certified accountants-tax advisers upon passing the practical aptitude examination relating to the tax matters of Article 3.

### § 2

It is the Traineeship Commission that will be responsible for conducting the practical aptitude examination referred to in § 1, which will consist of an oral test.

## CHAPTER V. — Exemptions

### Art. 17

§ 1. Certified accountants, members of the Professional Institute of Certified Accountants and Tax Advisers, who hold a diploma giving access to the title of certified accountant-tax adviser, and who apply for registration on the register of certified accountants-tax advisers, shall be exempted from completing a traineeship. They shall be registered by the Chamber on the register of certified accountants-tax advisers after successfully passing a practical proficiency examination on the tax matters referred to in Article 3.

### § 2

The practical proficiency examination as referred to in § 1 shall consist of an oral test conducted by the Traineeship Committee.

## CHAPTER VI. — Final provisions

### Art. 18

This Order shall enter into force on the day of its publication in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 19

Our Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes, is responsible for the execution of this Order.
Given at Brussels, 20 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes,
R. DAEMS

## CHAPTER VI. — Miscellaneous provisions

### Art. 18

This Order shall enter into force on the day it is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 19

Our Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes, is responsible for the execution of this Order.
Given at Brussels, 20 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Telecommunications and Public Enterprises and Holdings, responsible for the Middle Classes,
R. DAEMS

28 JANUARY 2003. — Royal Decree amending Royal Decree No. 23 of 29 December 1992 regulating the rules of application of Article 53quinquies of the Value Added Tax Code (1)
ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
Having regard to the Value Added Tax Code, in particular Article 53quinquies, inserted by the Act of 28 December 1992 and amended by the Acts of 7 March 2002 and 20 December 2002 and Article 53octies, inserted by the Act of 28 December 1992 and amended by the Act of 5 September 2001;
Having regard to Royal Decree No. 23 of 29 December 1992 regulating the rules of application of Article 53quinquies of the Value Added Tax Code, in particular Article 1, amended by the Royal Decrees of 5 September 2001 and 2 April 2002 and the annex, replaced by the Royal Decree of 26 November 1998;
Having regard to the Acts on the Council of State, coordinated on 12 January 1973, in particular Article 3, § 1, replaced by the Act of 4 July 1989 and amended by the Act of 4 August 1996;
Having regard to the urgent necessity;
Considering that the model of the annual list of taxable customers to whom goods and services have been supplied must be amended in order to allow scanning, which was started from 1 January 2003;
On the proposal of Our Minister of Finance,
We have decreed and decree:

### Article 1

In Article 1 of Royal Decree No. 23 of 29 December 1992 regulating the implementing arrangements for Article 53quinquies of the Value Added Tax Code, amended by the Royal Decrees of 5 September 2001 and 2 April 2002, the following amendments are made:
a) the first paragraph is replaced as follows:
"The taxable persons referred to in Article 53quinquies of the Code are required each year before 31 March, for each taxable customer who must be identified for value added tax purposes pursuant to Article 50 of the Code, with the exception of taxable customers who carry out exclusively transactions exempted from tax pursuant to Article 44 of the Code, and to whom they have supplied goods or provided services during the previous year, to submit to the administration having VAT under its authority a list containing the following information:

1° the VAT identification number of that taxable customer;

2° the total amount, excluding tax, of the goods supplied to him and the services provided to him;

3° the total amount of tax charged to him.";

b) the fourth paragraph is repealed;

c) the fifth and sixth paragraphs become the fourth and fifth paragraphs respectively;

d) in the fifth paragraph, new, the words "shall submit a list on which they enter the mention 'Nil'." are replaced by the words "must also submit that list within the time limit set in the first paragraph."

### Art. 2

The annex to the same decree, replaced by the Royal Decree of 26 November 1998, is replaced by the annex to this decree.

### Art. 3. This decree produces its effects on 1 January 2003

### Art. 4

Our Minister of Finance is responsible for the execution of this decree.
Given at Brussels, 28 January 2003.

ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Finance,
D. REYNDERS

### Art. 3. This decree takes effect from 1 January 2003

### Art. 4. Our Minister of Finance is charged with the execution of this decree

Given in Brussels, 28 January 2003.

ALBERT
On behalf of the King:
The Minister of Finance,
D. REYNDERS

30 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree taken in execution of article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters

REPORT TO THE KING
Sire,
The decree which we have the honour to submit for the signature of Your Majesty is taken on the basis of article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters.
Title III of the aforementioned law and the present draft decree give concrete form to the will of the government expressed notably during its Council of 19 July 2001 to update the policy on advance agreements and decisions and to adapt current practice.

According to article 20 of the same law, an advance decision is the legal act by which the Federal Public Service (FPS) Finance determines in accordance with the provisions in force how the law will apply to a particular situation or operation which has not yet produced effects on the tax level.
The proposed text intends, in execution of article 26 of the aforementioned law, to define an advance decision procedure combining simplicity, rigour, flexibility, and transparency.

In a start-up phase of the new system, applications will be submitted to a service which will have to be created within the central services of the department at a sufficient functional hierarchical level.
In this regard and taking into account the fact that the FPS Finance is currently in a restructuring phase, the competent Authority will ensure that the new advance decisions service is situated at an appropriate level of the administrative structure.

Taking into account the remark of the Council of State, the modalities for designating the service to which applications for advance decisions must be submitted are not included in this decree. Likewise, it is superfluous to recall at this level the legal provision according to which applications must imperatively be submitted in writing to the competent service.

In any event, the advance decisions will have a uniform character for all applications of the same nature, with an obvious concern for equal treatment of applications. It is indeed important to ensure the coherence of the administration's positions at all stages of the tax procedure.

Article 1 provides that an acknowledgement of receipt is issued in order to confirm to the applicant the receipt of his submission, which must be done within five working days from the date of receipt of the application.
This formality is of course distinct from that provided for by article 21, paragraph 6 of the law of 24 December 2002 aforementioned which provides for informing the applicant of the deadline by which the administration's decision must be made when the latter finds that the application submitted is complete. However, this finding requires an initial examination of the application on the merits and, where appropriate, inviting the applicant to complete it.

Article 2 provides for the possibility of involving, in the context of the examination of applications, any official of the FPS Finance. This consultation may, if necessary, be organised in a collegial manner, by the establishment of knowledge groups. These groups of officials will provide the expertise necessary for the processing of certain matters. Such a group will not systematically be called upon, but this may prove useful in particular when processing applications relating to several taxes, complex applications requiring the pooling of technical or legal expertise from several services, or when it appears that an application requires a new position to be taken on the application of an existing law.

30 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree in execution of article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters

REPORT TO THE KING
Sire,
The decree submitted for signature by Your Majesty is taken on the basis of article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters.
Title III of the aforementioned law and the present draft decree are a concretisation of the will of the government which was expressed in particular during its Council of 19 July 2001 to update the policy on advance agreements and decisions and to adapt current practice.
According to article 20 of the same law, an advance decision is the legal act by which the Federal Public Service (FPS) Finance determines in accordance with the provisions in force how the law is applied to a particular situation or operation which has not yet had effect on the tax level.
The proposed text aims, in accordance with article 26 of the aforementioned Law, to establish an advance decision procedure that combines simplicity, straightforwardness, flexibility and transparency.
In a start-up phase of the new system, the applications will be submitted to a service which will be established for that purpose within the central services of the department and this at a sufficiently functional hierarchical level.
In this respect and given the fact that the FPS Finance is currently in a phase of restructuring, the competent Authority will ensure that the new advance decisions service is placed at an appropriate level within the administrative structure.
Taking into account the remark of the Council of State, the modalities by which the service is designated to which applications for advance decisions must be submitted are not included in this decree. It is likewise superfluous to repeat at this level the legal provision according to which applications must be submitted in writing to the competent service in a mandatory manner.
In any case, for the sake of obvious concern for equal treatment of applications, the advance decisions must be taken in a uniform manner for all applications of the same nature. It is indeed important to ensure the coherence of the positions of the administration in each phase of the tax procedure.

### Artikel 1

provides that an acknowledgement of receipt shall be issued in order to confirm to the applicant that his application has been received. This issue must take place within five working days from the date of receipt of the application.
This formality must be clearly distinguished from that provided for in Article 21, 6th paragraph, of the aforementioned law of 24 December 2002, which aims to inform the applicant of the final date by which a decision of the administration must be made, as soon as the latter establishes that the submitted application is complete. However, this establishment requires an initial examination regarding the substance of the application and where appropriate the applicant will be requested to complete it.

### Artikel 2

provides for the possibility of involving any official of the FPS Finance in the examination of applications. This consultation may, insofar as necessary, be organised in a collegial manner through the establishment of knowledge groups. These groups of officials will be able to provide the knowledge necessary for the handling of certain matters. Such a group will not be systematically called upon, but only when this proves useful, in particular in the handling of applications relating to different taxes, for complex applications requiring a joint deployment of technical or legal expertise from different services or again, if it appears that an application necessitates a new position regarding the application of an existing legal provision.

In response to the remark of the Council of State concerning this provision, it is specified that the participation of any official of the FPS Finance whose collaboration proves useful for the examination of the application, as indicated in the preceding paragraph, is expressly provided for in this decree in order to prevent any possible challenge, based on other regulatory provisions, concerning the validity of decisions marked by such collaboration. Indeed, the advance decisions service with which applications must be submitted is not called upon to examine these applications exclusively in all circumstances. Moreover, a provision pursuing a similar objective of legal certainty in the context of decision-making assistance was already provided for by the royal decree of 4 April 1995 relating to the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, confirmed by the law of 2 June 1998.

The draft submitted to the opinion of the Council of State provided that: "Every service concerned by the application must be consulted during the processing thereof." This provision has been removed from the decree in view of the opinion of the Council of State. That being said, care will be taken for the proper application of the new system to ensure that the services concerned by the application are consulted according to appropriate procedures compatible with the objectives pursued by the adoption of the new advance decision system.

It goes without saying that in accordance with common law, the applicant may be assisted or represented at all stages of the procedure.

Article 2 further provides that the applicant may request to meet the officials responsible for examining his application in order to present his arguments to them and hear their possible remarks, questions and reservations.

It was specified in the explanatory memorandum of the aforementioned law that the request for an advance decision is a possibility offered to taxpayers and in no case an obligation. In this spirit, the applicant may withdraw his application at any stage of the procedure, without this clarification needing to be provided by regulatory means. The decree has therefore been adapted in this sense in accordance with the opinion of the Council of State.

Article 3 provides for a delegation to the Minister of Finance or to his delegate in order to designate the officials responsible for ruling on applications.

In accordance with the obligation to state reasons for administrative acts, the advance decision – favourable or unfavourable – must be clearly justified having regard in particular to the arguments put forward by the applicant throughout the procedure.

Article 4 provides that the decision is notified to the applicant by registered mail in order to ensure its proper reception by the person concerned.

Article 24 of the aforementioned law provides for the publication of advance decisions in a rapid and anonymous manner, in compliance with professional secrecy. Article 5 of this decree provides that advance decisions will be published in the form of individual or collective summaries. This publication may in particular be made, insofar as possible, on the website of the FPS Finance.

In this regard, the advance decision being able to have an international character where a foreign taxpayer and a foreign tax administration could be parties to the decision, it will be necessary to take into account the opinion of all these parties as to the publicity to be given or not to the decision.

Moreover, the notion of professional secrecy must be understood in the broad sense, and also take into account respect for the confidentiality of the applicant's business who may, for just reasons, claim the absence or postponement of publication of the decision concerning him.

In response to the remark of the Council of State with regard to this provision, it is clarified that the participation of any official of the FPS Finance whose collaboration, as indicated in the previous paragraph, would be useful in the examination of the application, is expressly provided for in this decree in order to prevent any possible challenge, based on other regulatory provisions, concerning the validity of decisions showing such collaboration. The advance decisions service with which applications must be submitted is indeed not required to examine these applications alone in all circumstances. Moreover, a provision with a similar objective of legal certainty in the context of providing assistance in the decision-making process was already provided for in the royal decree of 4 April 1995 relating to the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, confirmed by the law of 2 June 1998.

The draft that was submitted to the opinion of the Council of State provided: "Every service involved in the application must be consulted during the processing thereof." This provision was deleted from the decree taking into account the opinion of the Council of State. Consequently, care must be taken to ensure that, for the proper functioning of the new system, the services concerned by the application are consulted in accordance with appropriate procedures compatible with the objectives pursued by the introduction of the new advance decisions system.

It goes without saying that, in accordance with common law, the applicant may be assisted or represented during each stage of the procedure.

### Artikel 2

therefore provides that the applicant may request a meeting with the officials responsible for examining his application in order to set out his arguments to them and hear their possible remarks, questions and reservations.

In the explanatory memorandum of the aforementioned law it was clarified that the request for an advance decision is a possibility offered to taxpayers and in no way an obligation. In this line of thinking, the applicant may withdraw his application at any stage of the procedure, without this clarification needing to be provided by regulatory means. The decree was therefore brought into conformity in this sense with the opinion of the Council of State.

### Artikel 3

provides for a delegation to the Minister of Finance or his delegate to designate the officials who will be responsible for ruling on the applications.

In accordance with the duty to state reasons with regard to administrative acts, an advance decision – favourable or unfavourable – must be clearly reasoned, taking into account in particular the arguments put forward by the applicant throughout the entire procedure.

### Artikel 4

stipulates that the decision shall be notified to the applicant by registered letter sent by post in order to ensure its proper receipt by the interested party.

### Artikel 24

of the aforementioned law provides for the publication of advance decisions in a rapid and anonymous manner and with respect for professional secrecy.

### Artikel 5

of this decree provides that advance decisions shall be published in the form of individual or collective summaries. This publication may, to the extent possible, take place via the website of the FPS Finance.

Should an advance decision in this respect have an international character or should a foreign taxpayer or a foreign tax administration be a party to the decision in question, it would be appropriate to take into account the opinion of these parties regarding whether or not to publish that decision.

Moreover, the concept of professional secrecy must be interpreted in a broad sense so that account can also be taken of the confidential nature of the applicant's business interests, who may, on the basis of justified reasons, require that the decision not be published or that publication be postponed to a later date.

Article 6 sets the final date for submission to the Minister of Finance of the annual activity report at 30 June of the year following the reference year.

Article 7 contains the repealing measures of the regulatory provisions that governed the old "ruling" system.

Article 8 contains transitional provisions in order to allow the old provisions to continue to apply by way of derogation from Article 7 to applications submitted before the entry into force of the present provisions.

Following the remark of the Council of State, Article 8 has been supplemented by paragraphs 3 and 4 containing additional transitional measures.

Moreover, the provisions of this decree have no effect on agreements concluded and decisions taken before the entry into force of the new advance decision system.

I have the honour to be,
Sire,
Of Your Majesty,
The very respectful and very faithful servant, The Minister of Finance,
D. REYNDERS

30 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree taken in execution of Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
Having regard to Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters;
Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance, given on 5 December 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget, given on 5 December 2002;
Having regard to the urgency motivated by the fact:
—	that the provisions of this decree are taken in application of Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters;
—	that the provisions of Articles 20 to 28 of the same law apply from 1 January 2003;
—	that it is therefore appropriate to bring the provisions of this decree into force from the same date in order to ensure the immediate functioning of the system of advance decisions in tax matters;
Having regard to the opinion of the Council of State, given on 15 January 2003 in application of Article 84, paragraph 1, 2°, of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of Our Minister of Finance and on the opinion of Our Ministers who deliberated thereon in Council,
We have decreed and decree:

Article 1. Applications for advance decisions referred to in Article 20 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters shall be the subject of an acknowledgement of receipt issued to the applicant within a period of five working days from the date of receipt of the application.

### Artikel 6

sets the final date for submission to the Minister of Finance of the annual activity report at 30 June of the year following the reference year.

### Artikel 7

contains the repealing provisions of the regulatory provisions that applied to the old "ruling" practice.

### Artikel 8

contains transitional provisions in order to allow the old provisions to continue to apply, by way of derogation from Article 7, with regard to all applications that were submitted before the entry into force of the present provisions.

As a result of the remark of the Council of State, Article 8 has been supplemented by paragraphs 3 and 4 containing additional transitional provisions.

On the other hand, the provisions of this decree have no effect on agreements concluded and decisions taken before the entry into force of the new system of advance decisions.

I have the honour to be,
Sire,
Of Your Majesty,
The very respectful and very faithful servant, The Minister of Finance,
D. REYNDERS

30 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree in execution of Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters (1)

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Our Greetings.
Having regard to Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters;
Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance given on 5 December 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget of 5 December 2002;
Having regard to the urgent necessity motivated by the fact:
—	that the provisions of this decree are taken in execution of Article 26 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters;
—	that the provisions of Articles 20 to 28 of the same law take effect from 1 January 2003;
—	that it is therefore appropriate to bring the provisions of this decree into force on the same date in order to ensure the functioning of the system of advance decisions in tax matters immediately;
Having regard to the opinion of the Council of State given on 15 January 2003 in application of Article 84, first paragraph, 2°, of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of Our Minister of Finance, and on the opinion of Our Ministers assembled in Council,
We have decreed and decree:

### Artikel 1

Applications for advance decisions referred to in Article 20 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters shall be the subject of an acknowledgement of receipt issued to the applicant within a period of five working days from the date of receipt of the application.

### Art. 2

Any official of the Federal Public Service Finance may take part in the examination of the application.

The applicant may be heard on the initiative of the service or at his request.

### Art. 3

The Minister of Finance or his delegate designates the officials of the Federal Public Service Finance responsible for ruling on applications for advance decisions.

### Art. 4. The decision is notified to the applicant by registered mail

### Art. 5

Advance decisions are published in the form of individual or collective summaries.

### Art. 6

The annual report referred to in Article 25 of the Act of 24 December 2002 amending the corporate regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters is sent to the Minister of Finance no later than 30 June of the year following that to which the report relates.

### Art. 7

The Royal Decree of 4 April 1995 relating to the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, confirmed by the Act of 2 June 1998, the Royal Decree of 3 May 1999 organising a system of advance decisions in tax matters, the Ministerial Decree of 27 May 1999 granting delegation to rule on applications for advance decisions in tax matters and the Ministerial Decree of 6 June 2000 appointing the members of the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, amended by the Ministerial Decree of 4 January 2002, are repealed.

### Art. 8

§ 1er. The Royal Decree of 4 April 1995 relating to the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, confirmed by the Act of 2 June 1998, and the Ministerial Decree of 6 June 2000 appointing the members of the Commission for Advance Tax Rulings, amended by the Ministerial Decree of 4 January 2002, as they existed before being repealed by Article 7, continue to apply to applications for advance tax rulings submitted before the date of entry into force of this decree in accordance with Article 18, § 3 to § 6, of the Code of Registration, Mortgage and Clerk's Fees, and Article 345 of the Income Tax Code 1992, as they existed before their repeal by Article 27 of the Act of 24 December 2002 amending the corporate regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters, and Article 106, paragraph 2, of the Inheritance Tax Code, as it existed before being replaced by Article 28 of the same Act.

The Royal Decree of 3 May 1999 organising a system of advance decisions in tax matters and the Ministerial Decree of 27 May 1999 granting delegation to rule on applications for advance decisions in tax matters, as they existed before being repealed by Article 7, continue to apply to applications for advance decisions submitted before the date of entry into force of this decree.

### § 2

The advance decisions service established by Article 2 of the aforementioned Royal Decree of 3 May 1999 as it existed before being repealed by Article 7 continues to carry out its tasks until the decisions relating to the applications referred to in § 1, paragraphs 1 and 2, have been published and the reports relating to those activities have been submitted to the competent authority.

### § 3

Articles 20 to 28 of the Act of 24 December 2002 amending the corporate regime in matters of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters, the provisions adopted in application of Article 22, paragraph 2 of the same Act and the provisions of this decree apply:

1° to applications for advance tax rulings that were submitted between 1 January 2003 and 15 January 2003 in accordance with Article 18,

### § 3

Articles 20 to 28 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in respect of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters, the provisions implementing Article 22, second paragraph of the same law and the provisions of this decree are applicable:

1° to the applications for advance tax rulings that were submitted between 1 January 2003 and 15 January 2003 in accordance with Article 18, § 3 to § 6, of the Code of Registration, Mortgage and Clerk's Fees, and Article 345 of the Income Tax Code 1992, as they existed before their repeal by Article 27 of the law of 24 December 2002 amending the company regime in respect of income taxes and establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters, and to Article 106, second paragraph, of the Inheritance Tax Code, as it existed before being replaced by Article 28 of the same law;

2° to the applications for advance decisions that were submitted between 1 January 2003 and the date of publication of this decree in accordance with the royal decree of 3 May 1999 establishing a system of advance decisions in tax matters, as it existed before being repealed by Article 7.

### § 4

By way of derogation from Article 1, the acknowledgement of receipt may be issued until the fifth working day following the date of publication of this decree for applications for advance decisions that were submitted between 1 January 2003 and this date of publication, as well as for the applications referred to in paragraph 3.

### Art. 9. This decree takes effect on 1 January 2003

### Art. 10. Our Minister of Finance is charged with the execution of this decree

Given at Brussels, 30 January 2003.

ALBERT
By the King:

The Minister of Finance,

D. REYNDERS

Note
(1) References to the Belgian Official Gazette:
Law of 24 December 2002, Belgian Official Gazette of 31 December 2002,
edition 2.

Coordinated laws on the Council of State, Law of 12 January 1973, Belgian Official Gazette of 21 March 1973.

21 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree amending the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 30 and 31 July 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
Having regard to the law of 12 July 1976 relating to the compensation for certain damage caused to private property by natural calamities, in particular Article 2, § 1, 1°, and § 2;
Having regard to the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 30 and 31 July 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity;
Having regard to the requests of the Governors of the Provinces of Antwerp and Limburg seeking that the geographical extent of the disaster area be extended to the territory of other municipalities;
Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance given on 11 December 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of Our Minister of Budget of 16 January 2003;
On the proposal of Our Minister of the Interior and on the opinion of Our Ministers who deliberated thereon in Council,

We have decreed and decree:

Article 1. The following amendments are made to Article 2 of the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 30 and 31 July 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity:

1° under the heading "Province of Antwerp", the word "Balen" is added after the word "Arendonk";
2° under the heading "Province of Limburg", the word "Hasselt" is inserted between the words "Beringen" and "Hechtel-Eksel".

### Art. 2

This decree enters into force on the day of its publication in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 3

Our Minister of the Interior is charged with the execution of this decree.
Given at Brussels, 21 January 2003:

ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of the Interior,
A. DUQUESNE

### Artikel 1

In Article 2 of the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public disaster the abundant rainfall that occurred on 30 and 31 July 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this disaster, the following amendments are made:
1° under the title "Province of Antwerp", after the word "Arendonk" the word "Balen" is added;
2° under the title "Province of Limburg", between the words "Beringen" and "Hechtel-Eksel" the word "Hasselt" is inserted.

### Art. 2

This decree enters into force on the day it is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 3

Our Minister of the Interior is charged with the execution of this decree.
Given at Brussels, 21 January 2003.

ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of the Interior,
A. DUQUESNE

21 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree amending the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.

Having regard to the law of 12 July 1976 relating to the repair of certain damage caused to private property by natural calamities, in particular Article 2, § 1, 1°, and § 2;

Having regard to the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity;

Having regard to the requests of the Governor of the Province of Limburg seeking that the geographical extent of the disaster area be extended to the territory of the municipalities of Hasselt and Overpelt;

Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance given on 11 December 2002;

Having regard to the agreement of Our Minister of Budget of 16 January 2003;

21 JANUARY 2003. — Royal decree amending the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Our Greetings.

Having regard to the law of 12 July 1976 relating to the repair of certain damage caused to private property by natural calamities, in particular Article 2, § 1, 1°, and § 2;

Having regard to the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity;

Having regard to the requests of the Governor of the Province of Limburg to extend the geographical extent of the disaster area to the territory of the municipalities of Hasselt and Overpelt;

Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance given on 11 December 2002;

Having regard to the agreement of Our Minister of Budget of 16 January 2003;

On the proposal of Our Minister of the Interior and on the opinion of Our Ministers who deliberated in Council,
We have decreed and decree:
Article 1. In Article 2 of the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on 23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity, the following amendments are made: under the title "Province of Limburg":

1° the word "Hasselt" is inserted between the words "Bree" and "Houthalen-Helchteren";
2° the word "Overpelt" is inserted between the words "Maasmechelen" and
"Saint-Trond".

### Art. 2

This decree enters into force on the day of its publication in the Moniteur belge.

### Art. 3

Our Minister of the Interior is charged with the execution of this decree.
Given in Brussels, 21 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of the Interior,
A. DUQUESNE

On the proposal of Our Minister of the Interior and on the opinion of Our Ministers assembled in Council,
We have decreed and decree:

### Artikel 1

In Article 2 of the royal decree of 20 September 2002 considering as a public calamity the heavy rainfall that occurred on
23 and 24 August 2002 on the territory of several municipalities, and delimiting the geographical extent of this calamity, the following amendments are made: under the title "Province of Limburg":
1° the word "Hasselt" is inserted between the words "Bree" and "Houthalen-Helchteren";
2° the word "Overpelt" is inserted between the words "Maasmechelen" and "Sint-Truiden".

### Art. 2

This decree enters into force on the day on which it is published in the Belgisch Staatsblad.

### Art. 3

Our Minister of the Interior is charged with the execution of this decree.
Given at Brussels, 21 January 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of the Interior,
A. DUQUESNE
