N. 2003 — 579

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE PERSONNEL AND ORGANISATION
[C − 2003/02023]

3 FEBRUARY 2003. — Royal decree containing various regulatory measures with a view to simplifying certain administrative control procedures

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings.
Having regard to Articles 37 and 107, paragraph 2, of the Constitution;
Having regard to the royal decree of 12 May 1927 fixing the retirement age of civil servants, employees and service staff of the State administrations, in particular Article 3, paragraph 2, replaced by the royal decree of 13 September 1989;

Having regard to the royal decree of 29 June 1973 establishing the pecuniary status of the personnel of the federal public services, in particular Article 14, § 1, paragraph 2, inserted by the royal decree of 27 March 2001 and amended by the royal decree of 5 September 2002;

Having regard to the royal decree of 8 August 1983 relating to the exercise of a higher function in the State administrations, in particular Article 7, § 3, amended by the royal decree of 20 February 1989;
Having regard to the royal decree of 3 September 2000 regulating the State's and certain public bodies' contribution to the transport costs of federal personnel members and amending the royal decree of 20 April 1999 granting an allowance for the use of bicycles to members of the personnel of certain federal public services, in particular Articles 6, 9 and 10;
Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance, given on 19 August 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of Our Minister of Budget, given on 29 August 2002;

Having regard to protocol No. 435 of 17 September 2002 of the Committee of federal, community and regional public services;
Having regard to protocol N-156 of 18 October 2002 of the Negotiating Committee for military personnel of the armed forces;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Council of Ministers on the request for an opinion to be given by the Council of State within a period not exceeding one month;
Having regard to opinion 34.443/1 of the Council of State, given on 12 December 2002, in application of Article 84, paragraph 1, 1°, of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of Our Minister of the Civil Service and Modernisation of the Administration and on the advice of Our Ministers who deliberated thereon in Council,
We have decreed and decree:
Article 1. In Article 3, paragraph 2, of the royal decree of 12 May 1927 fixing the retirement age of civil servants, employees and service staff of the State administrations, replaced by the royal decree of 13 September 1989, the words "and subject to the agreement of the minister who has the civil service within his attributions" are deleted.


This excerpt sits under: MONITEUR BELGE > CHAPTER VI. — Miscellaneous provisions

### Art. 2

Article 14, § 1, paragraph 2, of the royal decree of 29 June 1973 establishing the remuneration regulations for staff of the federal public services, inserted by the royal decree of 27 March 2001, and amended by the royal decree of 5 September 2002, is repealed.

3 FEBRUARY 2003. — Royal decree containing various regulatory measures with a view to simplifying certain administrative control procedures

ALBERT II, King of the Belgians,
To all, present and to come, Greetings. Having regard to Articles 37 and 107, second paragraph, of the Constitution;
Having regard to the royal decree of 12 May 1927 concerning the retirement age of civil servants, officials and service staff of the State, in particular Article 3, second paragraph, replaced by the royal decree of 13 September 1989;
Having regard to the royal decree of 29 June 1973 establishing the remuneration regulations for staff of the federal public services, in particular Article 14, § 1, second paragraph, inserted by the royal decree of 27 March 2001 and amended by the royal decree of 5 September 2002;
Having regard to the royal decree of 8 August 1983 concerning the exercise of a higher office in the state administrations, in particular Article 7, § 3, amended by the royal decree of 20 February 1989;
Having regard to the royal decree of 3 September 2000 regulating the contribution of the State and certain public institutions to the transport costs of federal staff members and amending the royal decree of 20 April 1999 granting an allowance for the use of bicycles to staff members of certain federal public services, in particular Articles 6, 9 and 10;
Having regard to the opinion of the Inspector of Finance, given on 19 August 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of Our Minister of Budget of 29 August 2002;
Having regard to protocol No. 435 of 17 September 2002 of the Committee of federal community and regional public services;
Having regard to protocol N-156 of 18 October 2002 of the Negotiating Committee of the military personnel of the armed forces;
Having regard to the decision of the Council of Ministers on the request to the Council of State to give an opinion within a period of one month;
Having regard to opinion 34.443/1 of the Council of State given on 12 December 2002, in application of Article 84, first paragraph, 1° of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of Our Minister of the Civil Service and Modernisation of Public Administrations and on the opinion of Our Ministers assembled in Council,
We have decreed and decree:

### Article 1

In Article 3, second paragraph, of the royal decree of 12 May 1927 concerning the retirement age of civil servants, officials and service staff of the State, replaced by the royal decree of 13 September 1989, the words "and subject to the agreement of the Minister responsible for the Civil Service" are deleted.

### Art. 2

Article 14, § 1, second paragraph, of the royal decree of 29 June 1973 establishing the remuneration regulations for staff of the federal public services, inserted by the royal decree of 27 March 2001, and amended by the royal decree of 5 September 2002, is repealed.

### Art. 3

In Article 7, § 3, of the royal decree of 8 August 1983 concerning the exercise of a higher function in the state administrations, amended by the royal decree of 20 February 1989, the following amendments are made:
1° in paragraph 5, littera b), the words "with the agreement of the Minister responsible for the Civil Service" are deleted;
2° paragraphs 6 and 7 are repealed.

### Art. 4

In Article 6 of the royal decree of 3 September 2000 regulating the contribution of the State and certain public institutions to the transport costs of federal staff members and amending the royal decree of 20 April 1999 granting an allowance for the use of bicycles to staff members of certain federal public services, the words "or as a complete network subscription" are inserted between the words "as an urban subscription to a network" and the words ", regardless of the distance".

### Art. 5. Article 9 of the same decree is replaced by the following provision

"Art. 9. - The staff member who does not use public transport daily obtains a contribution of 80% towards the price of travel cards, on condition that the amount of this contribution is monthly less than that which would result from the use of a subscription and that the transport ticket is used exclusively for the journey from home to the place of work."

### Art. 6

In Article 10 of the same decree, the words "In application of Article 9 and" are deleted.

### Art. 7

This decree enters into force on the first day of the month following that in which it is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 8

Our Ministers and Our Secretaries of State are charged, each insofar as it concerns them, with the execution of this decree.
Given at Brussels, 3 February 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Budget,
J. VANDE LANOTTE
The Minister of the Civil Service
and Modernisation of the Administration,
L. VAN DEN BOSSCHE

### Art. 3

In Article 7, § 3, of the royal decree of 8 August 1983 concerning the exercise of a higher office in the state administrations, amended by the royal decree of 20 February 1989, the following amendments are made:
1° in the fifth paragraph, littera b), the words "with the agreement of the Minister responsible for the Civil Service" are deleted;
2° the sixth and seventh paragraphs are repealed.

### Art. 4

In Article 6 of the royal decree of 3 September 2000 regulating the contribution of the State and certain public institutions to the transport costs of federal staff members and amending the royal decree of 20 April 1999 granting an allowance for the use of bicycles to staff members of certain federal public services, the words "or as a complete network subscription" are inserted between the words "as an urban subscription to a network" and the words ", regardless of the distance".

### Art. 5. Article 9 of the same decree is replaced as follows

"Art. 9. - The staff member who does not use public transport daily receives a contribution of 80% towards the price of travel cards, on condition that the amount of that contribution is monthly less than the amount that would result from the use of a subscription and that the transport ticket is used exclusively for the distance between the place of residence and the place of work."

### Art. 6. In Article 10 of the same decree, the words

"In application of Article 9 and" are deleted.

### Art. 7

This decree enters into force on the first day of the month following that in which it is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.

### Art. 8

Our Ministers and Our State Secretaries are, each for what concerns them, charged with the execution of this decree.
Given in Brussels, 3 February 2003.
ALBERT
By the King:
The Minister of Budget,
J. VANDE LANOTTE
The Minister of Civil Service
and Modernisation of Public Administrations,
L. VAN DEN BOSSCHE

GOUVERNEMENTS DE COMMUNAUTE ET DE REGION GEMEENSCHAPS- EN GEWESTREGERINGEN GEMEINSCHAFTS- UND REGIONALREGIERUNGEN

COMMUNAUTE FRANÇAISE — FRANSE GEMEENSCHAP

F. 2003 — 580

MINISTERE DE LA COMMUNAUTE FRANÇAISE

[C − 2003/29006]

5 DECEMBER 2002. — Decree of the Government of the French Community amending the decree of 10 October 2002 relating to the procedure for monitoring the practice of doping, and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 relating to the promotion of health in the practice of sport, the prohibition of doping and its prevention in the French Community
The Government of the French Community,
Having regard to the decree of 8 March 2001 relating to the promotion of health in the practice of sport, the prohibition of doping and its prevention in the French Community, in particular Article 10;
Having regard to the decree of 10 October 2002 relating to the procedure for monitoring the practice of doping, and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 relating to the promotion of health in the practice of sport, the prohibition of doping and its prevention in the French Community, in particular Article 15;
Having regard to the opinion of the French-speaking Commission for the promotion of health in the practice of sport, given on 22 November 2002;
Having regard to the opinion of the Higher Council for Physical Education, Sports and Outdoor Life, given on 29 November 2002;

Having regard to the urgency motivated by the fact that the new list of prohibited substances and methods of the International Olympic Committee enters into force on 1 January 2003, and that the list applicable in the French Community must conform to this list;
On the proposal of the Minister having Health in her attributions;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of the French Community of 5 December 2002; Decrees:
Article 1. The annex to the decree of 10 October 2002 relating to the procedure for monitoring the practice of doping, and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 relating to the promotion of health in the practice of sport, the prohibition of doping and its prevention in the French Community, is replaced by the annex attached to this decree.

### Art. 2

This decree enters into force on 1 January 2003. Brussels, 5 December 2002.
For the Government of the French Community,
The Minister for Youth Assistance and Health, Mrs N. MARECHAL

Annex to the decree of 10 October 2002 relating to the procedure for controlling the practice of doping, and establishing the entry into force of the decree of 8 March 2001 relating to the promotion of health in the practice of sport, the prohibition of doping and its prevention in the French Community, amended by the decree of 5 December 2002
List of prohibited substances and methods
I. PROHIBITED CLASSES OF SUBSTANCES
A. Stimulants
a. The prohibited substances belonging to class (A. a.) include the following examples, as well as their L and D isomers:
amiphenazole, amphetamines, bromantan, caffeine*, carphedon, cocaine, ephedrines**, fencamfamine, mesocarb, pentetrazol, pipradol,
... and related substances.
* For caffeine, a concentration in urine exceeding 12 micrograms per millilitre will be considered as a positive result.
** For ephedrine and methylephedrine, a concentration in urine exceeding 10 micrograms per millilitre will be considered as a positive result.
For cathine, a concentration in urine exceeding 5 micrograms per millilitre will be considered as a positive result.
For phenylpropanolamine and pseudoephedrine, a concentration in urine exceeding 25 micrograms per millilitre will be considered as a positive result.
NOTE: All imidazole preparations are acceptable for local application. Vasoconstrictors may be administered with local anaesthetic agents. Preparations for local use (for example by nasal, ophthalmological, rectal route) of adrenaline are permitted. Bupropion, synephrine and phenylephrine are permitted.
b. The prohibited substances belonging to class (A. b.) include the following examples as well as their L and D isomers:
Formoterol***, salbutamol***, salmeterol*** and terbutaline***
... and related substances
*** Substance permitted by inhalation only to prevent and/or treat asthma and exercise-induced asthma, provided that this authorisation is prior, and issued by the competent medical authority, in application of the regulation of the sports federation or the international sports federation.
B. Narcotics
The prohibited substances belonging to class (B) include the following examples:
buprenorphine, dextromoramide, diamorphine (heroin), methadone, morphine, pentazocine, pethidine,... and related substances.
NOTE: Codeine, dextromethorphan, dextropropoxyphene, dihydrocodeine, diphenoxylate, ethylmorphine, pholcodine, propoxyphene and tramadol are permitted.
C. Anabolic agents
The prohibited substances belonging to class (C) include the following examples:
1. Androgenic anabolic steroids
a/ clostebol, fluoxymesterone, metandienone, metenolone, nandrolone, 19-norandrostenediol, 19-norandrostenedione, oxandrolone, stanozolol,
... and related substances.
b/ androstenediol, androstenedione, dehydroepiandrosterone (DHEA), dihydrotestosterone, testosterone*,
... and related substances.
* The presence of a testosterone (T)-epitestosterone (E) ratio greater than six (6) in the urine of an athlete constitutes an offence unless it is established that this ratio is due to a physiological or pathological condition, e.g. low excretion of epitestosterone, androgenic production from a tumour or enzymatic deficiencies.
In the case of a T/E ratio greater than 6, the sports federation or the international sports federation may, in application of its regulation, have an examination carried out under the direction of its competent medical authority before the sample is declared positive. A full report will be drawn up; it will include where appropriate a study of previous and subsequent tests as well as the results of endocrine tests. If previous tests are not available, the athlete must undergo an unannounced test at least once a month for three months. The results of these examinations must be included in the report. In the absence of cooperation on the part of the athlete, this will result in a declaration of a positive sample.
NOTES: Evidence obtained from metabolic profiles and/or the study of isotopic ratios may be used in order to draw definitive conclusions.
2. Other anabolic agents clenbuterol, salbutamol*

* For salbutamol, a urinary concentration of non-sulfated salbutamol exceeding 1000 nanograms per millilitre will be considered as a positive result.
D. Diuretics
The prohibited substances belonging to class (D) include the following examples:
acetazolamide, ethacrynic acid, bumetanide, chlorthalidone, furosemide, hydrochlorothiazide, mannitol*, mersalyl, spironolactone, triamterene,
... and related substances.
* Prohibited substance if injected by intravenous route.
E. Peptide hormones, mimetic substances and analogues
The prohibited substances belonging to class (E) include the following substances and their analogues as well as mimetic substances:
1. Chorionic gonadotrophin (hCG) in men only.
2. Pituitary and synthetic gonadotrophins in men only.
3. Corticotrophins (ACTH, tetracosactide).
4. Growth hormone (hGH).
5. Insulin-like growth factor (IGF-1) and all their respective releasing factors as well as their analogues.
6. Erythropoietin (EPO).
7. Insulin*.
* Insulin may be permitted only to treat athletes suffering from insulin-dependent diabetes, provided that this authorisation is prior, and issued by the competent medical authority, according to the regulation of the sports federation or the international sports federation.
NOTE: The presence in the urine of an athlete of an abnormal concentration of an endogenous hormone belonging to class (E) or of its diagnostic marker(s) constitutes an offence unless it is proved that it is due to a physiological or pathological condition.
F. Agents having an anti-oestrogenic action.
Aromatase inhibitors, clomiphene, cyclofenil, tamoxifen are prohibited in men only.
G. Masking agents.
The prohibited substances of class (G) include the following examples:
Diuretics, epitestosterone*, probenecid, plasma substitutes (such as hydroxyethyl starch)
Masking agents are prohibited. These are products which have the capacity to hinder the excretion of prohibited substances or to conceal their presence in urine or other samples used in doping control.
* The presence of a concentration of epitestosterone exceeding 200 ng/mL in urine constitutes an offence unless it is established that it is due to a physiological condition. Isotope ratio mass spectrometry (IRMS) may be used to draw definitive conclusions. If the results of IRMS are not conclusive, the competent medical authority will conduct an investigation before the sample is declared positive.
II. PROHIBITED METHODS
The following methods are prohibited:
A. Enhancement of oxygen transfer
Blood doping: this is the administration of autologous, homologous or heterologous blood, or red blood cell products, other than for legitimate therapeutic purposes.
The administration of products which enhance the uptake, transport or delivery of oxygen, such as products based on modified haemoglobins including in a non-exhaustive manner bovine and cross-linked haemoglobins, products based on microencapsulated haemoglobin, perfluorocarbons and RSR 13.
B. Pharmacological, chemical and physical manipulation
Pharmacological, chemical and physical manipulation is the use of substances and methods, masking agents (see I.G), which alter, attempt to alter or may reasonably be expected to alter the integrity and validity of urine samples used in doping controls, such as, in a non-exhaustive manner, catheterisation, substitution and/or alteration of urine, inhibition of renal excretion and modification of measurements carried out on testosterone and epitestosterone (see I.G).
C. Gene doping
Gene or genetic doping is defined as the non-therapeutic use of genes, genetic elements and/or cells which have the capacity to enhance sporting performance.
III. CLASSES OF SUBSTANCES SUBJECT TO CERTAIN RESTRICTIONS
A. Alcohol
Where the regulation of a competent sports authority (sports federation or international federation) so provides, tests will be carried out for ethanol.
B. Cannabinoids
Where the regulation of a competent national or international sports federation so provides, tests will be carried out for cannabinoids (such as marijuana and hashish).
C. Local anaesthetics
Injectable local anaesthetics are permitted under the following conditions:
a) bupivacaine, lidocaine, mepivacaine, procaine and related substances may be used but not cocaine; vasoconstrictive agents may be used in conjunction with local anaesthetics;
b) only local or intra-articular injections may be administered;
c) only when the administration is medically justified.
Where the regulation of a competent sports authority so provides, it may prove necessary to notify the administration of local anaesthetics.
D. Glucocorticosteroids
The systemic use of glucocorticosteroids is prohibited when they are administered by oral or rectal route or by intravenous or intramuscular injection.
In the case of medical necessity, local and intra-articular injections of glucocorticosteroids are permitted. Where the regulation of a competent sports authority so provides, it may prove necessary to notify the administration of glucocorticosteroids.
E. Beta-blockers

The prohibited substances belonging to class (E) include the following examples:
acebutolol, alprenolol, atenolol, labetalol, metoprolol, nadolol, oxprenolol, propranolol, sotalol,
... and related substances.
Where the regulation of a competent sports authority so provides, tests will be carried out for beta-blockers.
IV. SUMMARY TABLE OF CONCENTRATIONS IN URINE ABOVE WHICH THERE IS AN OFFENCE.
Caffeine > 12 micrograms/millilitre Carboxy-THC > 15 nanograms/millilitre Cathine > 5 micrograms/millilitre
Ephedrine > 10 micrograms/millilitre Epitestosterone > 200 nanograms/millilitre Methylephedrine > 10 micrograms/millilitre Morphine > 1 microgram/millilitre
19-norandrosterone > 2 nanograms/millilitre in men 19-norandrosterone > 5 nanograms/millilitre in women Phenylpropanolamine > 25 micrograms/millilitre
Pseudoephedrine > 25 micrograms/millilitre
Salbutamol (as anabolic agent) > 1000 nanograms/millilitre T/E ratio* > 6
* Mentioned in points I.C.b. and I.G.
LIST OF EXAMPLES OF PROHIBITED SUBSTANCES
WARNING: This is not an exhaustive list of prohibited substances. Many substances which are not listed in this list are considered as prohibited under the designation related substances.
Athletes must ensure that any medication, supplement, over-the-counter preparation or any other substance they use does not contain any prohibited substance.
STIMULANTS:
amfepramone, amiphenazole, amphetamine, bambuterol, bromantan, caffeine, carphedon, cathine, clobenzorex, cocaine, cropropamide, crotetamide, ephedrine, etamivan, etilamfetamine, etilefrine, fencamfamine, fenetylline, fenfluramine, fenproporex, formoterol, heptaminol, mefenorex, mephentermine, mesocarb, methamphetamine, methoxyphenamine, methylenedioxyamphetamine, methylenedioxymethamphetamine, methylephedrine, methylphenidate, nikethamide, norfenfluramine, parahydroxyamphetamine, pemoline, pentetrazol, phendimetrazine, phenmetrazine, phentermine, phenylpropanolamine, pholedrine, pipradol, prolintane, propylhexedrine, pseudoephedrine, reproterol, salbutamol, salmeterol, selegiline, strychnine, terbutaline.
NARCOTICS:
buprenorphine, dextromoramide, diamorphine (heroin), hydrocodone, methadone, morphine, pentazocine, pethidine.
ANABOLIC AGENTS:
androstenediol, androstenedione, bambuterol, bolasterone, boldenone, clenbuterol, clostebol, danazol, dehydrochloromethyltestosterone, dehydroepiandrosterone (DHEA), dihydrotestosterone, drostanolone, fenoterol, fluoxymesterone, formebolone, formoterol, gestrinone, mesterolone, metandienone, metenolone, methandriol, methyltestosterone, mibolerone, nandrolone, 19-norandrostenediol, 19-norandrostenedione, norboletone, norethandrolone, oxandrolone, oxymesterone, oxymetholone, reproterol, salbutamol, salmeterol, stanozolol, terbutaline, testosterone, trenbolone.
DIURETICS:
amiloride, acetazolamide, ethacrynic acid, bendroflumethiazide, bumetanide, canrenone, chlorthalidone, furosemide, hydrochlorothiazide, indapamide, mannitol (by intravenous injection), mersalyl, spironolactone, triamterene.
MASKING AGENTS:
diuretics (cf. above), epitestosterone, probenecid, hydroxyethyl starch. PEPTIDE HORMONES, MIMETIC SUBSTANCES AND ANALOGUES:
ACTH, erythropoietin (EPO), hCG*, hGH, insulin, LH*, IGF-1. SUBSTANCES HAVING AN ANTI-OESTROGENIC ACTION
clomiphene*, cyclofenil*, tamoxifen*.
* Substances prohibited in men only. BETA-BLOCKERS:
acebutolol, alprenolol, atenolol, betaxolol, bisoprolol, bunolol, carteolol, carvedilol, celiprolol, esmolol, labetalol, levobunolol, metipranolol, metoprolol, nadolol, oxprenolol, pindolol, propranolol, sotalol, timolol.
Seen to be annexed to the decree of the Government of 5 December 2002,

For the Government of the French Community,
The Minister for Youth Assistance and Health, Mrs N. MARECHAL

N. 2003 — 580

MINISTRY OF THE FRENCH COMMUNITY

[C − 2003/29006]

5 DECEMBER 2002. — Decree of the Government of the French Community amending the decree of the Government of the French Community of 10 October 2002 concerning the control procedure for the practice of doping and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 concerning the promotion of health in sports practice, the prohibition of doping and the prevention of doping in the French Community
The Government of the French Community,
Having regard to the decree of 8 March 2001 concerning the promotion of health in sports practice, the prohibition of doping and the prevention of doping in the French Community, in particular Article 10;
Having regard to the decree of the Government of the French Community of 10 October 2002 concerning the control procedure for the practice of doping and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 concerning the promotion of health in sports practice, the prohibition of doping and the prevention of doping in the French Community, in particular Article 15;
Having regard to the opinion of the French-speaking Commission for the promotion of health in sports practice, given on 22 November 2002;
Having regard to the opinion of the High Council for Physical Education, Sport and Outdoor Life of the French Community, given on 29 November 2002;
Whereas there is an urgent necessity because the new list of prohibited substances and methods of the International Olympic Committee enters into force on 1 January 2003, and because the list applicable in the French Community must be in conformity with that list;
On the proposal of the Minister with responsibility for health;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of the French Community of 5 December 2002; Decrees:

### Article 1

The annex to the decision of the Government of the French Community of 10 October 2002 concerning the control procedure for doping practices and establishing the entry into force of certain provisions of the decree of 8 March 2001 concerning the promotion of health in sports practice, the prohibition of doping and the prevention of doping in the French Community, is replaced by the annex attached to this decision.

### Art. 2

This decision enters into force on 1 January 2003. Brussels, 5 December 2002.

F. 2003 — 581

On behalf of the Government of the French Community,
The Minister for Aid to Youth and Health, Mrs N. MARECHAL
*
MINISTRY OF THE FRENCH COMMUNITY

5 DECEMBER 2002. — Decree of the Government of the French Community

[C − 2003/29019]

amending the decree of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school
The Government of the French Community,

Having regard to the decree of 20 December 2001 concerning health promotion at school, in particular Article 28, §§ 1 and 9, amended by the decrees of 16 May and 20 June 2002;
Having regard to the decree of the Government of the French Community of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school;
Having regard to opinion No. 7 of the Commission for health promotion at school, given on 16 September 2002; Having regard to the opinion of the Finance Inspectorate, given on 25 September 2002;
Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget, given on 26 September 2002;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of 26 September 2002 on the request for an opinion to be given by the Council of State within a period not exceeding one month;
Having regard to opinion No. 34.212/4 of the Council of State, given on 19 November 2002, in application of Article 84, paragraph 1, 1°, of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of the Minister having Health in his or her powers;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of the French Community of 5 December 2002, Decrees:
Article 1.

### § 1

In Article 1, § 1, of the decree of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school, an Article 5°bis is inserted, worded as follows:
"5°bis. a head of establishment of ordinary or special secondary education of the French Community, or a head of establishment of ordinary or special fundamental education of the French Community, proposed by the competent Minister; the full member and the substitute shall be from a different level of education;"

### § 2

Article 1 of the same decree is supplemented by a § 3, worded as follows:
"When an opinion concerning health promotion in higher education is placed on the agenda of the commission, the commission is also composed of the following members:
1° an official of the General Service of Higher Education Institutions and higher artistic education, designated by the Secretary-General of the Ministry of the French Community;
2° two representatives of organisations representing students at community level, chosen after consultation with these organisations."

### Art. 2

The Minister having Health in his or her powers is responsible for the execution of this decree. Brussels, 5 December 2002.
On behalf of the Government of the French Community: The Minister for Aid to Youth and Health, Mrs N. MARECHAL

N. 2003 — 581

TRANSLATION
MINISTRY OF THE FRENCH COMMUNITY

5 DECEMBER 2002. — Decision of the Government of the French Community

[C − 2003/29019]

amending the decision of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school
The Government of the French Community,
Having regard to the decree of 20 December 2001 concerning health promotion at school, in particular Article 28, §§ 1 and 9, amended by the decrees of 16 May and 20 June 2002;
Having regard to the decision of the Government of the French Community of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school;
Having regard to opinion No. 7 of the commission for health promotion at school, given on 16 September 2002;
Having regard to the opinion of the Finance Inspectorate, given on 25 September 2002; Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget, given on 26 September 2002;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of 26 September 2002 on the request for an opinion to be given by the Council of State within a period of at most one month;
Having regard to opinion No. 34.212/4 of the Council of State, given on 19 November 2002, in application of Article 84, paragraph 1, 1° of the coordinated laws on the Council of State;
On the proposal of the Minister to whose powers Health belongs;
Having regard to the deliberation of the Government of the French Community of 5 December 2002, Decides:

### Article 1

§ 1. In Article 1, § 1, of the decision of 8 February 2002 concerning the commission for health promotion at school, an Article 5°bis is inserted, worded as follows:
"5°bis. A head of establishment of ordinary or special secondary education of the French Community or a head of establishment of ordinary or special basic education of the French Community, proposed by the competent Minister; the full member and the substitute member must belong to a different level of education;"

### § 2

Article 1 of the same decision is supplemented by a § 3, worded as follows:
"When an opinion concerning health promotion in higher education is submitted to the commission, the commission is also composed of the following members:
1° an official of the General Service of Higher Education Institutions and higher artistic education, appointed by the Secretary-General of the Ministry of the French Community;
2° two representatives of organisations representing students at community level, chosen after consultation with these organisations."

### Art. 2

The Minister to whose powers Health belongs is responsible for the execution of this decision. Brussels, 5 December 2002.

On behalf of the Government of the French Community:
The Minister for Aid to Youth and Health, Mrs N. MARECHAL

MINISTRY OF THE WALLOON REGION
F. 2003 — 583	[C − 2003/27104]
19 DECEMBER 2002. — Decree of the Walloon Government amending the decree of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 on the integration of foreign persons or persons of foreign origin

The Walloon Government,

Having regard to the decree of 4 July 1996 on the integration of foreign persons or persons of foreign origin, the staff salary scales;
Having regard to the decree of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 on the integration of foreign persons or persons of foreign origin;
Having regard to the opinion of the Finance Inspectorate; Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget;
Having regard to the collective labour agreement C of 16 September 2002 defining the job classification and remuneration conditions for the sectors of the Joint Committee for the socio-cultural sector dependent on the Walloon Region: training-through-work enterprises, socio-professional integration organisations, regional integration centres for populations of foreign origin, regional employment missions and training and/or professional rehabilitation centres approved by the Walloon Agency for the Professional Integration of Persons with Disabilities;
Having regard to the laws on the Council of State, coordinated on 12 January 1973, in particular Article 3, § 1, amended by the laws of 4 July 1989 and 4 August 1996;
Having regard to the urgency specially motivated by the fact that, on the one hand, the collective labour agreement of 16 September 2002 provides, in its Article 8, for its retroactivity from 1 October 2001 in accordance with the non-commercial agreement and that, on the other hand, the centres are experiencing significant cash flow difficulties;
On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health; After deliberation,
Decrees:
Article 1. This decree regulates, in application of Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in Article 128,

### § 1, thereof

### Art. 2

Article 12 of the decree of the Walloon Government of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 on the integration of foreign persons or persons of foreign origin is replaced by the following provision:
« The person responsible for day-to-day management must possess:
1° a diploma of higher education, university or non-university, long-type or short-type; 2° useful experience of at least five years and professional experience of at least three years;
3° a supplementary diploma or certificate attesting to knowledge acquired in the management of the non-commercial sector. The Minister determines the diplomas and certificates that satisfy this requirement.
Useful experience includes experience in the sector and experience in the profession in the field of training delivered.
Personnel expenses are only taken into account for the calculation of the subsidy if they do not exceed the salary scales relating to their function and annexed to this decree.
The salary scale taken into account from 1 October 2004 for the person responsible for day-to-day management is the scale set out in Annex 2.

If the person responsible for day-to-day management was engaged after 1 October 2001, the scales set out in Annex 2bis must be applied for the period from 1 October 2001 to 1 October 2004.

If the person responsible for day-to-day management was engaged before 1 October 2001, the remuneration of said person must be calculated, for the period from 1 October 2001 to 1 October 2004, in accordance with the calculation grid determined by Annex 3 of the collective labour agreement defining the job classification and remuneration conditions for certain sectors dependent on the Walloon Region of the Joint Committee for the socio-cultural sector: E.F.T., O.I.S.P., CRI, MIRE, C.F.P. of the AWIPH signed on 16 September 2002.

For the position of secretary, the salary scale taken into account from 1 October 2004 is set out in Annex 3 or 4 depending on whether the worker holds a diploma of upper secondary education or a diploma of lower secondary education.

If the secretary was engaged after 1 October 2001, the scales set out in Annex 3bis or 4bis must be applied for the period from 1 October 2001 to 1 October 2004, depending on whether the worker holds a diploma of upper secondary education or a diploma of lower secondary education.

If the secretary was engaged before 1 October 2001, the remuneration of said secretary must be calculated, for the period from 1 October 2001 to 1 October 2004, in accordance with the calculation grid determined by Annex 3 of the collective labour agreement defining the job classification and remuneration conditions for certain sectors dependent on the Walloon Region of the Joint Committee for the socio-cultural sector: E.F.T., O.I.S.P., CRI, MIRE, C.F.P. of the AWIPH signed on 16 September 2002.

The salary scale and the annexed scales are intended to cover the gross salary to which employer social security contributions must be added, capped at 50% of the gross salary. »

### Art. 3

Article 14 of the decree of the Walloon Government of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 on the integration of foreign persons or persons of foreign origin is replaced by the following provision:

« With the exception of the subsidies referred to in Article 13, paragraph 3, an annual advance corresponding to 85% of the amount of subsidies granted in the previous year is granted to the approved centre during the first quarter of the calendar year.

To obtain payment of this advance, the service makes a request by returning to the administration a form established by it.

The balance is paid before 1 June of the following year, upon presentation of supporting documents for the expenses. »

### Art. 4

Annex 2 is repealed and replaced by Annexes 2, 2bis, 3, 3bis, 4 and 4bis of this decree.

### Art. 5

Persons responsible for day-to-day management engaged before the date of entry into force of this decree are presumed to fulfil the conditions set by Article 2 of this decree.

### Art. 6

Subject to paragraph 2, this decree enters into force on the day of its adoption by the Government. Article 2 produces its effects on 1 October 2001.

### Art. 7

The Minister of Social Affairs is responsible for the execution of this decree. Namur, 19 December 2002.
The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

N. 2003 — 583

MINISTRY OF THE WALLOON REGION

[C − 2003/27104]

19 DECEMBER 2002. — Decree of the Walloon Government amending the decree of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 concerning the integration of foreigners or persons of foreign origin

The Walloon Government,

Having regard to the decree of 4 July 1996 concerning the integration of foreigners or persons of foreign origin;

Having regard to the decree of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 concerning the integration of foreigners or persons of foreign origin;
Having regard to the opinion of the Finance Inspectorate; Having regard to the agreement of the Minister of Budget;
Having regard to the collective labour agreement of 16 September 2002 concerning the classification of functions and remuneration conditions for the sectors of the joint committee for the socio-cultural sector falling under the Walloon Region, namely: on-the-job training companies, institutions for social integration and integration into the labour process, regional centres for the integration of persons of foreign origin, regional missions for employment mediation and training and/or retraining centres recognised by the "Agence wallonne pour l'intégration professionnelle des personnes handicapées" (Walloon Agency for the Vocational Integration of Disabled Persons);

Having regard to the laws on the Council of State, coordinated on 12 January 1973, in particular Article 3, amended by the laws of 4 July 1989 and 4 August 1996;

Having regard to the urgent necessity, specially motivated by the fact that the collective labour agreement of 16 September 2002 in its Article 8 provides that it has retroactive effect from 1 October 2001 pursuant to the non-profit agreement, on the one hand, and that the centres are facing serious treasury problems, on the other hand;

On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health; After deliberation,
Decrees:

### Article 1

This decree regulates, in accordance with Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in Article 128, § 1, of the Constitution.

### Art. 2

Article 12 of the decree of the Walloon Government of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 concerning the integration of foreigners or persons of foreign origin is replaced as follows:
"The person responsible for day-to-day management has:
1° a diploma of higher education, whether or not university, of the long or short type; 2° at least five years of useful experience and at least three years of professional experience;
3° an additional diploma or certificate confirming the knowledge acquired in the field of management in the non-profit sector. The Minister determines the diplomas and certificates that meet those requirements.
Useful experience means experience gained in the sector and professional experience in the field of the training provided.
Staff expenses are only taken into account in the calculation of the subsidy if they do not exceed the salary scales attached to this decree.
The salary scale taken into account from 1 October 2004 in the case of the person responsible for day-to-day management is set out in Annex 2.
If the person responsible for day-to-day management was employed before 1 October 2001, the period between 1 October 2001 and 1 October 2004 falls under the application of the salary scales set out in Annex 2bis.
If the person responsible for day-to-day management was employed before 1 October 2001, his salary for the period between 1 October 2001 and 1 October 2004 is calculated in accordance with the method referred to in Annex 5 to the collective labour agreement of 16 September 2002 concerning the classification of functions and remuneration conditions for certain sectors falling under the Walloon Region of the joint committee of the socio-cultural sector, namely: the "E.F.T.", the "O.I.S.P.", the "CRI", the "MIRE", the "C.F.P." of the "AWIPH".

7056	MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

The salary scale concerning the post of secretary, which is taken into account from 1 October 2004, is set out in Annex 3 or 4, depending on whether the employee holds a diploma of higher or lower secondary education.
If the secretary was employed before 1 October 2001, the period between 1 October 2001 and 1 October 2004 falls under the application of the salary scales referred to in Annex 3bis or 4bis, depending on whether the employee holds a diploma of higher or lower secondary education.
If the secretary was employed before 1 October 2001, his salary for the period between 1 October 2001 and 1 October 2004 is calculated in accordance with the method referred to in Annex 5 to the collective labour agreement of 16 September 2002 concerning the classification of functions and remuneration conditions for certain sectors falling under the Walloon Region of the joint committee of the socio-cultural sector, namely: the "E.F.T.", the "O.I.S.P.", the "CRI", the "MIRE", the "C.F.P." of the "AWIPH".
The salary scale and the attached scales serve to cover the gross salary, to which employer charges must be added up to a maximum of 50% of the gross salary."

### Art. 3

Article 14 of the decree of the Walloon Government of 6 March 1997 implementing the decree of 4 July 1996 concerning the integration of foreigners or persons of foreign origin is replaced as follows:
"With the exception of the subsidies referred to in Article 13, third paragraph, the recognised centre receives during the first quarter of the calendar year an annual advance equal to 85% of the amount of the subsidies granted during the preceding year.
The service requests the administration for payment of that advance by means of a form, the model of which is determined by the administration.
The balance is settled before 1 June of the following year upon presentation of the documents confirming the expenditure."

### Art. 4

Annex 2 is repealed and replaced by Annexes 2, 2bis, 3, 3bis, 4 and 4bis of this decree.

### Art. 5

Persons responsible for day-to-day management who are employed before the entry into force of this decree are deemed to meet the conditions laid down in Article 2 of this decree.

### Art. 6

This decree enters into force on the day it is adopted by the Government, subject to the second paragraph.

### Art. 7

The Minister of Social Affairs is responsible for the implementation of this decree. Namur, 19 December 2002.
The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

F. 2003 — 584

MINISTRY OF THE WALLOON REGION

[2003/200089]

9 JANUARY 2003. — Decree of the Walloon Government approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council for mental health services
The Walloon Government,
Having regard to the decree of 4 April 1996 organising the approval and subsidisation of mental health services, in particular Article 24;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 7 November 1996 implementing the decree of 4 April 1996 organising the approval and subsidisation of mental health services, in particular section 12;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 regulating the functioning of the Government;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 establishing the distribution of powers between the Ministers and regulating the signing of acts of the Government;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 24 October 2002 appointing the members of the Regional Council for mental health services;
Considering the rules of procedure established by the Regional Council for mental health services at its meeting of 23 December 2002;
Considering that it is for the Walloon Government to approve the rules of procedure of the Regional Council for mental health services;
On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health; After deliberation,
Decrees:
Article 1. This decree regulates, in application of Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in Article 128, § 1, thereof.

### Art. 2

The Walloon Government approves the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of Mental Health Services, appearing in the annex to this decree.

### Art. 3

The decree of 20 February 1997 approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of Mental Health Services is repealed.

### Art. 4

This decree enters into force on the date of its adoption by the Government.

### Art. 5

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health is responsible for the implementation of this decree. Namur, 9 January 2003.
The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

Annex
REGIONAL COUNCIL OF MENTAL HEALTH SERVICES
Rules of procedure CHAPTER I. — General provisions
Article 1. The opinion requested from the Council by the Minister shall be delivered, unless otherwise provided, within two months unless
the Government has set another deadline.
Upon reasoned request, the Minister may request an urgent opinion. In that case, he shall set a reasonable deadline.

### Art. 2

At the beginning of each calendar year, the Council shall set the schedule of ordinary meetings.

## CHAPTER II. — Convocations

### Art. 3

The President shall convene the members of the Council as well as the persons who attend the meetings with a consultative vote.

### Art. 4

The convocation shall be sent at least eight days before the meeting to the members and shall systematically contain a proxy form.
If it has not been sent previously, the minutes of the previous meeting shall be attached to the convocation.
It shall state the agenda of the meeting. The convocations shall be sent to the address notified by the members. They shall indicate the place and time of the meeting. The eight-day period may be reduced in case of urgency.

### Art. 5. The agenda shall be set by the bureau

At the opening of the meeting, the agenda as well as the minutes of the previous meeting shall be approved.
The agenda may be modified or supplemented.

## CHAPTER III. — The Council

### Art. 6

§ 1. The Council may validly issue an opinion or make a report only on condition that at least half of the members are present or represented.
Absent members may be represented by proxy. Each member present may hold only one proxy.
Decisions shall be taken by simple majority. In case of a tie, the President's vote shall be decisive.
If the quorum of attendance is not reached, the members shall be convened again, at the latest within the fortnight that follows. For the item re-entered on the agenda, a simple majority of the members present shall be required.
If a member is absent from the Council three times in succession, he shall be questioned by the President who may, depending on the explanations given by the member concerned, propose his resignation to the Minister.

### § 2

The result of the vote shall be attached to the opinion; upon request, the minority may attach to the majority opinion a note specifying its point of view.

### Art. 7

Only items entered on the agenda attached to the convocation may be validly submitted to a vote.
However, in case of extreme urgency duly justified, an item may be added to the agenda during the session provided that the members present have agreed to examine it.

### Art. 8

No member of the Council directly interested in a request for an opinion submitted to the Council - because he is a member of the organizing authority or the staff of the requesting institution - may take part in the debate and vote relating to the opinion concerning that request.
In the event that the reality of a direct interest on the part of one or more member(s) is contested, the Council shall deliberate in the presence of that/those member(s).
In case of a tie, the decision shall rest with the President.

### Art. 9

If the Council deems it necessary, it may invite the Mental Health Service whose file is entered on the agenda in order to obtain additional or supplementary information.

### Art. 10. The Council may call upon experts whom it designates

## CHAPTER IV. — The bureau

### Art. 11

§ 1. A bureau responsible for the organization and coordination of the work shall be established within the Regional Council of Mental Health Services, hereinafter referred to as the Council.
The bureau shall prepare the meetings of the Council and shall ensure the transmission of proposals or opinions adopted by the Council.
The bureau may, in addition to requests for opinions from the Minister, formulate requests for opinions itself and submit them to the Council.

### § 2

The bureau shall consist of the President and the two vice-presidents designated by the Walloon Government by decree of 24 October 2002.
The vice-presidents shall, in turn, chair the sessions in case of impediment of the president.

### Art. 12

The two representatives of the Government shall be invited to the meetings of the bureau.

### Art. 13

The bureau may call all or part of the members of the Council for its work.

## CHAPTER V. — Working groups

### Art. 14

To fulfill its mission, the Council may establish working groups either permanent or temporary on the occasion of a specific problem or in the context of a request for an opinion formulated by the Government or the Minister.
In the context of these working groups, it may call upon experts.

### Art. 15. The members of the Council may be part of the working groups

Members - external to the Council - invited to a specific working group shall be so invited on the basis of their competence and in a personal capacity, on the proposal of the president of the working group and with the agreement of the bureau.
The bureau, after having taken the opinion of the president of the working group, may be led to limit the number of members of the working groups according to the matters studied.
The designation of experts shall be the subject of a decision of the Council.

### Art. 16

With the exception of the minutes, the documents of the working groups shall be sent only to the members of those groups.
The other members of the Council may consult the documents at the Secretariat upon request.
The member who is absent from the meetings of a working group three times consecutively shall be considered to have resigned.

### Art. 17

The Secretariat of the Council shall practically organize the activities of the working groups, to the extent of the means allocated to it by the Government.

### Art. 18

The presidents of the permanent working groups shall be designated by the Council on the proposal of the bureau for a period of three years renewable once.
They shall regularly report to the Council on the progress of the work, for approval.
The President of the Council may attend by right all the meetings of the various working groups and possibly chair them.

### Art. 19

A quarterly report from each working group is transmitted to the bureau and to the Council.
Seen to be annexed to the decree of the Walloon Government of 9 January 2003 approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services.
Namur, 9 January 2003.
The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

N. 2003 — 584

TRANSLATION
MINISTRY OF THE WALLOON REGION

[2003/200089]

9 JANUARY 2003. — Decree of the Walloon Government approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services
The Walloon Government,
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Region of 4 April 1996 concerning the recognition and subsidisation of mental health services, in particular Article 24;

Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 7 November 1996 implementing the decree of 4 April 1996 concerning the recognition and subsidisation of mental health services, in particular Section 12;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 regulating its operation;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 establishing the distribution of ministerial powers and regulating the signing of its acts;
Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 24 October 2002 appointing the members of the Regional Council of mental health services;
Having regard to the rules of procedure drawn up by the Regional Council of mental health services during its session of 23 December 2002;
Considering that the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services must be approved by the Walloon Government;
On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health, After deliberation,
Decrees:

### Article 1

This decree regulates, in accordance with Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in Article 128, § 1, of the Constitution.

### Art. 2

The rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services, which accompanies this decree, is approved by the Walloon Government.

### Art. 3

The decree of 20 February 1997 approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services is repealed.

### Art. 4

This decree enters into force on the day it is adopted by the Government.

### Art. 5

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health is responsible for the implementation of this decree. Namur, 9 January 2003.
The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

Annex
REGIONAL COUNCIL OF MENTAL HEALTH SERVICES
Rules of procedure

## CHAPTER I. — General provisions

### Article 1

Except where otherwise provided, the opinion requested by the Minister from the Council shall be given within two months unless the Government has established another time limit.
Upon reasoned request, the Minister may request urgent advice. In that case, he shall establish a reasonable time limit.

### Art. 2

The Council shall determine the calendar of ordinary meetings at the beginning of each calendar year.

## CHAPTER II. — Convocations

### Art. 3

The chairman shall convene the members of the Council, as well as the persons who attend the meetings in an advisory capacity.

### Art. 4

The convocation shall be sent to the members at least eight days before the meeting and shall systematically include a proxy form.
The minutes of the previous meeting shall be attached to the convocation if they have not been sent earlier. The convocation shall state the agenda of the meeting. The convocations shall be sent to the address communicated by the members. They shall state the place and time of the meeting. In urgent cases, the period of
eight days may be shortened.

### Art. 5. The agenda shall be determined by the bureau

The agenda and the report of the previous meeting shall be approved at the opening of the meeting. The agenda may be amended or supplemented.

## CHAPTER III. — The Council

### Art. 6

§ 1. The opinion or report of the Council shall only be validly issued if at least half of the members are present or represented.
Absent members may be represented by proxy. Each member present may only hold one proxy.
Decisions shall be taken by simple majority. In the event of a tie, the chairman's vote shall be decisive.
If the quorum of attendance is not reached, the members shall be convened again within the following fourteen days at the latest. An item placed on the agenda again shall require a simple majority of the members present.
A member who does not participate in the Council three times in succession shall be questioned by the chairman, who may propose his resignation to the Minister on the basis of the explanation given.

### § 2

The result of the vote shall be attached to the opinion; the minority may, upon request, attach to the majority opinion a note stating its position.

### Art. 7

Only the agenda items attached to the convocation may be validly submitted to the vote.

In the event of duly justified urgent necessity, an item may nevertheless be added to the agenda during the session if the members present are willing to examine it.

### Art. 8

A member of the Council who has a direct interest in a request for an opinion submitted to the Council - in particular as a member of the organising authority or of the staff of the requesting institution - may not participate in the debate or in the vote concerning the opinion relating to that request.
If it is disputed that one or more members have a direct interest, the Council shall deliberate thereon in the presence of the member(s) concerned.
In the event of a tie, the chairman shall decide.

### Art. 9

If the Council deems it necessary, it may invite the mental health service whose file is on the agenda in order to obtain additional or supplementary information.

### Art. 10. The Council may call upon experts whom it appoints

## CHAPTER IV.— The bureau

### Art. 11

§ 1. Within the Regional Council of mental health services, hereinafter referred to as "the Council", a bureau shall be established which shall be responsible for the organisation and coordination of the work.
The bureau shall prepare the meetings of the Council and shall ensure the communication of the proposals or opinions which it approves.
In addition to the requests for opinions from the Minister, the bureau may itself formulate requests for opinions and submit them to the Council.

### § 2

The bureau shall be composed of the chairman and two vice-chairmen appointed by decree of the Walloon Government of 24 October 2002.
In the event of the chairman being prevented, the chairmanship of the sessions shall be assumed alternately by the vice-chairmen.

### Art. 12

The two representatives of the Government shall be invited to the meetings of the bureau.

### Art. 13

The bureau may convene some or all of the members of the Council to participate in its work.

## CHAPTER V. — Working groups

### Art. 14

In order to fulfil its task, the Council may establish either permanent or temporary working groups for a specific problem or in the context of a request for an opinion formulated by the Government or by the Minister.
In the context of those working groups, it may call upon experts.

### Art. 15. The members of the Council may be part of the working groups

The members of a working group who do not belong to the Council are personally invited on the proposal of the chair of the working group and with the approval of the bureau on the basis of their competence.
Depending on the matters dealt with, the bureau may, after advice from the chair, be led to limit the number of members of the working groups.
The experts are appointed by decision of the Council.

### Art. 16

The documents of the working groups are only sent to the members of those working groups, with the exception of the minutes.
The other members of the Council who request them may consult the documents at the secretariat.
The member who misses three consecutive meetings of a working group is deemed to have resigned.

### Art. 17

The secretariat of the Council coordinates the activities of the working groups according to the resources allocated to it by the Government.

### Art. 18

The chairs of the standing working groups are appointed by the Council on the proposal of the bureau for a term of three years. That term may be extended once.
They regularly report to the Council on the state of the work. The Council must approve the report.
The chair of the Council may by right attend and possibly chair all meetings of the various working groups.

### Art. 19

Each working group submits a quarterly report to the bureau and to the Council.
Seen to be appended to the decree of the Walloon Government of 9 January 2003 approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of Mental Health Services.
Namur, 9 January 2003.

The Minister-President,
J.-Cl. VAN CAUWENBERGHE
The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

23 DECEMBER 2002. — Ministerial decree approving the care insurance manual
The Flemish Minister of Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,
Having regard to the decree of 30 March 1999 on the organisation of care insurance, amended by the decrees of 22 December 1999, 8 December 2000, 18 May 2001 and 20 December 2002;
Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 17 July 2000 concerning the organisation, management, operation and recognition of care funds and concerning the control of care funds, as amended by the decree of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001;
Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 on recognition, registration and authorisation, and on affiliation, application and coverage within the framework of care insurance, as amended by the decrees of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001, 25 October 2002 and 13 December 2002;
Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 3 July 2002 determining the powers of the members of the Flemish Government;
Having regard to the opinion of the Finance Inspectorate, given on 6 December 2002;
Having regard to the laws on the Council of State, coordinated on 12 January 1973, in particular article 3, § 1, replaced by the law of 4 July 1989 and amended by the law of 4 August 1996;
Having regard to the urgent necessity;
Considering that the Flemish care insurance starts according to the adapted procedures on 1 January 2003 so that the provisions of the decrees of the Flemish Government in this matter must be implemented without delay and the care funds concerned and the citizens must be provided with legal certainty regarding the new regulation within the framework of care insurance,
Decrees:


This excerpt sits under: BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE > CHAPTER V. — Working groups

### Article 1

The health care insurance manual, which is attached as an annex to this decision, is approved.

### Art. 2

The ministerial decision of 1 October 2001 approving the health care insurance manual is repealed.

### Art. 3

This decision enters into force on 1 January 2003. Brussels, 23 December 2002.
The Flemish Minister of Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,

Note: whenever Brussels is mentioned in this manual, this should be understood to mean the bilingual area of Brussels-Capital, as referred to in the decree of 30 March 1999 on the organisation of health care insurance and the associated implementing decisions
PART I. — Further definition of the actors

## CHAPTER I. — Representative

The representative of the user is a natural person who acts in place of the user in all actions that the user must take in order to obtain coverage of costs for non-medical care under the health care insurance.
Section I. — Representative without power of attorney
The following persons may act as representative of a user without power of attorney:
•	the husband, wife or a relative in the first or second degree ((grand)parent, (grand)child, brother or sister) of the user;
the representative must state on the application form in what capacity he or she is in relation to the user (wife, sister, etc.);
•	An adult member of the user's household. In this case, a certificate of household composition of the persons concerned is sufficient as proof. This certificate is issued by the municipality;

•	the legal representative of the user: being 1 of the following persons:
➢	one of the parents or, where applicable, the guardian for a non-emancipated minor. The same applies to the extended minor who is equated with a minor under fifteen years of age in terms of his person and his property;
➢	the guardian, being where applicable the husband or wife for a person declared incapable;
➢	a provisional administrator may be provided for a suspect who is interned and who is neither declared incapable nor under guardianship, if his interests so require. The provisional administrator collects, among other things, the claims, pays the debts and represents the interned person in court as defendant and as plaintiff;
➢	a provisional administrator may be appointed by the justice of the peace to an adult who is wholly or partially, albeit temporarily due to his state of health, unable to manage his property. Unless the contrary appears from the judicial decision appointing the provisional administrator, he represents the protected person in all legal acts and proceedings concerning his property.
Section II. — Representative with power of attorney
Persons who do not meet one of the descriptions mentioned in section I may act as representative of the user if they have a document stating that the user or his legal representative grants him power of attorney. This can be anyone, it is not limited to the legal concept of 'representative'.
This document must be dated and signed by the user or his legal representative.
The declaration on the document may be as follows:
'name of user' hereby grants power of attorney to 'name of representative' to carry out in the name and on behalf of 'name of user' all formalities necessary for him/her to be able to assert his/her right to coverage under the health care insurance.
PART II. — Description of the processes

## CHAPTER I. — Amount of contributions

Period: 1 October 2001 to 31 December 2002: 10 euros
year 2003: 25 euros
10 euros for persons who on 1 January 2002 are entitled to an increased reimbursement from the health care insurance

## CHAPTER II. — Payment of the annual contribution due by members

Under this chapter, payment is dealt with once one is a member.
The care fund is obliged to collect the annual membership contributions of its members. The care fund determines the manner in which it collects the contributions from its members.
The care fund or the founding body may issue the invitation to pay the contribution for the care insurance together with the invitation to pay for other products (of the care fund or of its founding bodies) via one document.
However, the member is provided with a separate notification clearly stating what amount is requested from the member for the Flemish care insurance. It is also clearly stated for which period the amount is requested.
Both documents can of course be sent in the same mailing. The notification must contain at least the following information:
•	the amount requested in the context of the care insurance and the period for which this amount is requested.
•	the measure of suspension of coverage for four months at the moment that the member is entitled to coverage, for each year that a member has not paid or has not fully paid his contribution.
•	if a member does not consider himself able to pay the annual membership contribution, then he can ask the OCMW whether it is willing to pay the membership contribution to the care fund in place of the member. However, the OCMW is not obliged to grant the request of this member
Existing direct debit mandates may only be used to have the contribution paid by a member if this person (or his head of household) has given prior consent for this.
The member pays his due annual membership contribution in the current year to his care fund. When a person does not pay, then the care fund may send a reminder letter to this person.
The care funds may set a deadline for payment of the contribution. This deadline may not fall before 1 January of the year to which the contribution relates. If this deadline falls in the current year, no sanctions may be attached to it.
A member for whom an application for coverage in the context of the care insurance is submitted pays his membership contribution for that year before submitting the application. By way of derogation, the person for whom an application is submitted in the first quarter of the year pays his contribution before 1 April of that year.
A member who already has entitlement to coverage pays his membership contribution in the first quarter of the year.
In case of transfer, a member who has changed care fund pays his first contribution to his new care fund in the first quarter of the year.
Third parties (employer, OCMW) may pay the contributions of persons who are members of a care fund. The care fund is in possession of a list of persons for whom the third party pays the contribution. The list that the third party provides to the care fund contains per member the data that must be kept in the register. This procedure is not mandatory for contributions paid jointly by a head of household.

## CHAPTER III

Membership with a care fund Section I. — Membership with a care fund of choice
A person is only affiliated with a care fund when the membership fee has been paid for the first time.
The founding bodies of the care funds may send a mailing to their members and/or insured persons with the notification that they can join a care fund. This mailing may be done in the name of the founding body. On the one hand there is the notification in connection with the care insurance and on the other hand the invitation to pay the contribution by which one joins. The mailing may be done together with the promotion of other products.
The notification and the invitation to payment (the transfer) must be distributed on separate documents.
The notification must contain at least the following information:
•	the amount requested in the framework of the care insurance and the period for which this amount is requested;
•	everyone who lives in Flanders and is older than 25 years is obliged to join one of the eight recognised care funds. This is done by paying the membership fee;
•	the measure of suspension of coverage for four months at the time when the member is entitled to coverage, for each year that a member has not paid or has not fully paid his contribution;
•	if a member does not consider himself able to pay the annual membership fee, he may ask the OCMW whether it will pay the membership fee to the care fund in place of the member. However, the OCMW is not obliged to grant the request of this member.
The invitation to payment mentions only the amount to be paid for the care insurance. It is emphasised that the first contribution, by which one joins, must be paid by deposit to an account number in the name of the care fund.
The care funds must respect the freedom of choice of the user. The notification and the invitation to payment may therefore not contain indications that suggest that membership with the care fund in question is mandatory. It must always be clear that membership with one of the eight recognised care funds is mandatory.
The care funds may set 1 April at the earliest as the deadline for paying the contribution, stating the reason that otherwise one will be affiliated ex officio with the Flemish care fund from 1 July. In no case may the suspension of four months be given as a sanction for this reason.
Subject to the consent of the persons concerned, third parties may affiliate persons with a care fund. The care funds that affiliate persons through third parties have a declaration from the person concerned showing that he voluntarily chooses to join the care fund.
If a third party wishes to realise the affiliation of other persons, the third party draws up a list of the persons who wish to be affiliated with the care fund through the third party concerned. The care fund will then have in its possession a list of affiliated persons for whom payment is made as a group. This procedure is not mandatory for joint affiliations paid by the head of household.
Only an employer or an OCMW can act as a third party. A head of household can affiliate family members.
The list that the third party provides to the care fund contains per member the data that must be kept in the register (national register number, name, address, date of birth, gender, nationality).
The third parties provide a list to the care fund of the persons for whom payment is made, together with the payment of the contributions.
The care fund must keep a register of the persons affiliated with it. It registers at least the following data of the members:
◆	national register number
◆	name
◆	address
◆	date of birth
◆	gender
◆	nationality
◆	date of payment of first membership fee
The care fund determines itself how this register will take shape. The Care Fund only requires that these data be kept on site per individual member in a comprehensible form so that they can be checked by an inspector.
When a person does not join, a reminder letter may be sent.
Section II. — Ex officio affiliation
A first payment of the contribution is not a condition for ex officio affiliation with the Flemish care fund.
The Flemish care fund must affiliate ex officio all persons who do not join a recognised care fund within the set deadlines:
In the ex officio affiliation in 2003, the person receives an invitation to pay the contribution for the years 2002 and 2003. If the person joins the Flemish care fund in the same year (the year 2003) and pays the contribution for the years 2002 and 2003, no suspension is imposed on the person.
The Flemish care fund will affiliate ex officio the person who has not joined a care fund within the set deadline and will inform him of this immediately and in writing. This letter will contain, in addition to an invitation to pay the membership fee to the Flemish care fund, the notification that the person concerned can still join a recognised care fund of his choice. If the person still joins another care fund, the Flemish Care Fund will inform the Flemish care fund of this. In that case, the Flemish care fund cancels the ex officio affiliation.
If the person concerned joins in the years following the one in which he was first obliged, the care fund with which the person still joins (with the exception of the Flemish care fund in the year 2003 if payment is made for 2002 and 2003) must impose a suspension on him for non-payment of the annual contributions. The care fund registers the suspension of this person.

The Flemish Care Fund will inform the care fund if it appears that the person was already affiliated with another care fund.
If the member has already paid a membership fee to the Flemish care fund once, the member can only change care fund via the procedure described in chapter V.
Section III. — Multiple affiliations with different care funds in the same period
A person cannot be affiliated with different care funds. The Flemish Care Fund will check this by requesting the national register numbers of the affiliated members from the different care funds.
As a principle, the person –who has paid his contribution– is affiliated with the care fund that was the first to notify the Flemish Care Fund within the deadlines provided for this purpose that the person is affiliated with it. If the care funds notify at the same time that a person is affiliated with them, the person is affiliated with the care fund that first received the membership fee from the member.
The other care funds inform the person in writing that his affiliation with them lapses because he was already affiliated with a care fund.
If the person has obtained multiple coverages from different care funds in the same period, the care funds –with the exception of the care fund with which the person is effectively affiliated– reclaim the amounts from this person.
Section IV. — Termination of affiliation An affiliation can only be terminated for the following reasons:
•	the member leaves Flanders or Brussels
•	the member dies
•	the member residing in Brussels terminates his affiliation on a voluntary basis
The affiliation is terminated on 31 December of the year in which the reason for termination of the affiliation occurred.
The member or his representative notifies the care fund by letter that the affiliation is terminated. In case of death, the representative of the user encloses a copy of the death certificate.
The member must in principle still pay the full membership fee for the year in which the reason for termination of the affiliation took place. By way of derogation, in the event of death of the member, the membership fees no longer have to be paid if the member would still owe them.
If a member leaves Flanders or Brussels, the care fund provides the following data of the user to the Flemish Care Fund within six months after terminating the user's affiliation:
•	the data of the member that are kept in the register
