•	an overview of the payments of all membership fees of the member
•	where applicable, an overview of the coverages of the last year

## CHAPTER IV. — Sanctions for non-membership and non-payment

When a person does not pay their membership contribution, the following sanctions apply:
* the suspension of coverage with loss of rights
The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months, for each year that they have not paid, although they were obliged to do so in that year.
One is obliged to pay for a year in the following cases:
➢	if one is affiliated with a care fund on the first of January of the year in question;
➢	if one is affiliated ex officio with an effective date in the year in question.
This suspension takes effect at the moment that the affiliate as user is entitled to coverage within the framework of care insurance (in other words, the first day of the third month following the submission of an application). The user also loses their rights to coverage for those suspended periods.
There is a suspension and a waiting period. If a user incurred both a suspension and a waiting period, then these periods are cumulated.
1.	suspension
The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months, for each year that they have not paid, although they were affiliated with a care fund. The non-payment of the contribution for the period from 1 October 2001 up to and including 31 December 2002 leads to a suspension of four months.
2.	waiting period
The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months, for each year that they lived in Brussels and did not affiliate with a care fund, although they could have affiliated. Not being affiliated during the period from 1 October 2001 up to and including 31 December 2002 leads to a waiting period of four months.
The care fund registers the suspension and/or waiting period when the contribution for a certain year has not been paid.
•	When an affiliate, who submits an application for coverage within the framework of care insurance, does not pay their membership contribution in the first quarter of the year, then the amount of the contribution is deducted from the amount of the coverage from the month of April in the month that the execution of the coverage takes effect.
•	When an affiliate, who already has a right to coverage, does not pay their membership contribution in the first quarter of the year, then the amount of the contribution is deducted from the amount of the coverage in the month of April.

## CHAPTER V. — Change of care fund

A change of care fund is legally valid if:
•	the affiliate has paid their membership contribution to the new care fund in the first quarter of the year that they, after their application for transfer, can be affiliated with the new care fund
•	the new care fund has received an overview of all payments of the membership contributions and an overview of the coverages of the last year from the old care fund.
For this purpose the following procedure is followed:
•	the affiliate notifies before 5 December of the calendar year preceding that in which they will be affiliated with their new care fund, to this new care fund that they wish to affiliate with it.
•	the new care fund requests at the latest before 15 December of the calendar year preceding that in which the person will be affiliated with the new care fund, an overview of all payments of the membership contributions and an overview of the coverages, carried out during the last year.
•	the old care fund provides before 15 January of the calendar year in which the person concerned will affiliate with the new care fund, the following data:
➢	the data of the user that must be included in the register (national register number, name, address, date of birth, gender, nationality);
➢	an overview of the payments of the membership contributions copy of the last decision of the old care fund with regard to coverages of the affiliate (only if the affiliate is entitled to coverages);
➢	the overview of the coverages, carried out during the last year
•	the old care fund terminates the membership of the affiliate concerned on 31 December of the year in which the new care fund requested the overviews within the set deadline
•	the candidate affiliate is obliged to pay their annual membership contribution to the new care fund in the first quarter of the year in which they can affiliate with their new care fund.
•	the affiliation takes effect retroactively on 1 January of the year in which the affiliate paid their contribution to the new care fund for the first time.
If the user has not paid their due membership contribution to the new care fund before 1 April, then the affiliation with the new care fund lapses. The user is then affiliated again with the old care fund. The new care fund informs the old care fund within 40 days that the transfer lapses and that the person is affiliated again with the old care fund.
Coverages are paid out by that care fund with which the user was affiliated in the month to which the coverage relates. This means that no credit transfer from the old to the new care fund will take place.
Recoveries from the user are also carried out by that care fund with which the user was affiliated at the moment that the coverages, to which the recovery relates, were wrongfully executed.

## CHAPTER VI

Application for coverage and decision on the formal conditions Section I. — Table

type of care	submit application	coverage
informal and home care	from 1 January 2003	from 1 April 2003*
residential care (C-score and PVT**)	from 1 July 2002	from 1 October 2002
residential care (B-score**)	from 1 January 2003	from 1 April 2003
* transitional provision: persons with a positive decision for informal care and/or home care and products in 2002 that is also valid for 2003, have a positive decision for informal and home care from 1 January 2003.
** the score on the evaluation scale in support of the application for reimbursement in a care institution
Section II. — Users who are older than 25 years
Subsection I. — General
The user or his representative submits an application for coverage to his care fund via the application form in the context of the Flemish care insurance.
A person cannot submit an application for coverage if he is not affiliated with the care fund by paying at least one contribution.
The user or his representative completes the application form in the context of the Flemish care insurance, signs it and sends it to his care fund.
The user must meet the following formal conditions at the time of his application for coverage:
Subsection II. — Formal conditions for coverage for users who reside in Flanders
1.	have resided continuously in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years; Belgians and nationals of another Member State of the European Union are not subject to this condition if they pay 5 times the amount of the annual contribution at the time of the application for coverage retrospectively
2.	have paid the contribution for the current year before 1 April or before submitting the application
3.	have not submitted an application for coverage to another care fund in the current year
4.	with regard to applications for coverage for residential care, actually reside in a recognised or equivalently recognised residential facility

Subsection III. — Formal conditions for coverage for users who reside in Brussels
1.	The resident of Brussels who joins late must be continuously affiliated with a recognised care fund for a period determined by the government, prior to the date on which he submits his application for coverage.
Persons join late when they:
•	either do not join within 6 months in the calendar year in which they turn 26 years old
•	or do not join within 6 months after they come to reside in Brussels in a calendar year in which they turn at least 26 years old.
The period of continuous affiliation extends as the care insurance exists longer. This is clarified in the table below:

year in which a resident of Brussels joins late	the period determined by the government that a resident of Brussels must be continuously affiliated, prior to the time of the application for coverage
2002	0 years
2003	1 year
2004	3 years
2005	5 years
2006 and subsequent years	10 years
2.	have resided continuously in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years. Belgians and nationals of another Member State of the European Union are not subject to this condition if they pay 5 times the amount of the annual contribution at the time of the application for coverage retrospectively.
3.	have paid the contribution for the current year before 1 April or before submitting the application;
4.	have not submitted an application for coverage to another care fund in the current year
5.	with regard to applications for coverage for residential care, actually reside in a recognised or equivalently recognised residential facility
The care fund verifies whether the user meets the formal conditions and requests, if necessary, further evidence than the declaration of the user or his representative on the application form in the context of the care insurance.
If the user does not meet the formal conditions, the care fund communicates that decision in writing to the user or his representative with clarification of the reason.
If the user meets the formal conditions, it is verified whether a completed indication or a certificate is available.
Subsection IV. — Choice of type of care
There are two possibilities:
•	informal and home care;
•	residential care.
Only persons who reside in a home environment can submit an application for informal and home care. The home environment is the place where the user actually lives or resides, excluding collective forms of housing where persons reside and are accommodated on a permanent basis. A service flat is considered a home environment.
Only persons who reside in a recognised or equivalently recognised residential facility can submit an application for residential care.
Section III. — Users (under 25 years) who cannot yet join a care fund by paying the contribution
Users who do not yet have to pay a contribution due to their age can nevertheless be eligible for coverage in the context of the care insurance provided they submit an application form in the context of the Flemish care insurance.
These users must also meet the formal conditions for coverage. Exception:
•	users who are younger than 25 years and who reside in Brussels cannot join late. They therefore do not have to meet the condition that they are continuously affiliated with a recognised care fund for a period prior to the date on which they submit their application for coverage.
•	users who are younger than 5 years and who have resided in Flanders or Brussels since their birth do not have to meet the condition of having resided continuously in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years.

## CHAPTER VII

Formal condition for coverage if the user has not lived continuously in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years
If the user does not meet the formal condition that at the time of the application for coverage he has lived continuously in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years, he may still meet the formal conditions.
For this purpose, the user or his representative must submit an application for regularisation for the user on condition that the user at the time of his application for regularisation:
•	is affiliated with the care fund where he submits his application for regularisation
•	has paid the membership fees due
The user or his representative can request his regularisation on the application form in the context of care insurance. With this application he attaches proof of his nationality, for example a copy of his identity card.

The care fund decides on the regularisation of the user. The care fund regularises the user if the user has the nationality of a member state of the European Union. This concerns the Benelux, Germany, France, Spain, Portugal, Italy, Greece, Denmark, Great Britain, Ireland, Finland, Austria and Sweden.
In the event of a positive decision by the care fund, the care fund asks the user to pay an additional membership fee. This amounts to a maximum of five times the contribution for the year in which the member submits his application for coverage. The five years is reduced by the number of years that the member has paid contributions in the last 5 years before the application for coverage.
situations
Someone who submits an application for coverage in the year 2002 must pay a total of 50 euros if he has not lived in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years.
Someone who submits an application for coverage in the year 2003 pays a maximum of five times the contribution he must pay in that year. If the user has already paid his contribution for the years 2002 and 2003, then he must still pay for the remaining three years, which is therefore three times his contribution to the care fund for the year 2003.
Someone aged 27 who, for example, submits an application for coverage in the year 2003, must also pay a maximum of five times the contribution for that year for regularisation. If the user has already paid his contribution for the years 2002 and 2003, then he must still pay for the remaining three years, which is therefore three times his contribution to the care fund for the year 2003.
Someone aged 23 who, for example, submits an application for coverage in the year 2003, does not need to pay any contribution for regularisation.

## CHAPTER VIII

Determination of seriously and long-term reduced self-care capacity in the application for coverage
With regard to the indication assessment, there are two possibilities:
Section I. — User or his representative relies on a certificate of an indication assessment that has already been carried out:
The following scores are eligible for home and community care:
◆	at least a score of 35 on the BEL profile scale of home care;
◆	at least a score of B on the Katz scale in home nursing;
◆	at least a score of 15 on the medico-social scale used for the evaluation of the degree of self-sufficiency with a view to the examination of the right to integration allowance, assistance for the elderly and assistance from third parties
◆	supplementary child benefit on the basis of at least 66% disability and at least 7 points with regard to the degree of self-sufficiency on behalf of the child
◆	at least a score of C on the evaluation scale in support of the application for allowance in a care institution
The following scores are eligible for residential care:
◆	at least a score of B on the evaluation scale in support of the application for allowance in a care institution
◆	a certificate on the basis of proof of admission of the user to a psychiatric care home
◆	persons who change from home and community care to residential care on or after 1 April 2003 are considered to be suffering from a seriously and long-term reduced self-care capacity and do not need to demonstrate further scores
The user requests the certificate on the basis of the score on the evaluation scale in support of the application for allowance in a care institution from his insurance institution (health insurance fund, Medical Care Fund of the NMBS, Auxiliary Fund for Health and Disability Insurance). He requests the certificate on the basis of proof of admission of the user to a psychiatric care home from his insurance institution or from the psychiatric care home.
He requests the certificates on the basis of the score on the BEL profile scale of home care from the home care service to which he appeals.
He can request a certificate on the basis of supplementary child benefit (66% disability and 7 points with regard to degree of self-sufficiency) from the Ministry of Social Affairs or the service that pays the child benefit for the person concerned.
He can request the certificates on the basis of the score on the medico-social scale used for the evaluation of the degree of self-sufficiency with a view to the examination of the right to integration allowance, assistance for the elderly and assistance from third parties from his insurance institution, if the insurance institution has the score, or at the following address:
Ministry of Social Affairs, Public Health and Environment:
Administration of Social Integration Service for allowances to disabled persons Zwarte Lievevrouwstraat 3C
1000 Brussels
The certificates must contain at least the following information:
name, address, date of birth and national register number of the user on whom the indication assessment was carried out
•	name of the institution that issued the certificate
•	name of the scale on which the certificate is based
•	the score (or category) on this scale
•	the date of administration of this scale or the date of commencement of recognition in the original system
•	the duration of validity of this indication assessment
By way of exception, a copy of the decision on integration allowance or allowance for assistance to the elderly also serves as a certificate.

The certificate on the basis of proof of admission to a psychiatric care home must contain at least the following information:
•	name, address, date of birth and national register number of the user
•	name of the institution that issued the certificate
•	proof of admission to a psychiatric care home
•	the period of validity
•	date of admission to a psychiatric care home
The user or his representative provides the certificate to the care fund.
On the day of the application for coverage within the framework of care insurance, the period of validity of the above-mentioned indication assessment or of the decision in the original system pursuant to the above-mentioned indication assessment may not have expired.

Section II. — User or his representative does not have a certificate
The user or his representative who does not have a certificate must have a new indication assessment carried out by an authorised indication assessor. At the request of the user or his representative, the care fund makes available to the user or his representative a list of all authorised indication assessors from the region.
If the user so wishes, the indication assessment takes place at the location where the user resides.
The user is assessed by the authorised indication assessor using the BEL profile scale of care insurance. The indication assessor completes the CHAPTER "Determination of reduced self-care capacity". The BEL profile of the user is completed using the BEL manual. It is signed by the person carrying out the indication assessment.
The user receives the indication assessment and provides the indication assessment to the care fund.
The user must score at least 35 points on the BEL profile scale to be considered as suffering from a seriously and long-term reduced self-care capacity for coverage for home and community care.
EXCEPTIONS
The determination for users who are between five and eighteen years old will be carried out using an adapted BEL scale (BEL-children). The user must score at least 10 points on the physical A.D.L. block of the BEL profile scale to be considered as suffering from a seriously and long-term reduced self-care capacity.
Users under five years of age cannot have an indication assessment carried out on them using the BEL profile scale of care insurance.

## CHAPTER IX. — Compensation for carrying out the needs assessment

The Flemish Care Fund pays the care fund 75 euros per needs assessment carried out on condition that
•	it concerns a needs assessment that was carried out within the framework of the care insurance by an authorised needs assessor at the request of the user or his representative (this therefore concerns the needs assessments that serve as a second option alongside the certificates)
•	the care fund compensates the authorised needs assessor for the needs assessment
•	it does not concern a needs assessment that was carried out by a recognised family care service or a PCSW that has a recognised family care service, whereby family care was provided to the indicated user within six months after the needs assessment was carried out by this facility.
Of each needs assessment of a user, that was carried out within the framework of the care insurance, the authorised needs assessors provide a copy of the BEL-photo (front and back) to the care fund to which the user is affiliated.
The family care service or the PCSW that has a recognised family care service, communicates where applicable to the care fund that it has provided family care to the user within six months after carrying out the needs assessment. This communication takes place within thirty days after the facts.
The care funds transfer 75 euros per needs assessment carried out that meets the above-mentioned conditions, to the authorised needs assessors.
Monthly, the care funds submit an application to the Flemish Care Fund. This application contains the number of needs assessments carried out that meet the above-mentioned conditions. The Care Fund compensates the care funds monthly. The Care Fund may draw up further instructions for this purpose.
If a needs assessment, for which the care fund has already received compensation, no longer qualifies for compensation, then the care fund mentions this in the next application. It submits a credit note for this purpose to the Flemish Care Fund. Where applicable, the compensation paid out for this is deducted from the compensation to be paid in the following month.

The application of the care funds must meet the following formal requirements:
•	the application is drawn up in the form of a collective statement, on which all invoices are mentioned that were submitted during the past month by the authorised needs assessors;
•	per invoice the following data are included on the collective statement:
➢	the name and the GKB number of the authorised needs assessor;
➢	the invoice date;
➢	the number of invoiced needs assessments per needs assessor;
➢	the invoiced amount per needs assessor;
➢	where applicable: the number of reclaimed needs assessments per needs assessor;
➢	where applicable: the reclaimed amount per needs assessor;
➢	where applicable: the difference between the invoiced and the reclaimed amount per needs assessor;
•	the collective statement mentions the total amount for which reimbursement is requested. This total amount is the difference between the total of all invoiced amounts and of all reclaimed amounts or credit notes
•	a copy of all invoices and any credit notes to which it relates is attached to the collective statement.
•	each care fund may only submit one collective statement per month. This collective statement may possibly be divided into sub-statements per department, but is always transmitted in one shipment to the Flemish Care Fund. Where applicable, the attached copies of invoices and credit notes are grouped per sub-statement.
•	the collective statement must be submitted to the Flemish Care Fund no later than the 15th day of the month following the month to which it relates.
The Flemish Care Fund has the right to transfer the collective statement that has not been submitted on time, to the next payment date.


This excerpt sits under: MONITEUR BELGE

## CHAPTER X. — BEL health insurance manual

BEL-PHOTO-USER
I.	 Who what and how is scored?
1)	WHO
We always score the user, for whom the coverage under the health insurance was requested.
2)	WHAT AND HOW?
We assess the care needs of the user by scoring the person in the broad sense of the word. We make a photo of the user via 27 items. These 27 items are categorized in blocks that each refer to a specific perspective.
We distinguish 4 blocks.
BLOCK A: HOUSEHOLD A.D.L.
This is the first facet of our photo. This block refers to household assistance. In the gradation of loss of functions in the user, those of block A will be the first to fail and/or where the care need becomes clear.
In block A the care need can be at different levels:
•	physically unable
•	unwilling to do, psychologically unable
•	not learned.
BLOCK B: PHYSICAL A.D.L.
This facet of the photo refers to physical functions that fail. This is the block that is analogous to home nursing, where the gradation of loss of physical functions is examined. This part of the photo provides information about the care needs of the user on a physical bodily level.
Here links are made to needs in terms of body care, hygienic and nursing care. In block B the care need is interpreted in the strict sense; namely physically unable
Block A and B form 1 kind of dimension, 1 fairly coherent whole, refer to material tasks. Both blocks follow the line of thought: a loss of functions.
BLOCK C: SOCIAL A.D.L.
In this block the care need for social support for certain facets is assessed.
This block makes links to concrete tasks in the field of general and family assistance. To know whether a user needs this type of assistance, we must have a photo of the care need for support. This can be at the social-relational level (support in processing loss, the social consequences for the user e.g.) as well as at the preventive (GVO) and at the supportive level (childcare, adherence to therapy and health rules, financial transactions,...). This block provides additional information about the extent to which a user is socially integrated, maintains themselves socially.
In block C the care need as in A can be at different levels: at physical, psychological and social level.
Block C is of a different nature than A and B. The care need that is expressed here will also lead to care tasks, but rather the less visible tasks, the tasks that are often named too little, or get their place too little. Working on self-reliance will often constitute an important part of the concrete assistance here. E.g. socially disadvantaged families, processing of social problems, rehabilitation after accident.
For elderly people the concrete situations will rather concern "social A.D.L." and for families it will often be about assistance with parenthood, when scoring one must keep this in mind.
We therefore translate 5 items in care needs towards elderly people, and 2 items, specifically towards families.

BLOCK D: MENTAL HEALTH.
This block refers to psychological dysfunction. This block provides additional and supplementary information to the other blocks.
In block D the care need is viewed at different levels, in addition to psychological dysfunctions, external behaviors and moods are also scored. This care need is translated into a degree of occurrence.
The facets of the photo that we want to obtain here are data and visible signs of the extent to which the user can maintain and function psychologically independently and autonomously.
II.	 The principles and rules of scoring in the B.EL. photo of the user
1)	METHOD OF SCORING: principles and motives. Four blocks therefore with 6 to 8 items each.
1.	Each item is scored separately but within the philosophy of the block to which it belongs.
2.	Each block (and item) is constructed equivalently. This means: there are always 4 gradation levels with the same value structure each time.
For each item the applicable level is checked. In case of doubt between 2 levels, one takes the highest (most care-needing) level.
3.	We use the SUM score everywhere, i.e. sum all scores in each block. The intermediate scores form a total score.
4.	We make a photo of the user. I.e. we assess the degree of care need of the user.
5.	For blocks A, B and C, this is expressed in:
LEVEL 0 NO CARE NEED LEVEL 1 SOME CARE NEED LEVEL 2 MORE CARE NEED LEVEL 3 COMPLETE CARE NEED
For block D this is expressed in a DEGREE OF OCCURRENCE.
In a further paragraph on the concrete application rules, these levels of care need are translated into a level at which the user still functions independently and needs help. Here no link is deliberately made to tasks. Tasks can get a derivation in a separate section, separate from the care need.
6.	When scoring an item, no account may be taken of the fact that the user already uses or will use a certain help (informal care, professional help,...).
7.	When scoring an item, there does not necessarily have to be a correlation with:
•	the cause of the need for help
•	the formulated help request of the help seeker
2)	DESIGN OF THE RULES: principles
The principles.
A short manual with level indications that are general but unambiguous
•	return consistently
•	be understood by everyone from the same language, but must be thought through or interpreted per scoring
•	contains no examples (except as illustration during training)
3)	THE CONCRETE RULES.
For the items
A Household A.D.L.
*	maintenance of the home
*	washing
*	ironing
*	shopping
*	preparing meals
*	organization of household work C Social A.D.L.
*	social loss
*	adherence to therapy and health rules
*	safety in and around the house
*	administration
*	financial transactions
*	hygienic care of children
*	childcare

For the items
B Physical A.D.L.
*	washing
*	dressing
*	moving
*	toilet visit
*	incontinence eating

ATTENTION: the no longer being able to function independently and being dependent on help is always and exclusively a consequence of physical discomfort, deterioration, dysfunctions
the item incontinence gets a translation analogous to block D. Translation: replace the

by each item
No care need: the user is completely independent for

Some care need: the user can independently manage

With the exception of certain sub-aspects OR: this costs the user effort or time
More care need: the user cannot independently manage

but can perform certain sub-aspects.
OR: the user needs partial help (persons/aids) Much care need: the user is completely dependent for

..
OR: the user is completely dependent on help (persons/aids) Translation
For the item INCONTINENCE' the translation becomes a degree of occurrence with the following gradation
1.	does not occur
2.	occurs occasionally
3.	occurs usually
4.	occurs constantly.
This is a linguistic gradation that everyone understands.
Can be through observation by the intake worker or via information from other care providers - own basic workers - referrers - informal carers.

D. For the items mental health
*	disorientation in time
*	disorientation in space
*	disorientation in persons
*	non-goal-directed behavior
*	disruptive behavior
*	initiative-less behavior
*	depressed mood
*	anxious mood
we use degree of occurrence with the gradation
1.	does not occur
2.	occurs occasionally
3.	occurs usually
4.	occurs constantly.
4)	THE DICTIONARY OF THE ITEMS.
Block A:
MAINTENANCE
= maintenance of the home in all its aspects ranging from tidying up to tasks requiring endurance and strength.
WASHING
= ranging from collecting and sorting the laundry to drying and/or taking down from the clothesline.
IRONING
= ranging from folding to putting away in the cupboard.
SHOPPING
= ranging from making the shopping list to putting away the purchased products.
PREPARING MEALS
= ranging from drawing up the menu to putting the prepared meal on the table.
ORGANIZATION OF HOUSEHOLD WORK
= managing a household, ranging from planning all household work that must be done regularly or sporadically to controlling its execution.
Block B
WASHING
= taking care of one's own body hygiene.
DRESSING
= being able to dress and undress oneself.
MOVING
= being able to move oneself, indoors or outdoors.
TOILET VISIT
= going to the toilet and making appropriate use of the toilet.
INCONTINENCE
= not feeling the urge to, not being able to control the discharge of urine or faeces
EATING
= one's own food intake: ranging from using the cutlery oneself to bringing the food to the mouth.
Block C:
SOCIAL LOSS
= absorbing, processing the social consequences of loss, regarding:
*	loss of trusted person
*	loss of familiar environment
*	loss of work, status
*	loss of physical or mental health
*	loss of ..
ADHERENCE TO THERAPY AND HEALTH RULES
complying with, following common sense rules or prescribed rules regarding:
*	medication use
*	therapy
*	rehabilitation
*	lifestyle rules
*	diets
*	prevention of complications

SAFETY IN AND AROUND THE HOUSE
= having insight into and eliminating safety risks in and around the house.
ADMINISTRATION
= ranging from following up correspondence, writing letters, filling in forms to visiting the post office and contacts with various institutions.
FINANCIAL TRANSACTIONS
= executing payments and financial orders
HYGIENIC CARE OF CHILDREN
= depending on the age of the children: ranging from washing, changing, dressing and feeding to guiding and supervising the hygienic care by the parent(s) or the child(ren) themselves.
CHILDCARE
= depending on the age of the children: ranging from watching the children to guiding and supervising the time use of the child(ren)
Block D:
DISORIENTATION IN TIME
= the extent to which the user no longer has correct insight into time. Insight into time includes:
•	correctly indicating the time when asked.
•	using the concept of time in an appropriate way (hour, morning, afternoon, day, night, week, month, year).
DISORIENTATION IN SPACE
= the extent to which the user no longer finds their way indoors or outdoors
E.g.: not finding the toilet, getting lost, believing to be in another house, wandering around,
DISORIENTATION IN PERSONS
= the extent to which the user no longer recognizes persons or no longer connects them with their correct name.
E.g.: regarding daughter as own mother, regarding grandchildren as own children, addressing nursing staff with wrong name,...
NON-GOAL-DIRECTED BEHAVIOR
= the extent to which the user displays behavior that is not meaningful, does not serve a purpose. This includes
•	compulsive actions (cleaning mania, fear of contamination, plucking at blanket,...)
•	restlessness: psycho-motor agitation (flight behavior, pacing behavior,...)
DISRUPTIVE BEHAVIOR
= the extent to which the user behaves disruptively in their social environment. This includes all interaction problems:
•	verbally disruptive (calling, shouting,)
•	indecent (undressing, spitting, urinating, masturbating, in public)
•	destructive violence towards persons, destructiveness.
INITIATIVE-LESS BEHAVIOR
= the extent to which the user shows lack of initiative and does not take action on their own. This includes:
•	no action without stimulus
•	difficulty starting activities
•	slow (no appropriate speed) in executing activities.'
DEPRESSED MOOD
= the extent to which the user displays a depressed mood (ranging from tendency to cry to depression) or expresses that they suffer from it.
ANXIOUS MOOD
= the extent to which the user appears anxious or expresses that they suffer from it (ranging from worrying about small matters to insomnia, to no longer functioning adequately out of fear).
III.	 The duration of the serious, reduced self-care capacity
After calculating the total score, the indicator must also make an estimate of the duration of the serious, reduced self-care capacity, i.e. how long will the user suffer from this degree of care dependency?
This is always a subjective assessment, which will be based on the observation of the user, but also on previous experiences.


This excerpt sits under: MONITEUR BELGE

## CHAPTER XI. — Decision on coverage

The care fund decides on the application for coverage and determines the validity period of the decision on the basis of the indication assessment or the certificate. The care fund may carry out or have carried out additional investigations concerning the indication assessment before deciding on the coverage. However, these additional investigations are limited to a review of the original indication assessment.
These investigations may be carried out by a person appointed by the care fund or by an authorised indication assessor designated by the care fund for that purpose. This indication assessor must be a different person from the one who carried out the user's most recent indication assessment.
The person carrying out the indication assessment as part of an additional investigation must at least hold the level of the degree of graduate in family sciences, graduate in the field of health care or the field of social-pedagogical work, or a diploma that is recognised as equivalent in both content and level by the Education Department of the Ministry of the Flemish Community.

The investigation must always be carried out using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance. The BEL user photo is completed. If the user so wishes, the indication assessment is carried out at the place where the user resides.
After carrying out the indication assessment, the indication assessor sends the completed form 'additional investigation or review concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' to the care fund.
The care fund may, on the basis of this additional investigation, dispute the severity and duration of the reduced self-care capacity. In that case, it shall notify the user or his representative thereof and shall also notify that the user or his representative has the right to be heard. If the user or his representative so requests, they shall be heard by the care fund before it takes its final decision.
The validity period of the positive decision of the care fund is determined in accordance with the chapter 'validity period of a decision'.
The care fund may refuse an application for coverage because the user is not considered to be in long-term and serious need of care.
The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing of its decision on the coverage. In the case of a positive decision, this notification contains at least the following information:
•	the statement that the user is eligible for care insurance
•	the statement from when the user may exercise his right to coverage
•	the statement of the validity period of the decision on coverage
•	the statement of which form of care and which amount the user may claim
•	a justification of the decision
•	the notification that the user or his representative has the right to request a review of the decision on coverage at any time
•	the notification that the user or his representative must immediately notify his care fund of a change in the form of care and any (serious) change in the user's state of health. It is added that failure to notify these changes may lead to recovery from a user of wrongly obtained coverage
•	where applicable, the period of suspension of coverage. In the case of a negative decision, this notification contains the following information:
•	the notification that the user is not eligible for care insurance
•	the statement of the reason or reasons why the user is not eligible
•	a copy of the application and the indication assessments
•	the notification that the user or his representative may lodge an objection against the decision of the care fund with the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities concerning the lodging of an objection and the procedure to be followed.

## CHAPTER XII. — Validity period of a decision

The care fund decides on the duration of validity of a positive decision on the basis of the indication assessment.
A positive decision of a care fund is valid for at least six months.
The validity period of a positive decision for informal and home care is a maximum of three years, except for the validity period of a positive decision on the basis of a certificate based on at least a B-score in home nursing for users who are younger than 75 years at the time the decision takes effect, where the maximum validity period is one year.
The validity period of a positive decision for residential care is valid for an indefinite period.
The validity period of the positive decision of the care fund is calculated from the day on which the decision on coverage takes effect.

## CHAPTER XIII. — Change of form of care

A change of form of care occurs when one switches from informal and home care to professional residential care recognised or treated as recognised under the care insurance (and vice versa).
The user or his representative must immediately apply to the care fund for any change in the choice of a particular form of care.
The care fund decides on the application for change of form of care. The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing of its decision.
In the case of a positive decision, this notification contains at least the following information:
•	the statement that the user is eligible for the change of form of care
•	the statement from when the user is entitled to coverage for the new form of care
•	the statement of which form of care and which amount the user may claim
•	the statement of the validity period of the decision
In the case of a negative decision, this notification contains at least the following information:
•	the notification that the user is not eligible for the change of form of care
•	the statement of the reason or reasons why the user is not eligible

In the case of a change from informal and home care to residential care, the coverage for the new form of care takes effect, possibly with retroactive effect, from the first day of the first full month that the user resides in a recognised or treated as recognised residential facility.
In the case of a change from residential care to informal and home care, the coverage for the new form of care takes effect, possibly with retroactive effect, from the first day of the month following the one in which the user left the recognised or treated as recognised residential facility.
A move from a recognised or treated as recognised residential facility to another residential facility cannot be considered as a change of form of care. This must be dealt with by means of an application for review.
Transitional measure
For users who change from informal and home care to residential care before 1 April 2003 and who have a B-score on the evaluation scale in support of the application for benefit in a care institution, the coverage for residential care is carried out from 1 April 2003.
The table below shows, depending on the user's situation, the actions that the care fund must undertake.

## CHAPTER XIV

Decision to revise, extend or terminate an (original) positive decision Section I. — Revision at the request of the user or his representative
The user or his representative has the right to request a revision at any time. The revision is done by completing a revision form. The user or his representative completes the form, signs it and submits it to his care fund.
The procedure regarding the verification of the formal conditions and the possible determination of reduced self-care capacity proceeds in accordance with the chapter on processing an application. The care fund decides on the revision of the coverage.
The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing of its decision on the revision of the coverage within 60 days after the application was submitted. This notification contains, in the case of a positive decision, at least the information that is communicated in the case of a positive decision on an application for coverage. The positive decision on the revision of the coverage takes effect from the first day of the month following the date on which the revision was requested by the user or his representative.
A move from a recognised or equivalent residential facility to another residential facility must be processed via an application for revision.

Section II. — Extension of a positive decision
The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing three months before the expiry of the validity period that he must submit an application for extension of the positive decision.
The user, in consultation with the care fund, uses a certificate or the user or his representative, in consultation with the care fund, appoints an authorised indication provider.
The serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity is determined in accordance with chapter VIII.
The care fund decides on the extension and determines the validity period of the decision on the basis of the indication or the certificate. The care fund may carry out or have carried out additional investigations regarding the indication before deciding on the coverage.
The care fund takes a positive decision on the extension if the user is still considered to be in long-term and serious need of care. In the other case, the decision is terminated.
The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing of its decision on the extension of the coverage. This notification contains, in the case of a positive decision, at least the information that is communicated in the case of a positive decision on an application for coverage. The extension of the coverage takes effect on the day that the validity period of the previous decision expires.
Section III. — Termination of a positive decision
The care fund terminates a decision if the user no longer meets the formal conditions or if the user is no longer considered to be in serious and long-term need of care. The care fund notifies the decision to terminate in writing to the user or his representative.
The notification contains the following information in the case of a termination:
•	the notification that the user is no longer eligible for care insurance
•	the notification of the date from which the user will no longer receive amounts
•	the indication of the reason or reasons why the user is no longer eligible
•	where applicable, a copy of the determination of the long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity
•	the notification that the user or his representative may lodge an objection against the decision of the care fund with the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities regarding the lodging of an objection and the procedure to be followed.
The decision takes effect on the first day of the month following the day on which the user no longer meets the formal conditions or on which the user is no longer considered to have long-term and seriously reduced self-care capacity.
The decision to terminate as a result of no longer being considered to have long-term and seriously reduced self-care capacity takes effect:
•	on the first day of the month after the decision to terminate by the care fund in the case of a decision for home and community care
•	on the first day of the month after the determination by the insurance institution, in the case of a decision for residential care


This excerpt sits under: MONITEUR BELGE

## CHAPTER XV. — Control of coverage

Additional examinations and controls are carried out on at least 10% of the first applications for coverage, where an indication assessment has been carried out by an authorised indication assessor or where a certificate has been issued by a family care service and where the application has not been refused on the basis of the formal conditions.
The care fund may check the indication assessment of the user who receives coverage. This concerns controls after the care fund has already taken a decision on the coverage. The care fund checks the indication assessment on its own initiative or on behalf of the Flemish Care Fund.
The control is carried out by a person appointed by the care fund or by an authorised indication assessor designated by the care fund for that purpose. This indication assessor must be different from the one who carried out the last indication assessment of the user. The person carrying out the indication assessment must at least have the level of the degree of graduate in family sciences, graduate in the field of health care or the field of socio-educational work, or a diploma that is equivalent to this in both content and level by the Education Department of the Ministry of the Flemish Community.
The control must always be carried out using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance. The BEL user photo is completed. If the user so wishes, the indication assessment is carried out at the place where the user resides.
The indication assessor must carry out the indication assessment within twenty days after the day on which the care fund designated the indication assessor. This date also determines the order in which the indication assessments of different users by the same indication assessor must be sent to the care fund.
After carrying out the indication assessment, the indication assessor sends the completed form 'additional examination or control concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' to the care fund.
The care fund decides on the control of the coverage and determines the validity period of the decision on the basis of the indication assessment.
The care fund may refuse an application for coverage because the user is no longer considered to be in long-term and serious need of care.
The care fund communicates its decision on the coverage to the user or his representative in writing within sixty days after the notification to the user or his representative that a new indication assessment will be carried out.
In the case of a positive decision, this notification contains at least the information that is communicated in the case of a positive decision on an application for coverage.

In the case of a negative decision, the notification contains the following information:
•	the notification that the user is no longer eligible for care insurance
•	the notification of the date from which the user will no longer receive amounts
•	the indication of the reason or reasons why the user is no longer eligible
•	a copy of the form 'additional examination or control concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity'
•	the notification that the user or his representative may lodge an objection against the decision of the care fund with the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities relating to lodging an objection and the procedure to be followed.
The decision as a result of the control of the coverage takes effect from the first day of the month following the date of decision.
If the control is carried out on behalf of the Care Fund, the care fund also informs the Care Fund of the decision.

## CHAPTER XVI

Implementation of the coverage Section I. — Financial arrangement: general
The right to coverage begins on the first day of the third month after the application. Any suspension of coverage (see chapter IV) also begins from the first day of the third month after the application.
The user receives, depending on his choice for a particular form of care, the following amount to cover his costs:

form of care	amount per month
informal and home care	90 euros
professional residential care	125 euros

In the case of a user who dies during the month, the coverage for the month in question is still implemented.
Persons residing in a recognised or equivalently recognised rest home or residential care home receive coverage until 31 March 2003 only if they meet the formal conditions and if they have a score C on the scale for allowance in a care institution. If such a person would have a B-score after reassessment, then the coverage for residential care is implemented again from 1 April 2003. The user or his representative does not need to submit a new application for this.
Section II. — Financial arrangement with regard to informal and home care for users residing in Flanders The care fund pays the amount for informal and home care to the user monthly in cash to the
user or by transfer to the user's account number.
Section III. — Financial arrangement with regard to informal and home care for users residing in Brussels The care fund pays the amount for informal and home care to the user in cash to the user or by
transfer to the user's account number.
The coverage for informal and home care is only implemented if the user in the same month uses or stays in one of the following facilities recognised by the Flemish government or equivalently recognised:
•	a family care service
•	a local or regional service centre
•	a short-stay centre
•	a day care centre
•	a respite care service
•	a service flat
•	a family care service or PCSW that provides cleaning assistance recognised or equivalently recognised in the context of care insurance.
If such a facility provides care to the user in a given month, then the user is entitled to the coverage for informal and home care for that month.
As proof of the provision of care, the invoice that the user provides to the care fund applies.
The invoice must be submitted within six months after the service month to which it relates.
The invoice contains the following information:
•	invoice date and invoice number
•	name and address of facility or GKB number of facility
•	name, first name and address of user
•	period (at least per calendar month) in which service was provided
•	description of type of assistance in accordance with recognition (for example: not logistical assistance but cleaning assistance)

Section IV. — Financial arrangement with regard to services provided by professional, residential facilities The care fund pays directly to the user the coverage for residential care in cash to
the user or by transfer to the user's account number. This is done on the basis of a valid certificate based on a score that qualifies for residential care in accordance with the chapter determination of the serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity in the application for coverage'.
The user or his representative provides the care fund with the said certificate.
The residential facility reports to the care fund the following data in the month following that in which the situation of the users has changed in these areas:
•	termination of the residence agreement with the residential facility by the user;
•	death of the user;
•	change in the score on the evaluation scale in support of the application for allowance in a care institution whereby the user no longer falls under the conditions in the area of care needs in the context of care insurance.
The residential facility reports this before the fifth day of the month following that in which the user's situation has changed.
Payment takes place at the earliest during the month following the month to which the coverage relates.

## CHAPTER XVII

Recovery of unduly executed reimbursements Section I. — Recovery
The care fund can recover reimbursements that it has unduly executed for a user in two ways:
•	it deducts the amounts from the reimbursements that it will execute for the user in the following months
•	it recovers the amounts from the user
Before the care fund decides to recover the unduly executed reimbursements, it notifies the user or his representative of this and also notifies that the user or his representative has the right to be heard. If the user or his representative requests this, they will be heard by the care fund before it makes its final decision.
The care fund communicates its decision in writing to the user. This notification contains at least the following information:
•	the amount that is being recovered
•	the reason or reasons why the amount is being recovered
•	the manner in which the amount will be recovered with, where applicable, the repayment plan If the undue execution of the reimbursements is due to information deliberately withheld by the user or his representative or information deliberately provided incorrectly or incompletely, then the care fund can suspend the execution of the reimbursement for a maximum of six months. The care fund communicates its decision in the letter announcing the recovery of the amount or amounts. The suspension takes effect on the first day of the month following the notification. Months in which the care fund deducts the reimbursement to recover amounts cannot count towards the suspension.
The user or his representative can appeal to the Flemish Care Fund against the decision of the care fund to suspend the user. The appeal does not lead to a suspension of the decision of the care fund.
The limitation period for recovering unduly executed reimbursements is 150 days if the user proves that he received reimbursements in good faith to which he is not entitled. In other cases, the thirty-year limitation period applies.

Section II. — Exemption from recovery
The care funds can be exempted by the Flemish Care Fund from recovery of unlawfully executed reimbursements from a user under the following conditions:
•	the undue payment may not result from an error, a mistake or a negligence of the care fund;
•	the care fund demonstrates that all means available to it, including legal remedies, have been used to claim repayment. This condition is considered fulfilled when the recovery is considered uncertain or when the costs associated with the execution of the recovery exceed the amount to be recovered. Sending a bailiff is not required if the amount to be recovered is less than 270 euros;
•	the undue payment of a reimbursement for home care may not have occurred before the twentieth of the month to which it relates.
The care fund requests the exemption from recovery from the Flemish Care Fund. The Fund designs a form for this purpose.
The chief official of the Flemish Care Fund makes a decision whether or not to exempt the care fund from the recovery of the unduly executed reimbursements. A negative decision by the chief official is reasoned. The Care Fund communicates this decision to the care fund.
The Flemish Care Fund can determine further rules regarding the exemption from recovery.

## CHAPTER XVIII

Cumulation of the reimbursement with benefits under other provisions Section I. — Home care and personal assistance budget (PAB)
Persons are not entitled to the execution of a reimbursement for home care if they use the personal assistance budget (PAB) in the same month. However, the decision cannot be refused or stopped due to the fact that someone uses the personal assistance budget.
The Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities regularly provides an updated file to the Flemish Care Fund. This file contains the following data of people who have a PAB:
•	national register number
•	name and address of disabled person
•	PAB start date
The Fund notifies the care funds which of their members are entitled to a PAB. These are then not entitled to home care.
Section II. — Home care and residence in a residential facility recognised by the Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities
Persons are not entitled to a reimbursement for home care if they reside according to the protocol of residence in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognised by the Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities. These are the following facilities:
•	homes for non-workers
•	boarding schools
•	homes for workers
•	sheltered living services
•	centres for observation, orientation and medical, psychological and pedagogical treatment Where applicable, the reimbursements for the months in question are suspended.
If a person who resides according to a protocol of residence in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognised by the Flemish Fund is absent from this facility for 21 or more consecutive days, then he is entitled to a reimbursement for home care for the month or months in which this continuous period of absence falls.
The user or his representative must declare on the application form whether the user resides in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognised by the Flemish Fund or is on the waiting list for such a facility.
If the user only goes to reside in such a facility after submitting his application form, then he (or his representative) must report this to the care fund.

## CHAPTER XIX. — Objection to the Flemish Care Fund

The user or his representative may submit an objection to the Flemish Care Fund against the following decisions of the care fund:
•	the decision regarding the application for coverage whereby the care fund decides that the user does not meet the formal conditions to be eligible for coverage
•	the decision on the coverage, whether or not after additional investigation
•	the decision on the revision of the coverage
•	the decision on the extension of a coverage
•	the decision on a termination of the coverage
•	the decision following the inspection of the coverage
•	the decision to suspend the coverage as a result of information deliberately withheld by the user or his representative or information deliberately provided incorrectly or incompletely
The objection must be accompanied by all relevant documents and must be reasoned. For each decision this amounts to the following:
•	regarding the decision on the application for coverage, the objection contains:
➢	the application form for coverage under the care insurance
➢	the decision of the care fund regarding the application for coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user does not meet the formal conditions.
•	Regarding the decision on the coverage
➢	the application form for coverage under the care insurance
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection"
➢	the decision on the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user is not eligible for coverage under the care insurance
•	regarding the decision on the revision of the coverage:
the application form for coverage under the care insurance
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection"
➢	the decision on the coverage
➢	where applicable, the dated and signed addition to the original application
➢	the revision form
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection" that contains the record of the additional investigation concerning the revision
➢	the decision on the revision of the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user is no longer eligible upon revision of the decision for coverage.

•	regarding the decision on the extension of the coverage:
➢	the form 'determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' or the certificate
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection"
➢	the decision on the extension of the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user is no longer eligible upon revision of the decision for coverage.
•	regarding the decision on the termination of the coverage:
➢	the last decision on the coverage before the decision to terminate
➢	where applicable, the form 'determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' or the certificate that led to the decision to terminate
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection" that led to the decision to terminate
➢	the decision to terminate the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the decision was terminated
•	Regarding the decision following the inspection of the coverage
➢	the application form for coverage under the care insurance
➢	where applicable, the form "additional investigation or inspection"
➢	the decision on the coverage
➢	the written notification that an indicator designated by the care fund will carry out a new indication
➢	the decision following the inspection of the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user is no longer eligible after inspection of the decision for coverage
•	Regarding the decision to suspend the coverage as a result of information deliberately withheld by the user or his representative or information deliberately provided incorrectly or incompletely
➢	the decision on the coverage
➢	the last decision (following inspection or revision)
➢	where applicable, the last decision on the change of form of care
➢	the decision to suspend the coverage
➢	a contestation with argumentation of the reason or reasons stated by the care fund why the user is no longer eligible after inspection of the decision for coverage
The user or his representative must send the objection by registered letter to the Care Fund within thirty days after receipt of the decision of the care fund or submit the objection to the Care Fund within thirty days against acknowledgement of receipt. If one wishes to be heard, this must be requested in the objection.
The care fund provides at the request of the user or his representative within five days after receipt of the request the documents that are necessary for the parties concerned to be able to submit an objection to the Care Fund. If the care fund can prove that it has already provided the documents to the parties concerned, the care fund may charge costs for this.
The secretariat of the chamber of the objection committee at the Flemish Care Fund notifies the care fund in writing within fifteen days after receipt of an admissible objection that an admissible objection has been submitted. The notification contains at least the following information:
•	the decision of the care fund against which the objection was submitted
•	the contestation formulated by the user, his representative with argumentation of the reasons cited by the care fund for the decision
•	the notification that the care fund has the right to be heard
The secretariat of the chamber also notifies the user or his representative within fifteen days after receipt of an objection whether the objection has been declared admissible.
In case the objection is admissible, the notification contains the following information:
•	the notification that the objection is admissible
•	the notification that the objection committee must give a reasoned opinion on the objection and that the chief official of the Care Fund must make a reasoned decision
In case the objection is not admissible, the notification contains the following information:
•	the notification that the objection is not admissible
•	the notification of the reason(s) why the objection is not admissible
The chamber of the objection committee handles the objection and gives a reasoned opinion within three months after receipt of the objection. The chamber hears the care fund and the user or his representative, if they have requested this.
The chief official of the Care Fund makes a reasoned decision within one month after receipt of the opinion of the chamber or, in the absence of a timely opinion, after expiry of the period. The Care Fund sends the decision of the chief official within 8 days to the user or his representative and to the care fund in question.
The decision of the chief official has retroactive effect from the date on which the decision of the care fund came into effect.

## CHAPTER XX. — Correction by the care funds of erroneous decisions

If the care fund itself has taken an erroneous decision, it may correct this by sending a new decision to the user or his representative, whereby the old decision is declared null and void with mention of the reason why.
If the correction of the erroneous decision still occurs within sixty days after the application for (the revision of) the coverage, copies of the erroneous and the corrected decisions must be kept in the file.
If the erroneous decision is corrected later than sixty days after the application for (the revision of) the coverage, then, in addition to keeping the copies in the files, copies of the erroneous and the corrected decision must also be provided to the Flemish Care Fund.

## CHAPTER XXI. — Data collection

Section I. — Record type A: members
Structure
header: year and month, care fund, type A (YYYYMMXXXA) care fund code:
180 = CM care fund
280 = Neutral Care Fund Flanders
380 = Care fund of the Socialist Mutuals 480 = Care fund of the liberal health insurance funds
580 = Care fund of the Independent Health Insurance Funds 680 = Flemish care fund
780 = Omob Care Fund
880 = Care fund DKV Belgium
Fields:
a.	national register number (field length 11, numeric)
b.	year of birth (field length 4, numeric)
c.	municipality code (NIS code of merged municipality) (field length 5, numeric)
d.	date of commencement of membership DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
e.	date of payment of the last membership contribution DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
f.	processing date of the commencement of membership DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
g.	membership code (field length 2, numeric) 00 = membership at start-up of care insurance 10 = reaching age
20 = submission of application for coverage (younger than 26 years)
21 = approved application for coverage (younger than 26 years, is effective member of the care fund) 30 = coming to live in Flanders
40 = coming to live in Brussels (and membership within 6 months) 50 = voluntary membership in Brussels (after more than 6 months) 7X = transfer from other care fund
71 = CM care fund
72 = Neutral Care Fund Flanders
73 = Care fund of the Socialist Mutuals
74 = Care fund of the liberal health insurance funds
75 = Care fund of the Independent Health Insurance Funds
76 = Flemish care fund
77 = Omob Care Fund
78 = Care fund DKV Belgium
80= ex officio membership (only for Flemish care fund) 90 = unknown
h.	regularisation code (EU national) (field length 2, numeric) 00 = no regularisation
10 = Belgian nationality
11 = other nationality
i.	date of termination of membership DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
j.	processing date of the termination of membership DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)

k.	code of termination of membership (field length 4, numeric) 0000 = no termination of membership
1010 = death
1020 = end of care dependency (younger than 26 years) 1030 = leaving Flanders or Brussels
1050 = voluntary termination in Brussels 1060 = deletion of double membership 107X = transfer to other care fund
1071 = CM care fund
1072 = Neutral Care Fund Flanders
1073 = Care fund of the Socialist Mutuals 1074 = Care fund of the liberal health insurance funds
1075 = Care fund of the Independent Health Insurance Funds 1076 = Flemish care fund
1077 = Omob Care Fund
1078 = Care fund DKV Belgium 1080 = other
1090 = unknown
l.	amount paid during the calendar year (field length 5, numeric) (999.99 comma must not be added. If the member concerned has not yet paid in the calendar year concerned, this field is filled with zeros.
m.	increased insurance reimbursement (field length 1, numeric)
0 = provisionally unknown
1 = person does not benefit from increased insurance reimbursement
2 = person benefits from increased insurance reimbursement
Frequency and volume:
The database data must be submitted quarterly. The database data are cumulative: the data from previous months and years must also be added.
Transition 2002-2003:
Members who terminate or have terminated their membership on 31/12/2002 may no longer be included in text file A, except for outgoing transfers from 2002 - 2003. These must still be included in files 200303A and 200306A.
Explanation of fields:
national register number: national register number of the member concerned year of birth: year of birth of the member concerned
municipality code: NIS - code of the merged municipality where the person concerned was last resident in the Flemish Region or the Brussels-Capital Region (situation last day of the requested quarter).
date of commencement of membership: date of the membership of the member concerned with the care fund concerned. The membership date is always of the form 01/01/YYYY with YYYY the first year that the member concerned was affiliated with the care fund.
date of payment of the last membership contribution: date on which the last membership contribution was paid by the member concerned. If a regularisation is still paid by the member concerned in the year concerned, this field must not be adjusted.
processing date of the commencement of membership
membership code: the type of membership concerned
00 = membership start-up care insurance. From next year onwards, this code may no longer be used for new memberships (except for persons who do not wish to join the Flemish care fund ex officio and for the time being choose a care fund other than 680).
10 = reaching age. This concerns persons who turn 26 years old in the current year and live in Flanders or in the Brussels-Capital Region. The membership contribution must be paid before 1 July.
20 = this code must be used when a person younger than 26 years submits an application for coverage.
21= this code must be used when a person younger than 26 years has a positive decision for coverage.
30 = coming to live in Flanders. This concerns persons who turn or are 26 years old in the current year and come to live in Flanders. The contribution must be paid within six months after coming to live in Flanders.
40 = coming to live in Brussels. This concerns persons who turn or are 26 years old in the current year and come to live in Brussels. The contribution must be paid within six months after coming to live in Brussels
50 = voluntary membership in Brussels 7x = incoming transfer from care fund x
80 = the ex officio membership by the Flemish care fund. The Flemish care fund is the only care fund that may use this code.
90 = unknown
regularisation code: only complete this code if it concerns a regularisation (see manual pg 21) date of termination of membership: is always of the form 31/12/yyyy (also for double memberships) or this
field is filled with zeros (if there is no termination).
processing date termination of membership: code of termination of membership
0000 = no termination of membership

1010 = death
1020 = end of care dependency (code 21) or refused application (code20). This code can only be used if the member concerned is not yet 26.
1030 = leaving Flanders or Brussels 1050 = voluntary termination in Brussels 1060 = deletion of double membership 107X = transfer to other care fund
1080 = other
1090 = unknown
amount paid during calendar year: in this field the amount of the membership contribution for the calendar year is entered, increased with any amounts for regularisations.
increased insurance reimbursement indicates whether the member concerned is a VT'er or not. For 2003, the situation on 01/01/2002 is relevant.
Section II. — Record type B: coverage files
Structure
header: year and month, care fund, type B (YYYYMMXXXB) care fund code:
180 = CM care fund
280 = Neutral Care Fund Flanders
380 = Care fund of the Socialist Mutuals 480 = Care fund of the liberal health insurance funds
580 = Care fund of the Independent Health Insurance Funds 680 = Flemish care fund
780 = Omob Care Fund
880 = Care fund DKV Belgium
Fields:
a.	National register number (field length 11, numeric)
b.	Year of birth (field length 4, numeric)
c.	NIS code of merged municipality (field length 5, numeric)
d.	Code of decentralised point of the care fund (field length 3, numeric)
e.	Code application, revision or extension (field length 1, numeric)
0 = application
1 = revision
2 = extension
f.	date of the application, revision or extension DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
g.	code of indication assessor or certificate for application, revision or extension (field length 6, numeric) 207001 = family care services
201001 = public social welfare centres
213001 = centres for social welfare 207003 = local service centres
900210 = Katz scale home nursing
900220 = evaluation scale reimbursement in care institutions 900230 = proof of admission to psychiatric care home 900240 = BEL profile scale in family care
900280 = medical-social scale for integration allowance, allowance assistance to the elderly, allowance assistance from third parties
900290 = additional child benefit on behalf of the child
h.	result of the indication assessment for application, revision or extension (field length 2, numeric, field = 99 if it concerns a certificate)
i.	code additional examination (field length 1, numeric)
0 = no additional examination
1 = additional examination
j.	date of indication assessment additional examination DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
k.	result of the indication assessment for additional examination (field length 2, numeric, if no additional examination field 99)
l.	code decision (field length 1, numeric)
0 = no decision yet
1 = approved
2 = approved but limited by accumulation (including Flemish Fund)
4 = administratively refused file
5 = other refusals
m.	date of the decision DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
n.	date of last change of care form DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)

o.	code care form (field length 1, numeric)
0 = no positive decision after application, revision or extension
4 = residential care
5 = informal and home care
p.	code decision after inspection (field length 1, numeric)
0 = no inspection or no decision yet after inspection
1 = continuation
3 = termination
q.	result of the indication assessment for inspection (field length 2, numeric, if no inspection field = 99)
r.	date of the decision after inspection DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
s.	end date for execution of coverage DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
t.	code payment or recovery
0 = payment
1 = recovery
u.	amount that was paid or recovered during the calendar year concerned (possibly 0 euro) (field length 6, numeric) (9999.99 comma must not be added)
v.	amount of the outstanding credit at the end of the quarter (possibly 0 euro) (field length 6, numeric) (9999.99 comma must not be added)
w.	parameter 1 (field length 8, numeric)
x.	parameter 2 (field length 8, numeric)
y.	parameter 3 (field length 8, numeric)
z.	parameter 4 (field length 8, numeric)
Frequency and volume:
The database data must be submitted quarterly. The database data are cumulative: the data from previous months must also be added. Except where this is explicitly stated, fields that do not need to be completed are filled with zeros.
Transition 2002-2003:
Since the credits from 2002 may still be used until and including April 2003, this means that all active files from 2002 must be included in text file B of 2003.
Active files from the year 2002 are:
—	the current files (files whose coverage end date falls after 31/12/2002)
—	the files that are no longer current but whose credit is different from zero These records then remain in text file B of 2003 at least until the end of 2003.
The credit from 2002 can no longer be used after April 2003. This means that in file 200303B and 200306B the credit from 2002 is still included. After that, the credit from 2002 is set to zero and is therefore no longer communicated in files 200309B and 200312B.
For Brussels, it is the case that there must still be "credit" communicated for the year 2003. This means that in files 200303B and 200306B the credit field for the files in Brussels will contain both the credit from 2002 and that from 2003.
Explanation of fields:
a.	 national register number:
b.	 year of birth:
c.	 NIS code merged municipality: NIS - code of the merged municipality where the person concerned was last resident in the Flemish Region or the Brussels-Capital Region (situation last day of the requested quarter)
d.	 Code of decentralised point of the care fund: the department where the file concerned can be physically consulted.
e.	 Code application, revision or extension: this field indicates whether this concerns an application, a revision or an extension. This field was introduced since a revision and an extension becomes a new record.
f.	 date of the application, revision or extension: date on which the application or revision is requested. For an extension, this date is equal to the date of execution of the indication assessment or the date of submission of the certificate to the care fund.
g.	 code of indication assessor or certificate for application, revision or extension
