h.	result of the indication assessment for application, revision or extension: here the score of the care-dependent person must be entered for the indication assessment. If no indication assessment has taken place (a certificate is used) then this field must be filled by entering "99", and not 00. Therefore,
"fill nines" are used here instead of fill zeros. The score may be entered at the moment that the care fund becomes aware of the result of the indication assessment, i.e. before the actual decision was taken.
i.	 code additional examination: here it is indicated whether an additional examination has taken place or not.
j.	 date of indication assessment for additional examination: date on which the additional examination (i.e. actually the additional indication assessment) took place
k.	 result of the indication assessment for additional examination: here the score of the indication assessment must be entered for additional examination. The score may be entered at the moment that the care fund becomes aware of the result of the indication assessment. If no additional examination takes place or the result is not yet known, then this field is filled by entering "99".

l.	 Code decision: is a consolidation of the former fields j. "Code decision" and m. "code decision after additional examination". (The code here is the code whether or not after additional examination). The option 3 = additional examination in the former field j. has been replaced by field i. "code additional examination".
m.	 date of the decision: date on which the decision on the application, revision or extension was taken.
n.	 date of last change of care form: date on which the last change of the care form takes effect. If there are multiple changes, the last date is retained. This date is the date on which the amount of the payment effectively changes. It is therefore always a date of the form 01/mm/yyyy.
o.	Code care form: in this field the last valid care form is entered. The code for the application no longer needs to be entered. It is only when the application is approved that this field must be completed. When the care form changes, this field is simply adjusted and field n. "date of last change of care form" is completed.
p.	 code decision after inspection:
q.	result of the indication assessment for inspection: here the score of the indication assessment for inspection must be entered. If no inspection has taken place or the result of the inspection is not yet known, then this field must be filled by entering 99, and not 00. Therefore, "fill nines" are used here instead of fill zeros. The score may be entered at the moment that the care fund becomes aware of the result of the indication assessment, this can therefore fall before the date on which the actual decision of the inspection was taken.
r.	 date of the decision after inspection: date on which the decision after inspection was taken.
s.	 end date for execution of coverage: this field is the consolidation of fields n. "end date for execution of coverage" and x. "end date for execution of coverage after inspection or revision" in the former B files. The last valid end date must be entered here. For residential care, this date is equal to "00000000".
t.	 code payment or recovery: with this field we can determine whether field u. "amount paid or recovered during the calendar year concerned" is a positive or negative amount.
u.	 amount that was paid or recovered during the calendar year concerned: in this field the cumulative amount (or balance) of all payments and recoveries during the calendar year concerned is recorded.
v.	 amount of the outstanding credit at the end of the quarter concerned
fields w.; x.; y.; z.: are possibly still to be determined fields for the financial responsibility of the care funds
Section III. — Record type D: indication assessors and results
Structure
header: year and month, care fund, type D (YYYYMMXXXD) care fund code:
180 = CM care fund
280 = Neutral Care Fund Flanders
380 = Care fund of the Socialist Mutuals 480 = Care fund of the liberal health insurance funds
580 = Care fund of the Independent Health Insurance Funds 680 = Flemish care fund
780 = Omob Care Fund
880 = Care fund DKV Belgium
Fields:
a.	National register number (field length 11, numeric)
b.	Year of birth (field length 4, numeric)
c.	NIS code of merged municipality (field length 5, numeric)
d.	Code of decentralised point of the care fund (field length 3, numeric)
e.	GKB number indication assessor (field length 8, numeric)
f.	code indication assessor (field length 6, numeric) 207001 = family care services 201001 = public social welfare centres
213001 = centres for social welfare 207003 = local service centres
g.	date of the application, revision or extension DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
h.	date of the indication assessment DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
i.	result of the indication assessment (field length 2, numeric)
j.	date of the payment to the indication assessor DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
k.	date of the recovery from the indication assessor DDMMYYYY (field length 8, numeric)
Frequency and volume:
The database data must be submitted quarterly. The database data are cumulative: the data from previous months must also be added.
Transition 2002-2003:
File 2003 is rebuilt from 0. No records from 2002 are transferred to file 2003.

Explanation of fields:
a.	 national register number:
b.	 year of birth
c.	 NIS code merged municipality: NIS - code of the merged municipality where the person concerned was last resident in the Flemish Region or the Brussels-Capital Region (situation last day of the requested quarter).
d.	 Code of decentralised point of the care fund: department where the file concerned can be physically consulted.
e.	 GKB number indication assessor:
f.	 code indication assessor or certificate
g.	date of the application, revision or extension: date of the application, revision or extension (see explanation file B). If no application or revision has yet been submitted, this field may be "00000000". For an extension, this date is always equal to field h. date of the indication assessment.
h.	 date of the indication assessment: date on which the indication assessment is executed
i.	 result of the indication assessment: score of the care-dependent person for the indication assessment.
j.	date of the payment to the indication assessor: date on which the amount is paid. If it concerns an indication assessment that has not yet been paid or that does not need to be paid, then this field may remain "00000000".
k.	 date of the recovery from the indication assessor: date on which the recovery is collected. If no recovery takes place, then this field becomes "00000000".

## CHAPTER XXII. — Final provisions

The explanatory sheets 7/001, 8/001, 17/001, 18/001, 22/001, 25/001, 27/001, 27/002, 41/001, 41/002, 46/001 to the care insurance manual of the ministerial decree of 1 October 2001 approving the care insurance manual are repealed.
Seen to be annexed to the ministerial decree of approving the care insurance manual.
Brussels, 23 December 2002.
The Flemish Minister for Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,
M. VOGELS

MINISTRY OF THE FLEMISH COMMUNITY
Department of Welfare, Public Health and Culture

[C − 2003/35126]
23 DECEMBER 2002. — Ministerial decree determining the forms in connection with care insurance

The Flemish Minister for Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,

Having regard to the decree of 30 March 1999 on the organisation of care insurance, as amended by the decrees of 22 December 1999, 8 December 2000, 18 May 2001 and 20 December 2002;
Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 on recognition, registration and authorisation, and on affiliation, application and assumption of costs within the framework of care insurance, as amended by the decrees of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001, 25 October 2002 and 13 December 2002;
Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 3 July 2002 determining the powers of the members of the Flemish Government,
Decrees:

### Article 1

In implementation of Article 21, § 1, Article 29, and Article 34, § 1, of the decree of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 on recognition, registration and authorisation, and on affiliation, application and assumption of costs within the framework of care insurance, the user or his representative uses one of the following forms:
1˚ the application form annexed to this decree as Annex I, for an application for home and community care; 2˚ the application form annexed to this decree as Annex II, for an application for residential care.

### Art. 2

In implementation of Article 37, § 1, of the same decree, the needs assessor designated by the care fund uses the form annexed to this decree as Annex III.

### Art. 3

The ministerial decree of 1 October 2001 determining the forms in connection with care insurance is repealed.

### Art. 4

This decree enters into force on 1 January 2003. Brussels, 23 December 2002. The Flemish Minister for Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,
M. VOGELS

Annex I to the ministerial decree of 23 December 2002 establishing the forms in connection with care insurance

This annex establishes the application form for home and community care. Annex II to the ministerial decree of 23 December 2002 establishing the forms in connection with care insurance
This annex establishes the application form for residential care. Annex III to the ministerial decree of 23 December 2002 establishing the forms in connection with care insurance

This annex establishes the form for additional examination or inspection concerning the determination of long-term severely reduced self-care capacity. MINISTRY OF THE FLEMISH COMMUNITY
Department of Welfare, Public Health and Culture

Welfare Care

[C − 2003/35209]

By decree of the Flemish Minister for Welfare, Health and Equal Opportunities of 17 January 2003 it is determined:
The non-profit association Centre for General Welfare Work Metropool in Antwerp is, with effect from 1 January 2003, recognised for fulfilling the tasks in accordance with the commitment that the centre has entered into in this regard in its policy plan that applies for the period from 1 January 2003 to 31 December 2005 inclusive, including the additional task of social assistance and services to detainees and their relatives with a view to their social reintegration. In accordance with Article 9 of the decree of the Flemish Government of 12 October 2001 implementing the decree of 19 December 1997 concerning general welfare work, the minimum number of staff members for which the recognition applies is set at 78.25 full-time equivalents. The working area of this centre is determined as follows:
—	administrative district of Antwerp, with the exception of the municipalities of Boom and Rumst (general tasks and the additional task of debt mediation); —	judicial district of Antwerp (the additional task of offender assistance). This recognition may be withdrawn at any time for reason of non-compliance with the conditions set out in the regulations concerning the aforementioned organisation. This decree replaces the ministerial decree of 5 December 2002 concerning recognition of the non-profit association Centre for General Welfare Work Metropool in Antwerp. *

Welfare Care

By decree of the Director-General of the Administration for Family and Social Welfare, on behalf of the Flemish Minister responsible for assistance to persons, of 21 January 2003 it is determined:
The P.C.S.W. of Evergem, Sleidinge-dorp 54, at 9940 Evergem, is granted prior authorisation for the construction of a service flat building with 5 residential units, Spoorwegstraat 12, at 9940 Evergem. This prior authorisation is valid for five years and this period of validity may be extended once by three years on condition that an admissible application for obtaining an extension of the granted prior authorisation is submitted to the administration no later than two months before its expiry. If the initiator has not commenced the works necessary to realise the initiative within the period of validity of the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses by operation of law. If the initiative has only been partially realised twelve years after the date of the minister's decision granting the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses for the non-realised admission capacities. Against this decision, any interested party may lodge a claim for suspension and/or an appeal for annulment with the Council of State by registered letter within sixty days after notification of this decision. By decree of the Director-General of the Administration for Family and Social Welfare, on behalf of the Flemish Minister responsible for assistance to persons, of 21 January 2003 it is determined:
The non-profit association WZW Ter Hovingen, Kliniekstraat 29, at 9050 Ghent, is granted prior authorisation for the construction of a rest home with 153 residential units, in replacement and reconditioning of the existing rest home Ter Hovingen, same address. This prior authorisation is valid for five years and this period of validity may be extended once by three years on condition that an admissible application for obtaining an extension of the granted prior authorisation is submitted to the administration no later than two months before its expiry. If the initiator has not commenced the works necessary to realise the initiative within the period of validity of the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses by operation of law. If the initiative has only been partially realised twelve years after the date of the minister's decision granting the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses for the non-realised admission capacities. Against this decision, any interested party may lodge a claim for suspension and/or an appeal for annulment with the Council of State by registered letter within sixty days after notification of this decision. By decree of the Director-General of the Administration for Family and Social Welfare, on behalf of the Flemish Minister responsible for assistance to persons, of 22 January 2003 it is determined:
The recognition under number CE 432 of the rest home Molenkouter, Dreefstraat 11A, at 9260 Wichelen, managed by the P.C.S.W. of Wichelen, same address, is extended with effect from 1 March 2003 to 1 July 2007 for 70 residential units. The capacity expansion by 1 residential unit of the rest home Molenkouter, Dreefstraat 11A, at 9260 Wichelen, managed by the P.C.S.W. of Wichelen, same address, is recognised with effect from 1 July 2002 to 1 July 2007. The maximum admission capacity of the rest home amounts to 71 residential units. Against this decision, any interested party may lodge a claim for suspension and/or an appeal for annulment with the Council of State by registered letter within sixty days after notification of this decision. By decree of the Director-General of the Administration for Family and Social Welfare, on behalf of the Flemish Minister responsible for assistance to persons, of 23 January 2003 it is determined:
The ministerial decree of 11 June 2002 is amended as follows: The non-profit association Sint-Jozef, Zandstraat 33, at Sint-Gillis-Waas (Sint-Pauwels), is granted prior authorisation for the construction of a service flat building with 25 residential units located at Lamstraat 2, at Sint-Niklaas. The prior authorisation dated 11 June 2002 is valid until 11 June 2007 and this period of validity may, in accordance with the transitional provisions contained in the decree of the Flemish Government of 17 March 1998, aforementioned, be extended once by three years on condition that an admissible application for obtaining an extension of the granted prior authorisation is submitted to the administration no later than two months before its expiry. If the initiator has not commenced the works necessary to realise the initiative within the period of validity of the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses by operation of law. If the initiative has only been partially realised twelve years after the date of the minister's decision granting the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses for the non-realised admission capacities. Against this decision, any interested party may lodge a claim for suspension and/or an appeal for annulment with the Council of State by registered letter within sixty days after notification of this decision. *

MINISTRY OF THE FLEMISH COMMUNITY
Department of Environment and Infrastructure

[C − 2003/35208]
Sewage treatment infrastructure. — Declarations of public utility

BILZEN AND HOESELT. — By decree of the Flemish Minister of Environment and Agriculture of 17 January 2003, the establishment of a sewage treatment infrastructure is declared to be of public utility. No. 20045
1)	Geographical description:
Bilzen: collector Beverst phase 2. 2)	Cadastral data:
City: Bilzen
Cadastrally known as: Division: 2; Section: F; Parcels: nos. 171 e and 168 e. Municipality: Hoeselt
Cadastrally known as: Division: 1; Section: B; Parcel: no. 162 a. 3)	Reason for declaration of public utility:
For the establishment of the sewage treatment infrastructure (collector) under, on or above private undeveloped land, which is not enclosed by a wall or a fence in accordance with the building or urban planning regulations. 4)	Competent authority:
Following this declaration of public utility, N.V. Aquafin will be able to encumber the land with an easement or acquire it in the name of the Flemish Region. 5)	Plans available for inspection at:
— MINISTRY OF THE FLEMISH COMMUNITY
Department of Environment and Infrastructure Administration of Environmental, Nature, Land and Water Management Water Division
Environmental Investments Unit Alhambra
Emile Jacqmainlaan 20, box 5
1000 BRUSSELS
—	N.V. Aquafin Dijkstraat 8
2630 AARTSELAAR
—	College of mayor and aldermen of and at
3740 BILZEN
—	College of mayor and aldermen of and at
3730 HOESELT
6)	Legal basis:
Decree of the Flemish Government dated 20 March 1991 establishing rules concerning the execution of works by N.V. Aquafin in application of articles 32septies and 32octies of the law of 26 March 1971 on the protection of surface waters against pollution. 7)	Date + competent minister 17 January 2003
The Flemish Minister of Environment and Agriculture, Vera DUA
8)	Limitation period
Any interested party may, by means of a signed petition, lodge an appeal for annulment against this decision with the Council of State within a period of 60 days after notification. The petition must be filed by registered mail with the Council of State together with three certified copies and, in addition, as many copies as there are opposing parties (

### article 85 of the rules of procedure of the Council of State)

COURT OF ARBITRATION
[2003/200086]
Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration

By petition addressed to the Court by letter sent by registered post on 30 January 2003 and received at the registry on 31 January 2003, J. Van den Driessche, residing at 1700 Dilbeek, Baron de Vironlaan 80, box 25, has brought an action for annulment and an application for suspension of article 5 of the law of 13 December 2002 amending the Electoral Code as well as its annex, and article 16 of the law of 13 December 2002 containing various amendments to electoral legislation (published in the Belgian Official Gazette of 10 January 2003), on grounds of violation of articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution.

This case is registered under number 2621 of the Court's roll.

The Registrar,
L. Potoms.

COURT OF ARBITRATION
[2003/200081]
Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration

By judgment of 3 December 2002 in the case of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, the copy of which was received at the registry of the Court of Arbitration on 10 December 2002, the Court of Appeal of Mons has posed the following preliminary question:

"Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution, as well as article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, violated by articles 67, §§ 1 and 2, of the CWATUP, decree of 14 May 1984, and 155, §§ 1 and 2, of the CWATUP, decree of 27 November 1997, which allow the delegated official of the administration of urban planning and spatial planning of the Walloon Region to intervene in criminal proceedings to pursue there, without being the holder of the public action and without having to submit to the rules governing the constitution of a civil party, an action which falls within the public action and has as its object a claim of a civil nature?"
This case is registered under number 2582 of the Court's roll.
The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

COURT OF ARBITRATION
[2003/200081]
Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration

By judgment of 3 December 2002 in the case of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, the copy of which was received at the registry of the Court of Arbitration on 10 December 2002, the Court of Appeal of Mons has posed the following preliminary question:

"Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution, as well as article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms violated by articles 67, §§ 1 and 2 of the W.W.R.O.S.P., decree of 14 May 1984, and 155, §§ 1 and 2, of the W.W.R.O.S.P., decree of 27 November 1997, which allow a delegated official of the administration of urban planning and spatial planning of the Walloon Region to intervene in criminal proceedings to pursue there, without being the holder of the criminal action and without having to submit to the rules concerning the constitution of a civil party, a case which falls within the criminal action and which concerns a claim of a civil nature?"
This case is registered under number 2582 of the Court's roll.
The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

COURT OF ARBITRATION
[2003/200081]
Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration

In its judgment of 3 December 2002 in the case of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, the copy of which was received at the registry of the Court of Arbitration on 10 December 2002, the Court of Appeal of Mons has posed the following preliminary question:

"Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution as well as article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms violated by articles 6

### §

§ 1 and 2 of the Walloon Code on Spatial Planning, Urban Planning and Heritage, Decree of 14 May 1984, and 15

### §

§ 1 and 2 of the Walloon Code on Spatial Planning, Urban Planning and Heritage, Decree of 27 November 1997, which allow an authorised official of the Administration for Spatial Planning and Urban Planning of the Walloon Region to join a criminal proceeding, in order thus, without bringing the public prosecution and without being subject to the rules concerning the bringing of an action as a civil party, to conduct a procedure that belongs to the domain of the public prosecution and concerns a claim of a civil nature?»
This case was entered in the Court's register under number 2582.

The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

BANKING AND FINANCE COMMISSION
[S − C − 2003/03082]
27 JANUARY 2003. — Order amending the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium

The Banking and Finance Commission,
Having regard to article 53 of the law of 6 April 1995 on secondary markets, the status of and supervision of investment firms, intermediaries and investment advisers pursuant to which the Banking and Finance Commission establishes the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium;
Having regard to the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium, established on 31 December 2002;
Whereas the Banking and Finance Commission granted on 30 January 2003 to the company ING Investment Management Belgium S.A., in short IIM Belgium, having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, avenue Marnix 24, authorisation as a portfolio management company for the following investment services referred to in article 46, 1°, of the law of 6 April 1995:
1. a) the reception and transmission, on behalf of investors, of orders relating to one or more financial instruments.
3.	the management, on a discretionary and individualised basis, of investment portfolios under a mandate given by investors where such portfolios include one or more financial instruments,
Orders:
The list of investment firms authorised in Belgium, established on 31 December 2002, is amended by the insertion on 30 January 2003 in the heading Portfolio management companies of the company ING Investment Management Belgium S.A., in short IIM Belgium, avenue Marnix 24, at 1000 Brussels, for investment services 1. a) and 3.
Brussels, 27 January 2003.
The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH

BANKING AND FINANCE COMMISSION
[C − 2003/03083]
Authorisation of transfer of rights and obligations between credit institutions (

### article 31

of the Act of 22 March 1993 on the status and supervision of credit institutions)

In accordance with article 30 of the Act of 22 March 1993, the Banking and Finance Commission has authorised the transfer on 31 January 2003 by "KBC Bank, S.A., avenue du Port 2, 1080 Brussels" to "CBC Banque, S.A., Grand-Place 5, 1000 Brussels" of all pension savings accounts managed by KBC Bank, which cumulatively meet the following two conditions:

—	the account number begins with: "729/……./..";
—	the account statement mentions: "pension savings account".
The transfer of these pension savings accounts includes their balance in units in the PRICOS pension fund, as well as all their current rights, obligations and charges relating thereto.
Pursuant to article 31 of the Act of 22 March 1993, any transfer between credit institutions or between such institutions and other financial institutions of the rights and obligations resulting from the operations of the institutions or undertakings concerned and authorised in accordance with article 30 of the said Act, shall be enforceable against third parties from the publication in the Moniteur belge of the authorisation of the Banking and Finance Commission.

Brussels, 27 January 2003.
The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH.

COMMISSIE VOOR HET BANK- EN FINANCIEWEZEN
[C − 2003/03083]
Authorisation for transfer of rights and obligations between credit institutions (

### artikel 31

of the Act of 22 March 1993 on the status and supervision of credit institutions)

In accordance with article 30 of the Act of 22 March 1993, the Commission for Banking and Finance has granted its authorisation for the transfer on 31 January 2003 by "KBC Bank, N.V., Havenlaan 2, 1080 Brussels" to "CBC Banque, N.V., Grote Markt 5, 1000 Brussels" of all pension savings accounts held at KBC Bank which cumulatively meet the following two conditions:
—	the account number begins with: "729/……./..";
—	the account statement mentions: "pension savings account".
These pension savings accounts are transferred with their balance in units in the PRICOS pension fund as well as with all their current rights, duties and charges encumbering them.
Pursuant to article 31 of the Act of 22 March 1993, any transfer between credit institutions or between such institutions and other institutions operating in the financial sector, of rights and obligations arising from operations of the institutions or undertakings concerned, for which authorisation has been granted in accordance with article 30 of the aforementioned Act, shall be enforceable against third parties as soon as the authorisation of the Commission for Banking and Finance has been published in the Belgisch Staatsblad.
Brussels, 27 January 2003.
The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH.

COMMISSION BANCAIRE ET FINANCIERE
[C − 2003/03081]
Authorisation for merger between a financial institution and an investment firm (articles 73 and 74 of the Act of 6 April 1995 on secondary markets, the status of investment firms and their supervision, intermediaries and investment advisers). — Amendment of the list of investment firms (

### article 53 of the same Act)

In accordance with article 73 of the Act of 6 April 1995, the Banking and Finance Commission has authorised the merger by absorption on 30 January 2003 between the financial institution Caisse Privée Banque S.A., place du Champ de Mars 2, at 1050 Brussels, absorbing company, and the asset management company BBL Capital Management Corporation S.A., avenue Marnix 24 at 1000 Brussels, absorbed company.

Pursuant to article 74 of the Act of 6 April 1995, any total or partial transfer between investment firms or between such firms and other financial institutions of the rights and obligations resulting from the operations of the companies or firms concerned, and authorised in accordance with the provisions of article 73 of the Act of 6 April 1995, shall be enforceable against third parties from the publication in the Moniteur belge of the authorisation of the Banking and Finance Commission.

Given the merger of the two companies on 30 January 2003, the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium, established on 31 December 2002, will be amended on 30 January 2003, under the heading "Investment firms under Belgian law" in the section "Asset management companies" by the removal of the asset management company BBL Capital Management Corporation S.A., avenue Marnix 24, at 1000 Brussels.

Brussels, 27 January 2003.

The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH

COMMISSIE VOOR HET BANK- EN FINANCIEWEZEN
[C − 2003/03081]
Authorisation for the merger between an institution operating in the financial sector and an investment firm (articles 73 and 74 of the Act of 6 April 1995 on secondary markets, the status and supervision of investment firms, intermediaries and investment advisers). — Amendment of the list of investment firms (

### article 53 of the same law)

In accordance with article 73 of the law of 6 April 1995, the Banking and Finance Commission has granted its authorisation for the merger by acquisition on 30 January 2003 between the financial sector institution Private Kas Bank N.V., Marsveldplein 2, 1050 Brussels, acquiring company, and the asset management company BBL Capital Management Corporation N.V., Marnixlaan 24, 1000 Brussels, acquired company.
Pursuant to article 74 of the law of 6 April 1995, any total or partial transfer between investment firms or between such institutions and other financial sector institutions, of rights and obligations arising from transactions of the institutions or firms concerned, for which authorisation has been granted in accordance with article 73 of the law of 6 April 1995, is enforceable against third parties as soon as the authorisation of the Banking and Finance Commission is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.
Given the merger of both companies on 30 January 2003, the list of investment firms drawn up on 31 December 2002 will be amended on 30 January 2003 under the heading "Investment firms under Belgian law" in the section "Asset management companies" by the deletion of the asset management company BBL Capital Management Corporation N.V. with registered office at 1000 Brussels, Marnixlaan 24.

Brussels, 27 January 2003.

The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH

Pursuant to article 31 of the law of 22 March 1993, any total or partial transfer between credit institutions of rights and obligations arising from the transactions of the institutions concerned and authorised in accordance with article 30 of the law of 22 March 1993 is enforceable against third parties as soon as the authorisation of the Banking and Finance Commission is published in the Belgian Official Gazette.
Brussels, 29 January 2003.
The Chairman,
E. WYMEERSCH.

[C − 2003/11058]
Notice. — Prior notification of a concentration operation
Case No. MEDE — C/C — 03/003
Scarlet B.V./Planet Internet N.V. and KPN Belgium II N.V.
On 27 January 2003, the Competition Council received notification of a draft contract within the meaning of article 12, § 1, of the Act on the protection of economic competition, coordinated by Royal Decree of 1 July 1999 (Belgian Official Gazette of 1 September 1999) from which it appears that Scarlet B.V. acquires exclusive control of Planet Internet N.V. and KPN Belgium II N.V., through the takeover of all assets, contracts and debts of Planet Internet N.V. and KPN Belgium II N.V., with the exception of a certain number of assets and debts, by a subsidiary of Scarlet B.V.. Scarlet B.V. also acquires indirectly control of Internet Needs Content N.V. through the takeover of the shareholding that Planet Internet N.V. holds in that company.

According to the notification, the economic sector concerned by this operation would be that of telecommunications.
The Competition Council invites interested third parties to submit their possible observations on the concentration operation preferably before 14 February 2003.

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY
[C − 2002/11436]
Competition Council. — Decision No. 2002-C/C-32 of 18 April 2002
Case CONC-C/C-02/0015 - Autoliv Inc. - Visteon Corporation
Having regard to the notification of the concentration operation filed with the secretariat of the Competition Council on 4 March 2002.
Having regard to the Report of the Corps of Reporters of 27 March 2002.
Having heard at the hearing of 18 April 2002 the Reporter in his report. Although duly summoned, the parties did not consider it necessary to be heard by the Competition Council and, by letter of 12 April 2002, acquiesced to the conclusions of the report.
A.	The parties concerned The acquirer
Autoliv Inc. is a company incorporated under the laws of the State of Delaware, in the United States, and whose executive headquarters is located in Stockholm, Sweden. It develops, manufactures and markets automotive restraint systems. It owns a production unit in Belgium, Autoliv Belgium N.V., whose registered office is located at Mai Zetterlingstraat 70, at 9042 Ghent [business secret].
Autoliv acquired the automotive safety unit of Morton International in 1997. The seller
Visteon Corporation is a company incorporated under the laws of the State of Delaware, in the United States and established in Dearborn, in the State of Michigan, in the United States of America. It also manufactures and markets automotive restraint systems.
The target company
The operations acquired are the North American and European activities of restraint electronic components of Visteon Corporation (United States, Canada and Portugal).
B.	The notified operation
Autoliv proposes to acquire part of the restraint electronic components assets of Visteon in North America and Europe. This unit produces and sells electronic airbag control components. Both parties produce and sell these products, but Autoliv is not active in this market in Belgium.
This transaction will enable Autoliv to establish itself in the United States where it is not yet active, unlike Visteon.
The aforementioned companies are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the law and the notified operation is a concentration operation within the meaning of Article 9 of the law.
On the basis of the indications provided by the parties, the turnover thresholds referred to in Article 11 of the law are met.
C.	The markets concerned
The economic sector concerned by the concentration is that of the manufacture of parts and accessories for motor vehicles (NACE code 34.3), but also that of trade in automotive equipment (NACE code: 50.3).
1.	Product markets concerned
On a global level, both parties develop, manufacture and market electronic airbag control components. These consist of an electronic control unit comprising a detector. They are integrated into vehicles where they are linked, via a cable, to the airbag module which they trigger in the event of an impact such as an accident. Airbag modules consist of a gas generator and a bag in a plastic cover integrated into the steering wheel or dashboard.
Airbag modules and electronic airbag control components are sophisticated products individually adapted to each vehicle model. For safety and quality reasons, no replacement market for electronic airbag control components exists and all spare parts are supplied by the original automobile manufacturer who, in turn, obtains them directly from the airbag systems supplier. Automobile manufacturers, as a general rule, purchase electronic airbag control components separately from airbag modules. They prefer to perform the integration of products themselves, insofar as this allows them to increase their flexibility in terms of technology and price.
In accordance with the approach adopted by the European Commission in application of the regulation on concentrations, the parties consider the manufacture and marketing of electronic airbag control components as a distinct market (cf. decisions No. IV/M.686, Nokia/Autoliv of 05/02/1996 and No. IV/M.872, TRW/Magna of 01/01/1997).
Consequently, the product market in question that may be retained is that of the manufacture and marketing of electronic airbag control components.
2.	Geographical market concerned
The geographical market consists of the entire Belgian territory.
The parties note, however, that the geographical market is in reality at least the European Economic Area.

D.	Competitive analysis
1.	Structure of supply and demand
The value of the market for the manufacture and marketing of electronic airbag control components in Belgium is [business secret] Mio $ according to Siemens [business secret].
Autoliv does not sell electronic airbag control components in Belgium. There is therefore no market affected by the operation in this case.
Besides Visteon, two other companies are present in the market in question in Belgium, namely Bosch and Siemens. The parties estimate their market shares as follows:
VISTEON: [business secret] % SIEMENS: [business secret] % BOSCH: [business secret] %
Siemens attributes to Visteon a market share of the order of [business secret] % in Belgium and evaluates its market share at [business secret] % based on the figures mentioned in doc. 4 ([business secret]).
[business secret]
According to the parties, Autoliv has a market share of [business secret] % at European level, while the Acquired Operations represent approximately [business secret] %.
2.	Assessment
Since Autoliv is not present in the market in question, the market shares of the parties will remain unchanged after the operation.
Furthermore, no actor questioned by the Competition Service makes any comments regarding this concentration. The Council therefore considers that the notified concentration will not have the effect of acquiring or strengthening a dominant position which significantly impedes effective competition in the Belgian market in question or in a substantial part thereof.
FOR THESE REASONS
The Competition Council
Finds that the concentration falls within the scope of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition and is admissible pursuant to Articles 3

### § 1 and 33, § 2, 1.a, of that law

Thus decided on 18 April 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mrs Marie-Claude Grégoire, Chamber President, Mr Patrick De Wolf, Mr Pierre Battard and Mr Roger Ramaekers, Members. *
FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY
[C − 2002/11448]
Competition Council. — Decision No. 2002-C/C-44 of 13 June 2002
Case CONC-C/C-02/0026: VMS-Keytrade.Com S.A. – RealBank S.A. Having regard to the concentration notification filed on 29 April 2002; Having regard to the rapporteur's report of 24 May 2002; Having heard at the hearing of 13 June 2002:
—	Mr Patrick Marchand, rapporteur; —	Mr Henri Delwaide, lawyer registered with the French-speaking and German-speaking bar of Brussels, common representative of the notifying parties. 1. The notifying parties
1.1. Acquiring undertaking:
VMS-Keytrade.Com S.A. (hereinafter VMS) is a public limited company under Belgian law having its registered office at boulevard du Souverain 100, 1170 Brussels, and registered in the Brussels trade register under number 627.795. Its object is to make available to its clients, natural or legal persons, private or institutional, stockbroking firms or banks, both in Belgium and abroad, tools enabling them to manage their own portfolio, in real time or deferred, by electronic means in general and via the Internet in particular. VMS, in practice, concentrates its activities on offering an intermediation service (brokerage) relating to the execution of stock exchange orders. Orders may be transmitted to the company by telephone, fax, mail or directly online via the Internet. On the market for stock transaction execution services (on Euronext Brussels), its market share is less than [business secret] %. However, its status as a stockbroking firm did not allow it to be a full bank. 1.2. Acquired undertaking:
RealBank S.A. (hereinafter RealBank) is a public limited company under Belgian law having its registered office at rue des Colonies 1, 1000 Brussels, and registered in the Brussels trade register under number 81.690. Its object is banking operations, financial operations, brokerage and commission. It may carry out all undertakings and operations relating directly or indirectly to its object or of a nature to facilitate its realisation. The company may hold partnership shares and participations within the limits provided by the legal statute of banks. Concretely, this company offers a complete range of banking services, with the exception of credit which remains a marginal activity [business secret]. In the ranking of all Belgian banks established according to the total amount of "customer deposits", Realbank occupied, in 2000, ["between 50th and 100th position" - business secret]. On the market for stock transaction execution services (on Euronext Brussels), its market share is less than 2%. 2. Description of the operation
The notification concerns a concentration operation by which VMS wishes to absorb RealBank and its subsidiaries by means of a merger by absorption. According to the notifying parties, the purpose of this concentration is to create a new banking group targeting the "retail" sector in Belgium and progressively offering a complete range of banking products. However, even after the merger, the new company will not have the necessary means to compete with the large network banks for "retail" customers. Consequently, its strategy will consist mainly of approaching the customer directly, by all distance marketing techniques (Internet, mailing, affinity groups, etc.). The objective is therefore to create a "direct" bank, similar to what certain well-established competitors have achieved (DHB Bank, Dexia-Cortal, Deutsche Bank-Bank24, Citibank, Banque Delen, Robeco Bank Belgium, etc.). This merger is subject to the prior approval of the Banking and Finance Commission. 3. Scope of application
The aforementioned companies are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the law and the notified operation is a concentration operation within the meaning of Article 9 of the law. As provided for in Article 46, § 3, a), of the law, for banks, credit institutions and other financial institutions, turnover is replaced by one-tenth of the balance sheet total, which corresponds respectively for the VMS and RealBank groups to € 9.2 million and € 23.9 million. Article 11 of the law provides that the concentration falls within the scope of the law where the undertakings concerned together achieve in Belgium a turnover of more than 40 million euros and at least two of the undertakings concerned each achieve in Belgium a turnover of at least 15 million euros. In the present case, VMS and Realbank do not together reach in Belgium the threshold of 40 million euros required by Article 11 of the law. Moreover, VMS does not reach the threshold of 15 million euros required in Belgium. The thresholds referred to in Article 11 of the law are therefore not reached. FOR THESE REASONS
The Competition Council
— Finds that the concentration does not fall within the scope of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition and therefore did not need to be notified. Thus decided on 13 June 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of
Mrs Marie-Claude Grégoire (chamber president), Mr Patrick De Wolf, Mrs Dominique Smeets and Mr Roger Ramaekers, Members. *

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY
[C − 2002/11450]
Competition Council. — Decision No. 2002-C/C-46 of 26 June 2002
Case No. CONC-C/C-02/0027. - AB Electrolux/Diamant Boart International s.a. Having regard to the concentration notification filed by the notifying parties with the secretariat of the Competition Council on 8 May 2002. Having regard to the report filed by Patrick Marchand on behalf of the rapporteurs on 7 June 2002. Having heard at the hearing of 26 June 2002:
—	Mr Patrick Marchand, rapporteur. —	Messrs Dirk Vandermeersch and Marc Waha, common representatives of the notifying parties
1. The notifying parties
1.1. The acquirer
AB Electrolux (publ) is a company under Swedish law established at 143 S:t. Göransgatan SE-105 45 in Stockholm, Sweden, whose shares are listed on several stock exchanges, including Stockholm and London. It is active on a worldwide scale in several sectors: kitchen and catering appliances (cooking systems, dishwashers, refrigerators, etc.), cleaning appliances (vacuum cleaners, industrial washing machines, etc.) and outdoor work for private and professional use (hedge trimmers, chainsaws, garden tractors, lawnmowers, etc.). Electrolux is also active via its subsidiary Dimas AB in the manufacture of diamond cutting machines intended for the construction industry. Dimas AB also manufactures diamond abrasive tools (diamond tools) that can equip the machines it produces but also those produced by other manufacturers. In Belgium, Electrolux's activity in the diamond cutting sector is limited to wall saws and diamond tools (diamond discs) intended for certain types of saws, and only for use in the construction sector. 1.2. The sellers
The sellers concerned in the present operation number 22. They are the shareholders of Diamant Boart International composed of natural persons and legal persons including a company listed on the London stock exchange (Candover Investments plc). 1.3. The target undertaking
Diamant Boart International is a public limited company under Belgian law. It is established at avenue du Pont de Luttre 74, 1190 Brussels, and is registered in the Brussels trade register under number 638.636. This undertaking's activity is divided into three main areas:
—	the manufacture and sale of diamond cutting machines intended for the construction industry
—	the manufacture and sale of diamond tools, intended mainly for cutting machines in the construction industry, in the stone industry and in the ceramics industry; and
—	the sale of industrial diamonds and jewellery diamonds. This activity is carried out by two subsidiaries, Custodiam sa (a Belgian undertaking) and Custodiam (Xiamen) co., Ltd. which is a Chinese undertaking (hereinafter, for these two subsidiaries, Custodiam). 2. The notified operation
The notification concerns a concentration operation by which AB Electrolux (hereinafter Electrolux) wishes to acquire exclusive control of Diamant Boart International s.a. (hereinafter Diamant Boart). This notification follows a share transfer agreement signed on 2 May 2002 under which the Sellers transfer to Electrolux all the shares they hold in Diamant Boart. The envisaged operation aims to enable Electrolux to penetrate new segments of the diamond cutting industry and thus to compete with the main producers active in this market. Moreover, the operation will enable Electrolux to acquire a certain presence in certain regions of the world where it is not yet active in this field. The operation has also been notified in Austria, Germany, Ireland, Italy, Finland, Czech Republic, Brazil and the United States. The aforementioned companies are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the law and the notified operation is a concentration operation within the meaning of Article 9 of the law. With regard to the concentration operation as notified, it appears that Electrolux achieved in Belgium a consolidated turnover of approximately [confidential] million euros (of which less than 0.1% in the sector concerned by the operation) and that the target undertaking, Diamant Boart, achieved in Belgium a consolidated turnover of approximately [confidential] million euros. On the basis of the indications provided by the parties, the turnover thresholds referred to in Article 11 are therefore reached. 3. Relevant market
3.1. Economic sector concerned
The economic sectors concerned by the concentration are:
—	the sector of diamond cutting machines and corresponding diamond tools (NACE code 29 405); —	the diamond sector (NACE codes 36.221 and 51.561). 3.2. Relevant product market
The parties initially identify three relevant sectors of activity:
—	the diamond cutting machines sector; —	the diamond tools sector; —	the diamonds sector. 3.2.1. The diamond cutting machines sector
These machines are devices used to cut, sharpen, drill or polish hard materials. They are composed of an independent abrasive mechanism (called cutting tools) in order to be easily replaced when necessary. Cutting machines can be divided into three segments according to the sector of activity in which these machines are used:
—	construction; —	the stone industry; —	ceramics cutting. The parties consider that this distinction is necessary both from the demand side and from the supply side (the technical characteristics of the machines, their operation, the customers,...). 3.2.1.1. The stone and ceramics industry
Since the parties do not manufacture cutting machines in these sectors, there is no need to examine these segments further. 3.2.1.2. The construction industry
The parties consider that this sector is composed of seven distinct markets, seven categories of cutting machines that can be equipped with abrasive tools:
—	floor saws; —	wall saws; —	table saws; —	portable saws; —	wire cutting machines; —	drills; —	and surfacing devices. This distinction is necessary, according to the parties, because these machines are designed for a particular use, the final consumer generally does not use a machine designed for a certain type of work in the context of an application normally requiring another type of machine. These devices are moreover manufactured by different production lines that use equipment specific to them, having regard to their shape, their size and the power of their motor. 3.2.2. The diamond tools sector
The parties consider that these tools, intended to be used with appropriate cutting machines, can compete with conventional abrasive tools. However, given the envisaged operation, the parties did not consider it necessary to examine in more detail the degree of substitution between diamond tools and conventional tools since by adopting a narrow market definition limited to diamond tools, the envisaged operation does not create or strengthen a dominant position. The following description therefore concerns only diamond tools. This sector, like that of cutting machines, can be divided into three segments according to the sector of activity in which these tools are used:
—	construction; —	the stone industry; —	ceramics cutting. In this sector, the parties are active in the construction segment as well as in the stone industry segment. Since Electrolux and Diamant Boart are not present in the ceramics cutting segment, the latter will not be addressed. With regard to the construction segment, the parties have identified four distinct markets:
—	diamond discs. These discs for circular saws can be used and adapted both on floor saws, wall saws, table saws and portable saws. These discs are interchangeable to a large extent; —	diamond wires. Diamond wires are mainly used in the construction sector to cut various types of concrete. The use of this type of wire constitutes a different cutting technique; —	drill bits. The use of bits is only intended for drilling operations. They are therefore not substitutable for other forms of diamond tools; —	surfacing plates. These tools are exclusively used to polish, clean, or level dirty or rough surfaces, or to remove surface layers (for example mastic, resin,...). Generally speaking, diamond tools are used to cut, drill or treat different types of materials, among which several types of concrete, brick, steel, glass and asphalt. In their notification, however, the parties also explained that, although presenting functional differences, from the supply side, these different tools have several common characteristics. As a result, there is a high degree of substitutability at the supply level with regard to the manufacture of these tools in the construction sector. With regard to the stone industry segment, the parties have also identified four distinct markets:
—	diamond discs; —	diamond wires; —	surfacing plates; —	and gang saw blades. These blades are specifically intended for multi-blade saws used for stone cutting and in particular to slice stone blocks into slabs of regular dimension. Diamond tools used in the stone industry enable natural stone to be treated from its extraction in quarries to its final polishing. The parties consider that these four categories of tools constitute as many distinct markets from the demand side, even if, as is the case for the construction segment, there is real substitutability from the supply side. 3.2.3. The diamonds sector
Diamonds are stones produced naturally from carbon. Scientific discoveries have made it possible to produce artificial diamonds. These synthetic diamonds are manufactured by an industrial process combining high temperature and very high pressure. The multiple uses of diamonds, both synthetic and natural, are grouped into two categories, namely industrial and jewellery diamonds. 3.2.3.1. Industrial diamonds
These diamonds (synthetic or natural) are used for industrial applications (for example for oil or mining drilling) and are sold in the form of mixtures or blends. The majority of industrial diamonds are synthetic diamonds. In many industrial applications, diamonds compete with other superabrasives, such as cubic boron nitrides. Of the parties to the concentration, only Diamant Boart via its subsidiaries Custodiam is active in this market. It sources from the main manufacturers and then mixes them with particular know-how developed historically to serve the needs of its parent company (at a rate of two-thirds). The balance is sold to third parties including companies that were formerly part of the Diamant Boart group. A different mixture enables better adaptation to the industrial application for which the diamonds are intended. Custodiam thus competes with its suppliers who also develop blends adapted to the specific needs of certain industrial applications. In Belgium, sales to third parties are divided between industrial customers and resellers who in turn supply users, i.e. manufacturers of abrasive tools. A large proportion of the diamonds sold to these intermediaries is then exported to customers outside Belgium, according to Diamant Boart. 3.2.3.2. Jewellery diamonds

A large number of outlets in the field of jewellery and costume jewellery exist for natural and synthetic diamonds. Having regard to the absence of both horizontal and vertical relationships with the other relevant markets, these activities are not described further. Diamant Boart's market share in this field, the only active party, is extremely low. 3.3. Geographic market

The relevant markets identified above have a European dimension, or even wider. Indeed, as the Parties emphasise, the prices of these products and the way they are sold are very similar throughout Europe. With particular regard to the market for industrial diamonds, the parties emphasised that the manufacture of these diamonds takes place outside the European area and that they are exported to many regions of the world, thanks largely to very low transport costs (approximately 2% of the price) and very limited customs duties. The worldwide size of this market has moreover been recognised by the Competition Council in several recent cases. 4. Competitive analysis

The parties to the concentration are active in Belgium in the following markets:

The parties have provided several tables in their notification. The market for industrial diamonds has been considered by the parties as a vertical affected market as a precautionary measure insofar as it is necessary to limit the geographic market to the Belgian market, to focus only on industrial diamonds and given the absence of public statistics on total sales in Belgium. This is the reason why the figures cited concern the World and Europe. Custodiam specifies however that it estimates that total sales of industrial diamonds in Belgium are clearly higher than 50 million carats. Moreover, the parties have considered that Custodiam's sales in Belgium could exceed

more than 25% of total sales of industrial diamonds in the hypothesis that account should be taken of its captive sales to its parent company and if, having regard to the added value of the blending process, it should be considered that it did not act as a simple reseller on behalf of the producers of industrial diamonds. In this market, the major players are General Electric, De Beers and Ilijn (Korean). These three companies represent approximately [confidential]% of Custodiam's purchases. The balance is made from numerous producers, mainly Chinese. To the parties' knowledge, there are more than 100 production sites, mainly in China. 4.2. Market reactions. The operators present in the different markets who responded do not contradict the parties' assertions. Tyrolit presents itself as being active in the sector of manufacturing diamond cutting machines
intended for the construction sector as well as in the sector of diamond tools for construction and natural stone. It has a direct clientele and also uses distribution through specialised shops (hardware stores). In addition to Diamant Boart, Norton, Hilti and Adamas already cited by the parties, it also identifies Inter-Fixings, Sadu Abrasives, Carbodiam, Carat, Spit and Sedima as competitors. Hilti specifies that it is active only in the construction sector, including cutting machines and diamond tools (wall saws, portable saws, wire cutting machines and drills, on the one hand and on the other hand, diamond discs and wires, drill bits). It mentions Diacom as an additional competitor. Norton and Saint Gobain did not respond. Magediam for its part is present only in diamond tools intended for the stone sector with diamond wires and gang saw blades. It further specifies that its presence is reduced to the quarries sector. Its turnover is significantly different from that presented by the parties. On the market for industrial diamonds, presented as a precautionary measure by the parties as a vertical affected market, Ilijn and Diarop (De Beers) were questioned. Only one more or less useful response comes from Diarop. The latter considers it plausible that Custodiam buys more than 25% of the total purchased in Belgium, disregarding the Antwerp market which must be important. If this Antwerp market should be taken into account however, Custodiam does not represent 25%. The expression Antwerp market must be understood as referring only to the industrial diamond market (there may be other things than powder but in this case, it is limited to 10 stones per carat). It continues by indicating that, in its opinion, Custodiam plays the role of purchasing centre for the companies of the Diamant Boart group to which a probably majority part is re-exported. Given this response and provided that the proportions reserved for the group are correct, it is permissible to conclude that Custodiam's market shares are less than 25%. 5. Conclusion. The notified operation will result in only a very limited addition of market shares in only two specific markets (wall saws and diamond discs for the construction sector) in which moreover the market shares of the parties to the operation do not exceed 25%. There is therefore no affected market within the meaning of the law. On the vertical market for industrial diamonds, presented as affected by the parties for legal certainty, and if the elements of response provided by De Beers concerning the extent of the market are applied to the aforementioned case-law of the Competition Council, the market share of the parties to the operation does not exceed 25% and this market is not affected either within the meaning of the law. For these reasons
The Competition Council
—	considers that the notified concentration will not have the effect of acquiring or strengthening a dominant position which significantly impedes effective competition on the Belgian markets concerned or on a substantial part thereof. —	finds that the concentration in question falls within the scope of the law and declares it admissible, in accordance with Articles 33, § 1 and 33, § 2, 1.a, of the law. Thus decided on 26 June 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Patrick De Wolf, chamber president, and Mrs Marie-Claude Grégoire, Mrs Dominique Smeets and Mr Pierre Battard, members. *
FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY
[C − 2002/11453]
Competition Council. — Decision No. 2002-C/C-49 of 28 June 2002
Case CONC-C/C-02/29: ELECTRABEL CUSTOMER SOLUTIONS S.A./IDEG S.C.R.L. In the matter of:
Electrabel Customer Solutions S.A. (hereinafter ECS), having its registered office at boulevard du Régent 8, 1000 Brussels, and
Intercommunale de Distribution d'Electricité et de Gaz having taken the form of a S.C.R.L. (hereinafter Ideg), having its registered office at 5000 Namur, avenue Albert Ier 19; Having regard to the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition coordinated on 1 July 1999 (hereinafter LPCE); Having regard to the notification dated 21 May 2002 of a concentration which consists of the takeover by ECS, a subsidiary of Electrabel, of the intercommunale Ideg's clientele as it becomes eligible;Having examined the case file of the Competition Service; Having examined the reasoned report of the Corps of Rapporteurs dated 12 June 2002; Having examined the decision on the confidentiality of the case documents of 17 June 2002; Having examined the request submitted on the basis of Article 32quater, § 2 LPCE, on 13 June 2002 by S.A. Luminus to be heard in this procedure; Having examined the decision of 18 June 2002 granting this request; Having examined the request submitted on the basis of Article 32quater, § 2 LPCE, on 17 June 2002 by N.V. Nuon Energy Trade & Wholesale to be heard in this procedure; Having examined the decision of 19 June 2002 granting this request; Having examined the request dated 20 June 2002 and transmitted to the Competition Council on 21 June 2002 by which the Commission for Electricity and Gas Regulation (abbreviated CREG) requests on the basis of Article 32quater, § 2 LPCE and Article 23 of the law of 29 April 1999 relating to the organisation of the electricity market, to be heard in this procedure; Having examined the decision of 24 June 2002 granting this request; Having heard at the hearing of 26 June 2002, S.A. Luminus and Nuon Energy Trade & Wholesale and the Commission for Electricity and Gas Regulation; Having heard at the hearing of 27 June 2002 Mr Rapporteur P. Marchand and the notifying parties represented by Mr de Garcia and Mr Baeten and assisted by Mr Vandencasteele, Mr Brinckman and Mr Vronincks, lawyers in Brussels; 1. the notifying parties
1.1. Acquirer
The company Electrabel Customer Solutions (hereinafter ECS) is a public limited company under Belgian law established by deed of 12 December 2001 published in the annexes to the Moniteur belge of 28 December 2001, having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, boulevard du Régent 8, and having as its object the supply of electricity and gas as well as the supply of related products and services. This company is in fact a 100% subsidiary of Electrabel S.A. The public limited company under Belgian law Electrabel having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, boulevard du Régent 8, is an enterprise active at all stages in the electricity sector, from production to supply to the final customer. 1.2. Seller
The Intercommunale de Distribution d'Energie et de Gaz (hereinafter Ideg) is an intercommunal association having taken the form of a cooperative company with limited liability, established to ensure the distribution of electricity and gas to customers located in the territory of the affiliated municipalities of the province of Namur and two municipalities in Hainaut. Its registered office is established in Namur, avenue Albert Ier 19. Ideg is a mixed intercommunal association. It is established on the basis of a partnership with a private company, namely Electrabel, currently the majority shareholder (to the extent of 62.25%). The municipalities associated with it hold 37.75% of its capital. Regulatory provisions and in particular the provisions of the decree of 5 December 1996 relating to Walloon intercommunal associations, however provide that the municipalities always have the majority of votes as well as the presidency in the management and supervisory bodies, regardless of the proportion of the contributions of the various parties to the formation of the capital. 1.3. Target enterprise or part of enterprise
The operation targets Ideg's customer base becoming eligible on the electricity supply market. 2. Description of the operation
2.1. Preamble
The notified concentration concerns the electricity market which is currently in full liberalisation phase. This liberalisation of the electricity market was decided at European level by Directive 96/92 of 19 December 1996 which imposes a limited and progressive opening of the electricity market. This liberalisation is accompanied by regulation which pays particular attention to network access. Energy in Belgium is a matter whose competences are divided between the federal authority and the Regions. In a very schematic manner, this distribution of powers is established as follows:
— the federal State is competent for matters relating to production, transmission and tariffs; — distribution and supply are regional matters. The normative framework in this matter comprises:
— the law of 29 April 1999 relating to the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter electricity law) published in the Moniteur belge of 11 May 1999; — the decree of the Flemish Region of 17 July 2000 relating to the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter
Flemish decree); — the decree of the Walloon Region of 12 April 2001 relating to the organisation of the regional electricity market (hereinafter Walloon decree) and the order of the Walloon Government of 21 March 2002 relating to customers who have become eligible and the verification of their eligibility; — the ordinance of 19 July 2001 relating to the organisation of the electricity market in the Brussels-Capital Region (hereinafter the Ordinance). The different levels of competence have adopted their own market opening schedules. These differ both in terms of eligibility thresholds and dates. At the end of this liberalisation of the electricity market in Belgium, the distribution of electricity should be split into two activities to be entrusted to separate companies:
— network management, which concerns the operation, maintenance and development of the distribution network. The network operator must concentrate on its main activity. It may not carry out production activities - other than green electricity - nor sales activities - other than sales to captive customers and sales to so-called protected customers; — the sale of electricity which concerns the supply of energy to eligible customers. However, from 1996, municipalities in partnership with Electrabel had decided to pursue, within mixed intercommunal associations, network distribution management activities and sales to customers under municipal monopoly. Mixed intercommunal associations thus currently ensure the management of the distribution network as well as the supply of electricity. However, Article 8, § 1 of the Walloon decree relating to the organisation of the regional electricity market applicable to Ideg expressly provides that the distribution network operator may not carry out production activities other than green electricity or electricity sales other than sales necessitated by its activity as network operator. The distribution network operator may not engage in supply activities to eligible customers nor in the supply of other services on the electricity market which are not directly linked to the performance of the tasks referred to in Article 11 (that is to say network management). However, at the request of the municipalities, the distribution network operator may supply electricity to captive customers.... It follows that Walloon mixed intercommunal associations, such as Ideg, which wish to be designated as network operator, will no longer be able to simultaneously supply electricity to (become) eligible customers. Taking into account the implications of the ongoing liberalisation process, Electrabel and Intermixt Wallonie (public utility establishment grouping all public representatives of mixed intercommunal associations) have thus negotiated the terms of a new agreement capable of maintaining their partnership in a balanced framework. The principle adopted is that the municipalities and intercommunal associations will become more involved in network management, monopolistic and regulated type activities, while Electrabel will concentrate primarily on the activity of supply to customers. This specialisation of each party is not exclusive: the municipalities via the intercommunal associations will continue to have an interest in the result of the electricity marketing company and Electrabel will retain a participation - minority but significant - in the capital of the distribution network operators whose operation it will continue to ensure. In order to valorise the customer base that these intercommunal associations currently supply with electricity and which they will no longer be able to supply upon their eligibility, the municipalities have agreed with their partner within the mixed intercommunal associations, Electrabel, that a company ECS, established in December 2001 and whose shares are entirely held by Electrabel, will take over this customer base. In consideration for this takeover of control by ECS and this transfer of customer base by Ideg, the municipalities are offered the possibility of increasing their shareholding in the intercommunal association. These municipalities will moreover become minority shareholders (maximum 5% of the capital) in this company ECS and will participate in the profits to the extent of 40% of the result achieved. These negotiations resulted in the preparation of various agreements and a Memorandum of Understanding signed on 30 March 2001. 2.2. Operation covered by the notification
The operation which is the subject of this notification consists of the takeover by ECS (established on 12 December 2001 and a 100% subsidiary of Electrabel), of Ideg's customer base relating to the supply of electricity to its customers as and when they become eligible and to the extent that they do not decide to conclude a contract with another supplier. An eligible customer means the customer who has the right to conclude a supply contract with the enterprise of his choice and for this purpose has a right of access to the network. The takeover by ECS of this eligible customer base of Ideg covered by this notification was not the subject of a specific document but must be deduced from a set of documents among which the document entitled "Memorandum of Understanding between Intermix – Electrabel signed on 30 March 2001" and the amendments made to the articles of association of Ideg adopted on 19 April 2002. The board of directors of Ideg, on 6 June 2001, decided unanimously to have their intercommunal association adhere to the Memorandum of Understanding. In consideration for this transfer of customer base by Ideg, the municipalities are offered the possibility of increasing their shareholding in the intercommunal association on advantageous terms as well as participating in the profits of ECS to a greater extent than the amount of shares held in it. The participation of the municipalities in the company ECS will in fact be limited to 5% of its capital, but will give entitlement to a participation in the profits to the extent of 40% of the result. 3. Time limits
The notification was made on 22 May 2002. The time limit referred to in Article 33 of the law expires in application of Article 33,

### §§ 1 and 2 of the LPCE, 5 July 2002

4. Scope of application
The companies ECS and Ideg are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the LPCE. After the concentration, ECS will hold exclusive control of Ideg's activity of supplying electricity to eligible customers (or those who will become eligible). The operation therefore constitutes a concentration within the meaning of Article 9, § 1, of the law. On the basis of the figures provided by the parties in the notification, the turnover thresholds referred to in Article 11 of the law are met. 5. Relevant market
5.1. Relevant economic sector
The economic sector concerned by the concentration is that of the production and distribution of electricity (NACE code: 40.1). 5.2. Relevant product market
In the electricity sector, five different types of activities can be distinguished, namely generation, transmission, distribution, supply and trading. —	Generation consists of the production of electricity. —	Transmission is the conveyance of electricity over high-voltage cables. —	Distribution is the conveyance of electricity over medium and low-voltage cables. —	Supply consists of delivery to the final consumer. —	Trading is the purchase and resale of electricity. The first four activities can be considered as belonging to distinct product markets, since they require different assets and resources and because the market and competitive conditions are different for each of them. As regards electricity trading, the question of whether it constitutes a market distinct from the general trading of energy products has been left open by the European Commission. Since this market does not constitute a relevant market for the present case, there is no need to rule on this question. Supply of electricity
Following the liberalisation of part of the electricity market initiated by Directive EC 96/92, a further distinction must be made between the supply of electricity to eligible and non-eligible customers (or captive customers, i.e. those who cannot choose their supplier). These belong to two distinct product markets, insofar as the competitive conditions are different, and where, most often, they are subject to specific regulations. It is in this context that the present operation takes place since, pursuant to Article 8, § 1, of the Walloon decree, the distribution network operator is prohibited from any activity of supplying eligible customers. This network operator will probably be the intercommunal company or a new subsidiary thereof created for this purpose. Electrabel is active directly or indirectly in the markets for production, transmission, distribution, supply of electricity and trading of products related to electricity. Ideg is active in the markets for distribution and supply of electricity. The latter activity, insofar as it concerns customers becoming eligible who have not chosen a supplier, will be carried out by ECS, a 100% subsidiary of Electrabel. Consequently, the relevant product market is that of the supply of electricity to eligible customers. 5.3. Relevant geographic market
The geographic market is at most national. Indeed, given the problems related to the limited capacities of interconnections, imports are restricted to a fairly significant extent. 6. Competitive analysis
6.1. Structure of supply: Dominant position of Electrabel
Electrabel occupies a privileged position in the Belgian market since it holds extremely high market shares regardless of the market considered. 6.1.1. Supply
The market for the supply of electricity to eligible customers in Belgium is estimated by the parties in volume (GWh) at 30,962.4 for the year 2001. Electrabel's market share amounts to more than 90%. No other competitor, whatever its economic and financial power (EDF, RWE, E.ON), individually holds a market share greater than or equal to 5%. 6.1.2. Production
Electrabel's dominant position in the market for supply to eligible customers must also be examined taking into account Electrabel's quasi-monopolistic situation in the production market in Belgium. Since 1995, S.A. Electrabel and S.A. SPE have been grouped within a cooperative company (CPTE) whose purpose is to ensure the coordination of the production and transmission of electrical energy of the two companies. Electrabel is a shareholder therein to the extent of 91.5%. Out of a total production in 2001 estimated at 75,953 GWh by CREG and at [business secrets] GWh by the notifying parties, [business secrets] GWh were produced by Electrabel, representing a market share of [more than 90% - the exact percentage is a business secret]. No significant development is to be expected, either in the short or medium term, in this market. It would even tend to strengthen Electrabel's position. This situation poses all the more problems as electricity imports are at a fairly low level. 6.2. Transmission
The transmission activity concerns the conveyance of electricity over the high-voltage network. The cooperative company CPTE, a subsidiary of Electrabel and SPE, owned the major part of the high-voltage network in Belgium and ensured its management. On 28 June 2001, the high-voltage network was contributed to a subsidiary company of CPTE, the company ELIA. The latter will be the transmission network operator whose designation, missions and control are regulated by the electricity law and the implementing orders. 6.3. Distribution
Electrabel also has a privileged position at the distribution level (conveyance of electricity from the transmission network to the consumer over a medium and low-voltage network). Indeed, distribution activities fall under the monopoly of the municipalities which for the most part have come together within intercommunal companies. These intercommunal companies are for the majority of them qualified as mixed because they have a private partner, in this case in all cases Electrabel which is generally a majority shareholder. Thus, the 16 mixed intercommunal companies in which Electrabel is a shareholder group together more than 500 municipalities and serve daily more than 8 million inhabitants in Belgium, representing more than 82% of the electricity distributed. 6.4. Structure of demand
Currently, the eligibility thresholds in relation to the annual consumption level are as follows:
-	at federal level:	more than 20 GWh
